# BUSINESS RULES: `lazylabel`

| | |
|---|---|
| System | LazyLabel 2.0.8, legacy snapshot `legacy/lazylabel` at 2a7d5d8 |
| Produced | 2026-09-19 by `/code-modernization:modernize-extract-rules lazylabel` (Method A, workflow `extract-rules.js`) |
| Method | Three lens-scoped extractors (calculations, validations, lifecycle); every rule's citation re-read by an independent referee; every P0 candidate sent to two independent judges (compliance and fidelity lenses); near-duplicate cards from different lenses reviewed and merged |
| Result | 94 distinct rules from 173 confirmed cards (79 near-duplicates folded into another card); 2 candidate rules rejected by referees; 0 instruction-shaped source locations flagged |
| Companions | `analysis/lazylabel/DATA_OBJECTS.md` (data objects) and `analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md` (every P0 judge verdict with its reasoning) |

**Coverage note.** Extraction ran **one round** of the three lenses. The workflow normally repeats rounds until two in a row find nothing new; it was capped at one round after the first run's later rounds died on usage limits, and a failed round is indistinguishable from a dry one. Rules that only a later round would surface are therefore not in this catalog. Every one of the 175 candidate rules was refereed against its citation. The P0 panel needed 4 workflow runs, because judge agents kept failing on usage limits; in this record 74 of 74 P0 candidate cards carry both verdicts. In 37 cards the two judges disagreed in one direction: the compliance judge said the rule is not P0 because nothing here is financial or regulated, while the fidelity judge said it is P0 because it guards annotation data. The workflow demotes a card on any disagreement. Decision 14, taken on 2026-09-17, restored 21 of those rules to P0; the remaining 0 land only in Phase 3 or Phase 6 and stay P1 until those phases start. Every verdict and its reasoning is in `P0_PANEL.md`. The 173 confirmed cards were then reviewed for near-duplicates across lenses and merged into 94 distinct rules, with each folded card's own specification kept in the table at the end of this file. The data-object catalog returned 45 objects (`DATA_OBJECTS.md`).

How to read a card: **Source** paths are relative to the repository root. **Priority** P0 means the rule guards data integrity (for LazyLabel: what is written to or read from annotation files, and which pixels belong to which class), so it becomes part of the behavior contract every rewrite phase must prove equivalent. **Confidence** below High carries the exact question a subject-matter expert must answer.

## Calculation rules (36)

### RULE-001: COCO JSON export structure and area
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/coco.py:19-100`  
**Plain English:** One COCO file per image with image id 1, a category per class (alias 'name.supercategory' split at the last dot), and one annotation per object with polygon, [x, y, w, h] box, area and iscrowd 0.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An image file img.png that is 300 wide x 200 high. Exactly one class has segments on this image: class id 2, whose alias is 'dog.animal'; therefore class_order = [2] (sorted unique class ids of the image's segments, legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:87-95). One segment of class 2 whose FINAL mask - after pixel priority and after any active crop - covers exactly the 100 mask pixels x = 100..109 and y = 50..59 inclusive. State this as pixel extents, not as polygon corners: a polygon drawn with corners (100,50)-(110,60) rasterizes to 11x11 = 121 pixels, because cv2.fillPoly includes both edge rows/columns (segment_manager.py:182-184), and would instead yield bbox [100,50,11,11], area 100 and segmentation [100,50,100,60,110,60,110,50]. COCO JSON is among the selected export formats, so per-object contours are built (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/__init__.py:86-94; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:445-452).  
  When  The user saves the image's annotations and the COCO exporter runs (CocoExporter.export, legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/coco.py:40-97). It walks every object contour yielded by iter_object_contours (exporters/__init__.py:114-136) and writes one file, \<image basename without extension\> + '_coco.json' (coco.py:99-100), next to the image.  
  Then  img_coco.json is written containing exactly this object, with top-level keys in this order - 'images', 'annotations', 'categories' (coco.py:87-91): images = [ { "id": 1, "file_name": "img.png", "width": 300, "height": 200 } ] (coco.py:53-54; file_name is the basename only, width/height are the FULL image size); annotations = [ { "id": 1, "image_id": 1, "category_id": 2, "bbox": [100, 50, 10, 10], "area": 81, "segmentation": [[100, 50, 100, 59, 109, 59, 109, 50]], "iscrowd": 0 } ] (coco.py:67-81; field order as written); categories = [ { "id": 2, "name": "dog", "supercategory": "animal" } ] (coco.py:44-50). Verified by re-running cv2.boundingRect, cv2.contourArea and cv2.findContours(RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) on this exact mask with OpenCV 4.12.0 / numpy 2.2.6 from the app venv: contour has 4 points, boundingRect = (100,50,10,10), contourArea = 81.0 -\> int -\> 81. Note area 81, NOT the 100 mask pixels: the contour runs through pixel CENTRES, so area is about (w-1)*(h-1) for a rectangle.  
  And   The serialization is part of the contract because exports must stay byte-identical. The body is json.dump(coco_data, f, indent=2) (coco.py:96): 2-space indent, every array element on its own line, key separator ': ', item separator ',' with no trailing space, and NO trailing newline at end of file. ensure_ascii defaults to True, so any non-ASCII character in a class name or file name is escaped as \\uXXXX (verified: 'cafe' with an e-acute is written as caf\\u00e9, never as raw UTF-8) - JavaScript's JSON.stringify does NOT do this and must be post-processed. The file is opened in text mode, open(path, 'w') (coco.py:95), so on Windows every '\\n' is translated to '\\r\\n' (verified: the written file contained 17 CR and 17 LF); on Linux/macOS it stays '\\n'. The parent directory is created if missing (coco.py:94). If and only if there are zero annotations, no file is written and export() returns None (coco.py:84-85) - and nothing is deleted, so a stale _coco.json from an earlier save survives on disk and can still be read back (export_all never deletes, exporters/__init__.py:189-206). Deletion of annotation files happens only when the segment list is empty (save_export_manager.py:106-109; ui/main_window.py:6589-6594).  
**Parameters:** category_id = ctx.class_order[channel], i.e. the raw LazyLabel class id with no +1 background shift, so class 0 is written as category id 0 (coco.py:71; channel is the index into class_order, segment_manager.py:276, 283). category id = the same class id (coco.py:49). Category name/supercategory = the class alias split at the LAST '.' via rsplit('.', 1) (coco.py:19-27); with no dot, supercategory equals name (coco.py:27); with no alias at all the value is str(class_id), used for BOTH name and supercategory (coco.py:46). Categories are emitted for every id in class_order, in ascending class-id order, even for a class that ends up with zero annotations; classes that have no segment on this image get no category (coco.py:44-50). image id = 1, hardcoded (coco.py:54, 70). Annotation ids are 1, 2, 3 ... assigned in iteration order and restarting at 1 on every export call (coco.py:58, 82). bbox = [x, y, w, h] from cv2.boundingRect, all ints, edge-inclusive so w = maxX - minX + 1 (coco.py:65, 72). area = TRUNCATE-toward-zero of cv2.contourArea(contour) when the contour has 3 or more POINTS, else int(w) * int(h) of the bounding box (coco.py:73-77). The truncation is load-bearing: Python int() drops the fraction, it does not round - verified 24.5 -\> 24 (8x8 lower triangle) and 0.5 -\> 0 (1-pixel-wide L). In TypeScript use Math.trunc (equivalently Math.floor, since contourArea is never negative); Math.round would diverge on the many real masks whose area ends in .5. iscrowd = 0 always (coco.py:79). segmentation = [polygon], a list containing exactly ONE flat [x1,y1,x2,y2,...] integer array (coco.py:62, 78). Polygon vertices come from contour_to_polygon (exporters/__init__.py:139-157) and are NOT simplified - unlike YOLO Segmentation, which applies cv2.approxPolyDP with epsilon = 0.001 * arcLength (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/yolo_segmentation.py:33-36). Contours are cv2.findContours(mask, RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) - outer boundaries only, corner points only, in OpenCV's own order (segment_manager.py:303-305; exporters/__init__.py:132-134). Output suffix = os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + '_coco.json' (coco.py:99-100).  
**Edge cases handled:** Area under-counts pixels: the polygon runs through pixel centres, so the 100-pixel 10x10 block yields area 81, roughly (w-1)*(h-1). COCO's small/medium/large buckets (32^2, 96^2) in pycocotools will bucket objects differently than a pixel-count area would.; Area is truncated, not rounded (coco.py:74). An 8x8 lower-triangle mask gives contourArea 24.5 -\> 24; a 1-pixel-wide L shape gives 0.5 -\> 0. A contour with \>= 3 points uses the contour area even when that area is 0 - it does NOT fall back to the bounding box.; The \>= 3 test counts contour POINTS, not pixels (coco.py:75). A 1-pixel object gives a 1-point contour -\> area = 1*1 = 1. A 10-pixel horizontal line gives a 2-point contour -\> area = 10*1 = 10. A 10-pixel DIAGONAL line also gives a 2-point contour -\> area = 10*10 = 100 for 10 pixels of ink (a 5-pixel diagonal gives 25).; Contours of 1 or 2 points are padded to a closed 4-point ring rather than dropped (exporters/__init__.py:148-157), so segmentation always holds at least 8 numbers: a single pixel at (100,50) becomes [100,50,100,50,100,50,100,50]; a 10-pixel line becomes [100,50,109,50,109,50,100,50].; Only OUTER contours are kept (RETR_EXTERNAL), so holes are silently filled: a 20x20 ring with a 10x10 hole holds 300 pixels but exports one annotation with bbox [100,50,20,20] and area 361.; One segment can produce SEVERAL annotations. A segment split into two blobs yields one annotation per blob, all sharing the same category_id (exporters/__init__.py:122-126; segment_manager.py:303-313).; Annotation order is segment order, then OpenCV's contour order within a segment - it is not spatial and not sorted. Verified: for one mask with blobs at (5,5) and (30,30), OpenCV 4.12 returned (30,30) FIRST, so it gets annotation id 1. Do not assume top-left-first ordering.; An alias with an unintended dot splits wrongly: 'St. Bernard' -\> name 'St', supercategory ' Bernard' (leading space preserved); 'v1.0' -\> name 'v1', supercategory '0'; 'a.b.c' -\> name 'a.b', supercategory 'c' (last dot wins); 'dog.' -\> supercategory ''; '.dog' -\> name ''.; A class with no alias writes str(class_id) into BOTH name and supercategory, e.g. class 5 -\> {"id": 5, "name": "5", "supercategory": "5"} (coco.py:46).; Alias round-trip is lossy. The loader rebuilds the alias as name + '.' + supercategory only when supercategory != name (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:635-637), so an alias of 'x.x' is written as name 'x' / supercategory 'x' and reloads as plain 'x'.; A category is still written for a class whose every pixel was removed by an active crop or by pixel priority; that class then has a category and no annotation (coco.py:44-50 runs independently of the annotation loop).; Coordinates, width and height are always in FULL-image space, even with an active crop: cropping blanks the pixels outside the crop rather than cutting the image down (file_manager.py:712-739). The multi-view save path never applies a crop at all (main_window.py:6615-6631 passes no crop_coords).; Stale-file hazard: if segments exist but all of them are cropped away, zero annotations are produced, so the exporter writes nothing (coco.py:84-85) yet deletes nothing (exporters/__init__.py:189-206) - an out-of-date _coco.json stays on disk and is still eligible to be loaded back, since COCO_JSON is third in LOAD_PRIORITY (exporters/__init__.py:73-81).; Per-image ids collide when files are merged into one dataset: image_id is always 1 and annotation ids always restart at 1. LazyLabel's own loader ignores id, image_id, area, iscrowd and the whole images block, reading only categories and each annotation's category_id / segmentation / bbox (file_manager.py:626-690), so these fields are never validated on the way back in.; Polygon is NOT simplified, unlike YOLO Segmentation (coco.py:62 vs yolo_segmentation.py:33-36); the vertices are exactly OpenCV's CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE corner points, not every boundary pixel.; Structural fallback: iter_object_contours only uses per-object contours when ctx.instances is non-empty (exporters/__init__.py:122). If an empty instances list ever reaches the exporter, it contours the MERGED per-class mask channels instead (exporters/__init__.py:128-136), which fuses touching or overlapping same-class objects into one annotation. A port must reproduce both branches.  
**Suspected defect:** 'area' is the polygon area through pixel centers, which undercounts the object's pixel count (10x10 square -\> 81, roughly (w-1)x(h-1)); COCO small/medium/large buckets (32^2, 96^2) may misclassify objects.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** The open question - should COCO 'area' be the mask pixel count instead of the contour polygon area - is answered NO by the approved decision that exports stay byte-identical to legacy output: the Node/TypeScript port must keep area = trunc(contourArea(contour)) for contours of 3+ points and w*h otherwise (coco.py:73-77), which yields 81 for the 100-pixel 10x10 block. The suspectedDefect is real and stays as a documented deviation from the pycocotools convention (it shifts small/medium/large metric buckets), not as something to fix; if the product owner later wants true pixel-count area it is a versioned format change, not a port bug. Verifying the card clause by clause, every number in it is correct - I re-ran the OpenCV calls in-memory with OpenCV 4.12.0 from the app venv and got contour [100,50,100,59,109,59,109,50], boundingRect (100,50,10,10) and contourArea 81.0 - but the card was wrong or incomplete in seven ways. (1) The Given was ambiguous: it must mean mask pixels x=100..109, y=50..59, because a polygon with corners (100,50)-(110,60) fills 11x11 via cv2.fillPoly (segment_manager.py:182-184) and would give bbox [100,50,11,11] and area 100. (2) 'area = int(...)' is too weak for a TS implementer: Python int() TRUNCATES (coco.py:74) - 24.5 -\> 24, 0.5 -\> 0 - so Math.round would silently diverge on real masks even though it passes the card's example. (3) The \>= 3 test counts contour POINTS, not pixels (coco.py:75), and the card never gave the sub-3-point fallback numbers (single pixel -\> 1, 10-pixel line -\> 10, 10-pixel diagonal -\> 100) nor the 1-/2-point padding to a 4-point ring (exporters/__init__.py:148-157). (4) 'One annotation per object' actually means one per OUTER contour (RETR_EXTERNAL, segment_manager.py:303-305): holes are filled (a 20x20 ring with a 10x10 hole = 300 pixels but area 361) and a multi-blob segment produces several annotations sharing one category_id (exporters/__init__.py:122-126). (5) The card omitted the whole categories rule - every id in class_order in ascending order (segment_manager.py:87-95), emitted even with zero annotations, raw class id with no +1 background shift so class 0 is category 0, and str(class_id) for both name and supercategory when there is no alias (coco.py:44-50). (6) 'No file when there are no objects' was misleading: nothing is written (coco.py:84-85) AND nothing is deleted (exporters/__init__.py:189-206), so a stale _coco.json survives and stays loadable at LOAD_PRIORITY rank 3 (exporters/__init__.py:73-81); files are deleted only when the segment list is empty (save_export_manager.py:106-109), which matches the approved decision that annotation files are deleted only on explicit user action. (7) The card said nothing about serialization, which the byte-identical decision makes contractual: json.dump(indent=2) with fixed key order images/annotations/categories, ensure_ascii=True so non-ASCII is escaped as \\uXXXX (JSON.stringify does not), and text-mode write (coco.py:95) that emits CRLF on Windows and LF elsewhere, with no trailing newline. The three things an implementer must watch hardest are the truncation, the ensure_ascii escaping, and the annotation ORDER - OpenCV's contour order is not spatial, and in 4.12 a blob at (30,30) came back before one at (5,5), so ids 1..N cannot be assigned by sorting. **Still open for the owner:** Two items need a product-owner call, neither blocking the port. (1) The byte-identical target is platform-dependent: coco.py:95 writes in text mode, so the legacy bytes carry CRLF on Windows and LF on Linux/macOS. The equivalence harness must name which platform's output is the reference, or normalize line endings before comparing - otherwise a correct Node port fails on one OS and passes on the other. (2) The stale-file hazard (segments exist but all are cropped away: nothing written, nothing deleted, an out-of-date _coco.json stays loadable at LOAD_PRIORITY rank 3) is legacy behaviour that the approved 'delete only on explicit user action' decision preserves as-is. Whether that is acceptable, or should be surfaced as a warning in the rewrite, is a product decision and deserves its own rule card - it is a data-integrity gap, not a formatting one.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should COCO 'area' be the mask pixel count (COCO convention) instead of the contour polygon area? Panel: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-002: CreateML export/import: pixel center boxes
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/createml.py:33-67; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:495-540`  
**Plain English:** CreateML annotations store the box center in pixels (x + width/2, y + height/2) and integer width/height with the class alias as label; import rebuilds x1=round(cx-w/2), x2=x1+round(w).  
**Specification:**  
  Given Class 2 alias 'cat', object pixels columns 101-300 (x=101, w=200) and rows 50-149 (y=50, h=100)  
  When  Saved as CreateML and reloaded  
  Then  JSON [{image:'\<file\>', annotations:[{label:'cat', coordinates:{x:201.0, y:100.0, width:200, height:100}}]}]; reload fills columns 101-300, rows 50-149 exactly  
  And   Only the first image entry of the JSON array is read; boxes are clamped to the image and dropped if empty  
**Parameters:** Center = integer origin + integer size / 2 (float); suffix '_createml.json'  
**Edge cases handled:** Odd widths give .5 centers (x=100, w=201 -\> 200.5) and still round-trip; Label names map to class ids by first appearance (ids not preserved); No file when there are no objects; Same alias-only class identity as Pascal VOC  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-003: NPZ Class Map export resolves overlaps to lowest class and stores foreground
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/npz_class_map.py:20-76; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:282-333`  
**Plain English:** A single H x W map stores the class ID at each labelled pixel (overlap goes to the lowest channel, i.e. lowest class ID), with a separate foreground mask so class 0 differs from background.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An image img.png of height 2 and width 3 (H=2, W=3). Labelled pixels: class id 7 covers (row 0, col 0) and (row 0, col 1); class id 2 covers (row 0, col 1) only; every other pixel is unlabelled. NPZ Class Map is among the selected export formats. Pixel Priority is off (settings pixel_priority_enabled = false). class_order is NOT free-form: every live caller passes the per-image list of unique class ids sorted ascending by SegmentManager.get_unique_class_ids (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:87-95), from save_export_manager.py:400 and :494 and main_window.py:6602 — so class_order = [2, 7], meaning channel 0 = class 2 and channel 1 = class 7. The mask tensor is (H, W, C) uint8 holding only 0 or 1: it is allocated as np.zeros(dtype=np.uint8) and filled with np.logical_or per channel (segment_manager.py:223, 242-244), so here channel 0 = [[0,1,0],[0,0,0]] and channel 1 = [[1,1,0],[0,0,0]]. class_aliases is the whole session alias dict, not just this image's classes (save_export_manager.py:424; main_window.py:6622): {2: 'cat', 7: 'dog'}.  
  When  export_all runs the NPZ Class Map exporter over that context (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/npz_class_map.py:20-44). It returns None and writes nothing when mask_tensor.size == 0 or when no element is \> 0 (lines 21-26). Otherwise it builds class_map by taking np.argmax over the channel axis and indexing a uint16 lookup table built from class_order (lines 66-73), builds foreground = np.any(mask_tensor \> 0, axis=2) (line 33), creates the parent directory (line 36) and writes one np.savez_compressed archive (lines 37-43).  
  Then  One file is written at the image path with its extension stripped plus '_CM.npz' — img_CM.npz (npz_class_map.py:46-47) — containing exactly four arrays (lines 37-43). class_map = [[7, 2, 0], [0, 0, 0]], dtype uint16: pixel A (0,0) = 7 because only channel 1 is active and class_order[1] = 7; pixel B (0,1) = 2 because both channels hold 1 and np.argmax returns the FIRST maximum, channel 0, and class_order[0] = 2; pixel C (0,2) and all of row 1 = 0 because no channel is active there, so they keep the np.zeros fill (lines 67-72). foreground = [[true, true, false], [false, false, false]], dtype bool (line 33). class_order = [2, 7], dtype int64 (line 41: np.array over the Python int list; int64 on this platform under NumPy 2.x). class_aliases = {2: 'cat', 7: 'dog'} (line 42). I reproduced lines 66-73 verbatim in memory under NumPy 2.2.6 and got exactly class_map [7, 2, 0] and foreground [true, true, false].  
  And   Reloading img.png when no img.npz, img_seg.txt and img_coco.json sit beside it (FileManager._LOAD_CHAIN, file_manager.py:128-136) reads img_CM.npz through load_npz_class_map (file_manager.py:282-333) with image_size = (2, 3), in this order: the archive is opened (line 297), the class_map key is required (lines 298-300), foreground is data['foreground'].astype(bool) or, if the key is absent, class_map != 0 (lines 303-307), class_aliases is restored — REPLACING the whole session dict with {int(k): v} (line 308, _restore_aliases at lines 335-343) — and only then is the shape gate applied (lines 313-318). Here tuple(class_map.shape[:2]) == (2, 3) so the gate passes, and one segment is added per value of np.unique(class_map[foreground]) in ascending class-id order (lines 320-331): first class_id 2 with mask [[false, true, false], [false, false, false]] (1 pixel), then class_id 7 with mask [[true, false, false], [false, false, false]] (1 pixel), each {type: 'Loaded', vertices: null, class_id: int(value)}. The round trip is lossy by design: class 7 loses the overlap pixel B, a class whose pixels are all covered by lower ids disappears entirely, and per-object separation is gone — exactly one segment per distinct class id.  
**Parameters:** Output path: os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + '_CM.npz'; parent directory created if missing (npz_class_map.py:36, 46-47). Four keys are always written, never a subset (lines 37-43): class_map (H, W) uint16 with 0 as background; foreground (H, W) bool; class_order (C,) int64; class_aliases dict[int, str] — legacy writes it as a pickled object array, the rewrite writes it as a JSON string (see answer). Valid class id range is 0..65535 inclusive: np.array(class_order, dtype=np.uint16) at line 71 raises OverflowError ('Python integer 65536 out of bounds for uint16') for any id \> 65535 or \< 0 under numpy\>=2.1.2 (legacy/lazylabel/pyproject.toml:23) — verified on NumPy 2.2.6. It does NOT wrap around, and export_all does not catch it (exporters/__init__.py:189-206), so the class map and every format iterated after it in the non-deterministic set order are skipped. Overlap tie-break: np.argmax over axis 2 = the FIRST channel holding the maximum value; because the tensor is strictly 0/1 this is the first ACTIVE channel, and it equals 'lowest class id' only because class_order is sorted ascending — with an unsorted order [7, 2] the same pixel B becomes 7 (verified in memory). Pixel Priority is applied to the tensor before the exporter sees it (segment_manager.py:247-250, 317-373), never inside the exporter: ascending (the default) leaves B = 2 via argmin; descending makes B = 7 via argmax (lines 356-361). Loader constants: the shape gate compares only class_map.shape[:2] against (height, width) (file_manager.py:313-318) and is skipped when image_size is None, which production never does because load_existing_mask resolves the size first (file_manager.py:178-185); the foreground key name is exactly 'foreground' and its absence falls back to class_map != 0 (lines 303-307), it is never combined with that test.  
**Edge cases handled:** Empty or all-zero mask tensor: the exporter returns None and writes NO NEW file (npz_class_map.py:21-26). It does not delete. export_all never deletes (exporters/__init__.py:189-206), so a stale img_CM.npz written by an earlier save survives on disk and will be reloaded later. Files are removed only when the image has zero segments at all (save_export_manager.py:106-109 and main_window.py:6589-6594, via delete_all_outputs at exporters/__init__.py:209-215). The card's 'No file when no pixel is labelled' must be read as 'no new file written', not 'no file exists'.; Crop interaction: an active crop zeroes every pixel outside the crop box while keeping the (H, W, C) shape (file_manager._apply_crop_to_mask, file_manager.py:712-739) and is applied BEFORE the exporter's emptiness check (save_export_manager.py:412-417). A crop that excludes every labelled pixel therefore produces the stale-file case above with no warning.; Class id above 65535 or below 0: np.array(class_order, dtype=np.uint16) raises OverflowError at npz_class_map.py:71 and the save fails; it does not wrap. Reachable in practice because ids are taken from annotation files with no range check — file_manager.py:643 int(ann.get('category_id', 0)) for COCO and file_manager.py:366 int(label) for numeric labels.; Class id 0 is indistinguishable from background in class_map alone; only the foreground key recovers it (npz_class_map.py:30-33; file_manager.py:303-307). The default id for a new class is 0 (segment_manager.py:16, 24), so this is the common case, not a corner. A third-party or pre-2.0.8 file with no foreground key silently drops every class-0 annotation on reload (guarded by tests/unit/core/exporters/test_bbox_roundtrip.py:566-578).; Third-party readers that ignore the foreground key cannot tell class 0 from background; the key is deliberately additive (npz_class_map.py:30-32).; Shape mismatch: a class_map of shape (3, 2) loaded against a 2x3 image (or a 240x320 map against a 480x640 image) logs an error and adds ZERO segments, returning normally rather than raising (file_manager.py:313-318). Test: test_bbox_roundtrip.py:580-589.; Rejection has a side effect the card omitted: _restore_aliases runs at file_manager.py:308, BEFORE the shape gate at :313, so a rejected class map still replaces the whole session class_aliases dict (file_manager.py:343 assigns, it does not merge).; Rejection stops the fallback chain — known legacy defect, do NOT reproduce. Because load_npz_class_map returns normally instead of raising, load_existing_mask reaches its return at file_manager.py:205 and never tries the lower-priority .xml, _createml.json or .txt sidecars. The same happens for a missing class_map key (:298-300) and for OSError/ValueError (:309-311). This contradicts the method's own docstring (file_manager.py:161-163) and CHANGELOG.md:24. Auto-save on navigate is on by default (config/settings.py:45 auto_save = True; file_navigation_manager.py:156-160), and an empty segment list makes the next save delete every annotation file for that image (save_export_manager.py:106-109) — the exact data loss CHANGELOG.md:12 claims was fixed.; Asymmetric failure: a foreground array whose shape does not match class_map raises IndexError at file_manager.py:320, OUTSIDE the try/except at :296-311. load_existing_mask's broad except at :202-204 catches it and DOES continue to the next format — but only after the aliases at :308 were already replaced.; _CM.npz is only ever read when no img.npz, img_seg.txt and img_coco.json exist (file_manager.py:128-136, mirroring exporters.LOAD_PRIORITY at exporters/__init__.py:73-81). Since the default export set is {NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION} (exporters/__init__.py:60-63) and exporting never deletes, an img.npz normally wins and the class map is never read back.; Reload loses overlap data: the class that lost the argmax tie-break loses those pixels permanently, and a class entirely covered by lower ids vanishes from the reloaded segment list.; Per-object separation is lost: exactly one segment per distinct class id, mask = foreground AND class_map == id (file_manager.py:320-331). Two separate objects of one class come back fused.; Reloaded segments are added in ascending class-id order (np.unique), with class_id = int(value), type 'Loaded' and vertices null (file_manager.py:320-331). The guard 'if not np.any(class_mask): continue' at :322-323 is unreachable, since every id came from class_map[foreground].; class_aliases is the full session dict, not filtered to class_order, and may legitimately be {} — get_class_alias falls back to str(class_id) for unnamed classes (segment_manager.py:397-399; save_export_manager.py:424).; The exporter accepts an unsorted or non-contiguous class_order and would then make the FIRST-listed class win overlaps (verified: order [7, 2] gives pixel B = 7). No live code constructs such an order; SaveWorker would allow one but is never instantiated anywhere in src (only re-exported at ui/workers/__init__.py:15, 28). The rewrite must keep the ascending sort or state the rule as 'first channel wins'.; Security: legacy opens the archive with allow_pickle=True (file_manager.py:297) purely to read the pickled class_aliases dict, so a crafted _CM.npz can execute code on open. Per the approved decision, no rewrite code path may unpickle.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** Open question, answered: the card's export Given/When/Then is in fact faithful — I re-ran npz_class_map.py:66-73 verbatim under NumPy 2.2.6 with class_order [2, 7] and got class_map [7, 2, 0] and foreground [true, true, false] exactly as written; the fidelity judge's 'faithful no' verdict applies to a different folded sub-card (the import/size-mismatch scenario, P0_PANEL.md 'NPZ Class Map import requires matching size'), whose Given described a foreground key covering only some 0-valued pixels, which would make np.unique(class_map[foreground]) return [0] and produce no class-4 segment at all (file_manager.py:320) — that scenario is replaced here by the concrete 2x3 round trip in 'and'. On the product-choice half: class_aliases must be written as a JSON string with string keys and read back as {int(k): v} to match legacy's _restore_aliases normalisation (file_manager.py:343); class_map (uint16), foreground (bool) and class_order (int64) stay array-identical to legacy (npz_class_map.py:37-43); per-image class ids and aliases stay as legacy computes them (segment_manager.py:87-95; save_export_manager.py:400, :424); and no reader may enable pickle — a legacy file read with pickling disabled still yields class_map, foreground and class_order correctly and raises ValueError only on the class_aliases key (verified), so the reader must catch that one key and treat aliases as absent, falling back to str(class_id) (segment_manager.py:397-399). What was wrong in the card: (1) edge case 'Class IDs above 65535 overflow' is false — np.array(class_order, dtype=np.uint16) at npz_class_map.py:71 raises OverflowError under numpy\>=2.1.2 (pyproject.toml:23) and nothing catches it in export_all (exporters/__init__.py:189-206); it never wraps, and negative ids fail the same way; this also duplicated edge case 4, so the two are merged; (2) 'No file when no pixel is labelled' is misleading — nothing new is written but nothing is deleted either (npz_class_map.py:21-26 with exporters/__init__.py:189-206), so a stale img_CM.npz survives, including when a crop blanks every pixel before the check (file_manager.py:712-739 applied at save_export_manager.py:412-417); (3) 'tie-break = lowest class' is only true downstream of the ascending sort at segment_manager.py:87-95 — the exporter itself picks the first CHANNEL (argmax, line 70), which coincides with the lowest id only because callers sort, and is the first ACTIVE channel only because the tensor is strictly 0/1 (segment_manager.py:223, 242-244); (4) the card's Given fixed 'Pixel Priority off' without saying that descending priority flips the winner to 7 before the exporter runs (segment_manager.py:356-361); (5) 'and' was null and the card's import edge cases had no scenario, so the whole reload half (file_manager.py:282-333) is now specified; (6) the rejection path was understated — aliases are replaced at file_manager.py:308 before the shape gate at :313, and a rejected file returns normally so load_existing_mask stops at :205 instead of trying .xml/_createml.json/.txt, contradicting its own docstring at :161-163. Implementers must watch three things: keep the ascending class_order sort or the overlap winner silently changes; always write the foreground key, because class id 0 is the default (segment_manager.py:16, 24) and dropping it deletes every class-0 label on reload (file_manager.py:303-307); and do not port the chain-stop at file_manager.py:205 — combined with auto_save = True (config/settings.py:45; file_navigation_manager.py:156-160) it lets one rejected sidecar empty the segment list and trigger delete_all_outputs on the next save (save_export_manager.py:106-109). **Still open for the owner:** Three behaviour choices sit outside the approved NPZ-format decision and need the product owner. (1) When a _CM.npz is rejected (shape mismatch, missing class_map key, unreadable archive), should the loader continue to the lower-priority .xml / _createml.json / .txt sidecars instead of stopping as legacy does at file_manager.py:205? The legacy stop, combined with default auto-save, can delete healthy ground truth. (2) Should a rejected file still be allowed to overwrite the session's class aliases (file_manager.py:308 running before the gate at :313)? (3) Should a save whose mask tensor is empty after cropping delete the stale _CM.npz rather than leaving an out-of-date file that will be reloaded (npz_class_map.py:21-26 with exporters/__init__.py:189-206)? Separately, writing class_aliases as a JSON string is a one-way compatibility break: legacy LazyLabel reading a new file hits AttributeError in _restore_aliases (file_manager.py:343, since .item() returns a str) and, caught by load_existing_mask at :202-204, skips the class map entirely and falls through to the next format — confirm that losing legacy-reader compatibility for new files is acceptable.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-004: Pascal VOC export uses alias names and exclusive max bounds
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/pascal_voc.py:22-63; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:456-493`  
**Plain English:** Each object is written with the class alias as its name and a box whose xmax/ymax equal xmin/ymin plus width/height (exclusive), with fixed pose, truncated and difficult values.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A 640x480 RGB image is open at C:\\ds\\img.png. SegmentManager holds exactly one segment: class_id 2, class_aliases {2: 'dog'}, whose visible pixels (after any crop and pixel-priority resolution) form ONE connected block covering 0-based columns 100-109 and rows 50-59 inclusive (100 px). The export context is built exactly as in save_export_manager.py:394-431 (identically at save_export_manager.py:488-515, save_worker.py:55-97 and main_window.py:6596-6630): - image_size = (pixmap.height(), pixmap.width()) = (480, 640), i.e. (HEIGHT, WIDTH) in that order (save_export_manager.py:397,421; main_window.py:6601). Getting this pair backwards is the single easiest way to break the rewrite. - class_order = SegmentManager.get_unique_class_ids() = [2] - the ASCENDING SORTED list of distinct class_id values (segment_manager.py:87-95). - class_labels[i] = get_class_alias(class_order[i]) = ['dog'] - the alias if one is registered for that class id, otherwise str(class_id) (segment_manager.py:397-399). - instances = [{'class_id': 2, 'channel': 0, 'contours': [the block's single contour]}]. 'channel' is the INDEX of the class inside class_order, not the class id (segment_manager.py:276,283). Contours come from cv2.findContours(RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) applied to each segment's own mask ANDed with the final tensor channel (segment_manager.py:297-311). - mask_tensor is (H, W, C) uint8 with C = len(class_order).  
  When  PascalVocExporter.export(ctx) runs (pascal_voc.py:22-63) - i.e. the user saves while Pascal VOC is among the selected export formats. Pascal VOC is NOT a default: DEFAULT_EXPORT_FORMATS = {NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION} (exporters/__init__.py:60-63), so this path is only reached when the user explicitly selects it.  
  Then  The file C:\\ds\\img.xml is written (path = os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + '.xml', pascal_voc.py:62-63; the parent directory is created if missing, pascal_voc.py:56) and its exact content is: \<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?\> \<annotation\> \<filename\>img.png\</filename\> \<size\> \<width\>640\</width\> \<height\>480\</height\> \<depth\>3\</depth\> \</size\> \<object\> \<name\>dog\</name\> \<pose\>Unspecified\</pose\> \<truncated\>0\</truncated\> \<difficult\>0\</difficult\> \<bndbox\> \<xmin\>100\</xmin\> \<ymin\>50\</ymin\> \<xmax\>110\</xmax\> \<ymax\>60\</ymax\> \</bndbox\> \</object\> \</annotation\> Byte-level facts that a byte-identical rewrite must reproduce (pascal_voc.py:57-59, which calls ET.indent(tree) then tree.write(path, encoding='unicode', xml_declaration=True)): - XML declaration uses SINGLE quotes and lowercase utf-8: \<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?\> - indentation is exactly two spaces per level; element order is fixed: filename, size(width, height, depth), then one object per box with children name, pose, truncated, difficult, bndbox(xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax). - the stream is written in Python text mode, so line separators are the PLATFORM newline: CRLF on Windows, LF on Linux/macOS. Verified on this Windows box - the first bytes are b"\<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?\>\\r\\n\<annotation\>\\r\\n  \<filename\>img.png\</filename\>\\r\\n". - there is NO trailing newline: the file ends with b'\</annotation\>' (verified). - encoding is UTF-8; &, \< and \> inside a class alias are escaped (alias 'a & \<b\>' is written as 'a &amp; &lt;b&gt;', verified). Quotes are NOT escaped in element text. Where each number comes from: width 640 and height 480 are w and h from ctx.image_size unpacked as 'h, w' at pascal_voc.py:23 and written at 28-30. depth is the LITERAL string '3', never derived from the image (pascal_voc.py:31). filename is os.path.basename(ctx.image_path), i.e. the bare file name with no directory (pascal_voc.py:26). name is ctx.class_labels[channel] = 'dog' (pascal_voc.py:37,40). pose 'Unspecified', truncated '0' and difficult '0' are literal strings (pascal_voc.py:41-43). cv2.boundingRect on the contour returns (x, y, bw, bh) = (100, 50, 10, 10) - boundingRect's w/h COUNT BOTH EDGE PIXELS - and the exporter writes xmin=x=100, ymin=y=50, xmax=x+bw=110, ymax=y+bh=60 (pascal_voc.py:36,46-49). So coordinates are 0-BASED and xmax/ymax are EXCLUSIVE: xmax-xmin is the pixel width.  
  And   File-level behaviour, and the import side the same rule must pin down (the writer and reader are one contract; changing either alone corrupts every save/load cycle): (1) If iter_object_contours yields nothing, export returns None, NO file is written, and an existing img.xml is left untouched (pascal_voc.py:33,50,52-53). export_all never deletes anything (exporters/__init__.py:189-206). Old sidecars are only removed when the image has zero segments at all, via a separate delete path (save_export_manager.py:106-109). (2) Reload precondition: FileManager.load_existing_mask walks _LOAD_CHAIN = (.npz, _seg.txt, _coco.json, _CM.npz, .xml, _createml.json, .txt) and reads the FIRST that exists (file_manager.py:128-136,173-205). The VOC file is only read when no .npz, _seg.txt, _coco.json or _CM.npz sits next to the image - which, given NPZ is a default export format, is often not the case. (3) Round-trip for the worked example, VERIFIED by executing the legacy code: navigating to an image calls SegmentManager.clear(), which WIPES class_aliases (segment_manager.py:20-26, called from file_navigation_manager.py:281 and main_window.py:2141). load_pascal_voc_xml then parses one object, label 'dog', box (100, 50, 110, 60) (file_manager.py:472-490), and _add_box_segments clamps it and fills mask[50:60, 100:110] (file_manager.py:394-401). Result: exactly ONE segment {'mask': bool (480,640) True over rows 50-59 and cols 100-109 = 100 px, 'type': 'Loaded', 'vertices': None, 'class_id': 0} and class_aliases == {0: 'dog'}. The PIXELS round-trip exactly; the CLASS ID DOES NOT - class 2 comes back as class 0. The alias text survives, the numeric id does not. (4) Label -\> class id resolution (_build_label_map, file_manager.py:345-379), in this order for the whole file at once: a label equal to an existing alias takes that alias's id; else a label that parses as a Python int takes that integer as its id; else the label is queued and, ONLY AFTER every numeric label has claimed its id, assigned the LOWEST FREE id and registered as a new alias. The two-pass ordering is deliberate: it stops a file mixing 'dog' with '0' from merging both into class 0 (test_bbox_roundtrip.py:525-547). (5) _build_label_map is fed EVERY parsed label BEFORE any box is clamped or dropped (file_manager.py:392). A label whose box is later discarded still consumes a class id and still registers an alias. Verified: an XML whose objects are ['' -\> '0', 'dog' (box collapses), 'cat', 'fox'] yields segments for class 0, 2 and 3 only, yet class_aliases == {1: 'dog', 2: 'cat', 3: 'fox'} - id 1 is burned by an object that produced nothing.  
**Parameters:** Hard-coded constants in the writer (pascal_voc.py): depth = '3' (line 31), pose = 'Unspecified' (41), truncated = '0' (42), difficult = '0' (43), output suffix '.xml' (63). Serialization constants (57-59): two-space indent, XML declaration \<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?\> with single quotes, UTF-8, platform newline (CRLF on Windows), no trailing newline. Coordinate convention: 0-based pixel indices, xmin/ymin inclusive, xmax/ymax EXCLUSIVE, so width = xmax - xmin and height = ymax - ymin. image_size is (height, width). Loader constants (file_manager.py): missing \<name\> text -\> '0' (482); missing bndbox child -\> '0' (483-486); coordinate conversion int(round(float(text))) using Python banker's rounding (halves to even); clamp to [0, w] x [0, h] (395-396); drop when x2 \<= x1 or y2 \<= y1 (397-398); loaded segments get type 'Loaded' and vertices None (406-407). The card's 'parameters' was missing pose/truncated/difficult and every serialization and loader constant.  
**Edge cases handled:** depth is the literal '3' for EVERY image - grayscale, RGBA, 16-bit, anything (pascal_voc.py:31). It is never read back on import, so it is cosmetically wrong but behaviourally inert. Reproduce it as-is.; \<size\> is written but IGNORED on import (file_manager.py:456-493 never reads it). The image size used to rebuild masks is the real image's, passed in by load_existing_mask (file_manager.py:178-185). Boxes are never rescaled to match a stale \<size\>.; pose, truncated and difficult are written and never read back. No import path parses them.; One \<object\> per CONTOUR, not per segment (pascal_voc.py:35 -\> exporters/__init__.py:114-136). A segment made of two disconnected blobs writes TWO objects. Order is: segment order in ctx.instances, then OpenCV contour order within each segment. Confirmed by test_bbox_roundtrip.py:256-268 (two segments of the same class -\> two \<object\> elements).; When ctx.instances is EMPTY the exporter falls back to contouring the merged per-class channel of mask_tensor, iterating channels 0..C-1 in class_order order and skipping all-zero channels (exporters/__init__.py:128-136). In that mode touching or overlapping objects of the same class MERGE into one box, and object order becomes ascending-class-id order. Two different orderings and two different box sets from the same annotations - the rewrite must keep both branches.; Boxes are measured AFTER crop and pixel priority. Each segment's mask is ANDed with the final tensor channel (segment_manager.py:297-299) and the crop zeroes pixels outside the crop rectangle while keeping full-image coordinates (file_manager.py:712-739). A crop can shrink a box, split one segment into several objects, or remove it entirely - but coordinates stay relative to the FULL image, never the crop.; No objects -\> return None, no file written, an existing img.xml is NOT deleted (pascal_voc.py:52-53; exporters/__init__.py:189-206). A stale VOC file therefore survives a save that produced nothing, and will be read back later if it is first in _LOAD_CHAIN.; A class with no alias is named by its id as text, e.g. \<name\>2\</name\> (segment_manager.py:397-399). On import that numeric name is parsed back to the same integer id (file_manager.py:365-368), so UNALIASED classes are the only ones whose id survives a round trip.; An ALIASED class loses its id on the normal navigation path. SegmentManager.clear() wipes class_aliases (segment_manager.py:20-26) before the file is read (file_navigation_manager.py:281; main_window.py:2141), so _build_label_map assigns the lowest free id. Exported class 2 alias 'dog' reloads as class_id 0 with alias {0: 'dog'} - VERIFIED by running the legacy code. Do NOT write a contract test asserting class_id 2 on reload.; Two class ids sharing the same alias string MERGE into one class on reload: _build_label_map keys on the label text (file_manager.py:346,356-358). Aliases are not validated for uniqueness (segment_table_manager.py:88-93 calls set_class_alias unguarded).; An EMPTY alias string serializes as the short form \<name /\> (ElementTree writes ' /\>' when text is falsy - verified). On reload name_el.text is None, so 'name_el.text or "0"' makes the label the string '0' (file_manager.py:482), which claims class id 0 and can merge with a real class 0.; Import skip vs drop are different failures. An object with NO \<name\> element or NO \<bndbox\> element is SKIPPED before parsing (file_manager.py:474-477). An object with a present-but-empty \<xmin/\> gives findtext() == '' -\> float('') raises ValueError -\> that object is skipped (file_manager.py:483-490). An object with a MISSING \<xmax\> or \<ymax\> defaults to '0', survives parsing, then collapses and is DROPPED at the clamp check (file_manager.py:397-398) - but it has already claimed a class id. A missing \<xmin\>/\<ymin\> defaults to 0, i.e. the box is extended to the image edge rather than dropped.; 'nan' as a coordinate raises ValueError from round() and is caught, skipping just that object. 'inf', '1e400' or any overflowing literal raises OverflowError, which the except (ValueError, TypeError) at file_manager.py:489 does NOT catch: it escapes load_pascal_voc_xml, is caught by the blanket handler in load_existing_mask (file_manager.py:202-204), which logs the failure and CONTINUES to the next format in the chain. One bad coordinate therefore discards the ENTIRE XML (zero segments) and silently falls through to _createml.json or .txt. Verified: float('1e400') == inf and round(inf) raises OverflowError.; Fractional coordinates written by other tools go through Python's round(), which is banker's rounding - halves go to the nearest EVEN integer: 100.5 -\> 100 but 101.5 -\> 102 (verified). JavaScript's Math.round() rounds halves UP and will disagree on every .5. A TypeScript port must implement round-half-to-even explicitly.; Boxes are clamped to the image: x1,y1 = max(0, .) and x2,y2 = min(w, .), min(h, .) (file_manager.py:395-396). Verified: a box (-5, -5, 9999, 9999) on a 100x100 image loads as the full image.; Interop with standard Pascal VOC tooling is BROKEN IN BOTH DIRECTIONS and is preserved deliberately. The devkit convention is 1-based with an INCLUSIVE max; LazyLabel is 0-based with an EXCLUSIVE max. For the worked example the devkit would write xmin=101, xmax=110; LazyLabel writes xmin=100, xmax=110. A standard consumer computing width as xmax-xmin+1 gets 11 instead of 10 - the box reads one pixel too wide and too tall, with the extra pixel on the LEFT and TOP edges (the max edges happen to coincide). Conversely a genuine devkit file loaded into LazyLabel fills cols xmin..xmax-1, losing the right-most column and bottom row and sitting one pixel left and up of where it was meant to be.  
**Suspected defect:** Hardcoded depth 3; exclusive xmax/ymax deviates from common VOC usage. | Depth is always '3' even for single-channel images; standard PASCAL VOC boxes are 1-based with inclusive xmax/ymax, so third-party VOC tools will be offset by 1 px and official VOC files read into LazyLabel lose a row/column.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** OPEN QUESTION, ANSWERED: Pascal VOC exports do NOT have to interoperate with standard VOC tooling. Per the approved decisions the rewrite reproduces legacy exports byte-identically, so the legacy convention is preserved and recorded, not fixed; legacy per-image class ids and aliases are kept as-is. Stated plainly, the legacy convention is: coordinates are 0-BASED, xmin/ymin are INCLUSIVE and xmax/ymax are EXCLUSIVE, so xmax-xmin is the pixel width (pascal_voc.py:46-49, whose docstring says so at pascal_voc.py:18-19, and the loader agrees at file_manager.py:459-460 and 400-401). The standard devkit is 1-based with an inclusive max. A consumer of these files should therefore read them as half-open 0-based pixel ranges [xmin, xmax) x [ymin, ymax); if it applies the devkit rule instead it will read every box one pixel too wide and too tall, with the extra pixel on the left and top edges, and if it FEEDS a real devkit file back to LazyLabel the box will lose its right-most column and bottom row. Both directions are accepted, documented deviations. WHAT THE CARD GOT WRONG: (1) 'parameters' listed only depth, the coordinate convention and the suffix - pose='Unspecified', truncated='0' and difficult='0' are equally hard-coded (pascal_voc.py:41-43), and the byte-identical mandate adds a whole class of serialization constants the card never mentioned: two-space ET.indent, the single-quoted \<?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?\> declaration, platform newline (CRLF on Windows) and NO trailing newline (pascal_voc.py:57-59, verified by running the exporter). (2) The 'then' omitted the \<filename\> element, which IS written and holds the bare basename (pascal_voc.py:26). (3) 'and' was null; the empty-export behaviour (no file written, existing .xml not deleted - pascal_voc.py:52-53, exporters/__init__.py:189-206) and the import-side contract belong there, because the writer and reader are one convention. (4) The edge case 'Most VOC consumers read xmax as inclusive, so boxes appear 1 px larger' is half right and imprecise about where the pixel lands - the max values coincide with the devkit's, the discrepancy is entirely at the min edge. (5) 'Class ids are not preserved when aliases exist' understates it: aliased class ids are NEVER preserved on the normal navigation path, because SegmentManager.clear() wipes class_aliases (segment_manager.py:20-26) from file_navigation_manager.py:281 and main_window.py:2141 before the XML is read, so _build_label_map hands 'dog' the lowest free id - I ran the legacy code and class 2 alias 'dog' came back as class_id 0 with class_aliases {0: 'dog'}. (6) The card's Given ('object pixels x 100-109, y 50-59') only produces one \<object\> if those pixels are CONNECTED; export is one object per external contour, not per segment (pascal_voc.py:35, exporters/__init__.py:114-136). WHAT AN IMPLEMENTER MUST WATCH FOR: image_size is (height, width) and is unpacked 'h, w' (pascal_voc.py:23; save_export_manager.py:397,421); 'channel' is the index into the sorted class_order, not a class id (segment_manager.py:276,283); cv2.boundingRect's w/h include both edge pixels, so xmax = x + bw already yields the exclusive bound; the loader's int(round(float(...))) is banker's rounding, which JavaScript's Math.round does not reproduce (file_manager.py:483-486); a missing \<name\> or \<bndbox\> skips the object (file_manager.py:474-477) while a missing \<xmax\> silently drops it AFTER it has already claimed a class id (file_manager.py:392,397-398); and an 'inf' coordinate raises OverflowError past the except at file_manager.py:489, so load_existing_mask (file_manager.py:202-204) discards the whole XML and falls through to the next format. Finally, the comment at tests/unit/core/exporters/test_exporters.py:469 calling VOC 'write-only (no loader)' is STALE - LOADABLE_FORMATS at test_exporters.py:114-122 and file_manager.py:456 both show a loader exists. Do not use that comment to justify skipping the import half of this contract. **Still open for the owner:** Nothing blocks the implementer. Two items are product-owner calls only if scope ever widens beyond byte-identical reproduction: (a) whether the rewrite should surface any warning when it IMPORTS a third-party Pascal VOC file, which the legacy code accepts silently and misreads by one pixel - byte-identical fidelity implies staying silent, so the default is 'no warning'; (b) whether user-facing documentation should state that LazyLabel .xml files are 0-based half-open and not devkit-compatible. Both are additive and neither changes the contract above.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Which VOC bound convention do downstream training tools expect? | Must VOC exports interoperate with standard VOC tooling (1-based inclusive) or only round-trip within LazyLabel? Panel: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-005: YOLO Detection export line format
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/yolo_detection.py:19-46; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/__init__.py:114-136`  
**Plain English:** One line per object: class ID, then box center x and y and box width and height, each divided by the image width or height, using the object's pixel bounding rectangle.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A 640x480 image at \<dir\>/img.png. ExportContext.image_size is (h, w) = (480, 640) -- HEIGHT FIRST (exporters/__init__.py:102; the UI passes (pixmap.height(), pixmap.width()) at save_export_manager.py:397). The segment list holds exactly one segment with class_id 3, so class_order = get_unique_class_ids() = [3] (segment_manager.py:87-95) and that segment's channel index is 0 (segment_manager.py:276). YOLO_DETECTION is instance-aware (exporters/__init__.py:86-94), so ctx.instances is populated by create_instance_contours (save_export_manager.py:445-452, save_worker.py:92-98, main_window.py:6624-6630): the segment's own mask is ANDed with final_mask_tensor[:, :, 0] (segment_manager.py:297-299), i.e. AFTER crop zeroing (file_manager.py:712-739, applied at save_export_manager.py:412-417) and pixel priority (segment_manager.py:247-250). The pixels that survive form ONE 8-connected blob covering columns 100..109 and rows 50..59 inclusive. No crop is active.  
  When  export_all({ExportFormat.YOLO_DETECTION}, ctx) (exporters/__init__.py:189-206) invokes YoloDetectionExporter.export (yolo_detection.py:19-46). This is the ordinary save path: YOLO_DETECTION is one of the two DEFAULT_EXPORT_FORMATS (exporters/__init__.py:60-63).  
  Then  \<dir\>/img.txt -- os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + '.txt' (yolo_detection.py:45-46) -- is created, or an existing file is truncated (open(path, 'w'), line 40), and its parent directory is created if missing (line 39). It receives exactly one line. cv2.boundingRect on that blob returns (x=100, y=50, bw=10, bh=10): the extent INCLUDES both edge pixels, so columns 100..109 give x=100 and bw=10, never 9 (yolo_detection.py:27; re-run against OpenCV 4.12.0). Then cx = (100 + 10/2)/640 = 0.1640625, cy = (50 + 10/2)/480 = 0.11458333333333333, nw = 10/640 = 0.015625, nh = 10/480 = 0.020833333333333332 (yolo_detection.py:29-32). The file content is exactly these bytes -- five fields separated by single ASCII spaces, class token first, no rounding, no truncation, no fixed decimal count, followed by one line terminator (yolo_detection.py:33, 41-42): 3 0.1640625 0.11458333333333333 0.015625 0.020833333333333332\<LF\> The card's '0.114583...' and '0.020833...' are the truncated forms and must never be emitted: the exact digit strings above are the contract. The terminator is CR LF (0x0D 0x0A) when the legacy app runs on Windows, because line 40 opens the file in Python text mode with newline=None (verified: the byte stream is b'3 0.5 0.5 1.0 1.0\\r\\n'); it is LF alone on Linux/macOS.  
  And   (1) GRANULARITY -- one line per OUTER CONTOUR, not per object. iter_object_contours yields every contour of every instance record (exporters/__init__.py:122-126) and create_instance_contours runs cv2.findContours(single, RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) separately per segment (segment_manager.py:303-305). One segment whose surviving pixels fall into two islands therefore writes TWO lines with two separate boxes, never one box around both (verified: islands at rows 10-19/cols 10-19 and rows 100-119/cols 300-339 produced rects (300,100,40,20) and (10,10,10,10), i.e. two lines). Holes are dropped (RETR_EXTERNAL). The docstring phrase 'One line per object' (yolo_detection.py:16) is wrong and must not be copied into the TS implementation. AND (2) LINE ORDER -- lines follow ctx.instances order, which is segment-list order (segment_manager.py:279), and within one segment OpenCV's contour order, which is NOT top-to-bottom: in the verified two-island case the LOWER island (y=100) was emitted first. Lines are never sorted by class id in this path. AND (3) FALLBACK PATH -- when ctx.instances is empty (falsy, so an empty list counts) the exporter instead contours the merged per-class channels of mask_tensor (exporters/__init__.py:128-136). That path emits lines in ascending channel index = ascending class id (class_order is sorted), skips all-zero channels, and FUSES touching or overlapping same-class objects into a single box. AND (4) NUMBER FORMAT -- the four coordinates are Python float repr, not 'full precision' in any looser sense; see parameters for the exact rule and the TS function that reproduces it. The two places a naive JavaScript writer diverges are whole numbers (Python '1.0' vs JS '1') and values below 1e-4 (Python '6.103515625e-05' vs JS '0.00006103515625'). AND (5) EVERY line, including the last, ends with a terminator (yolo_detection.py:42); the file never ends without one and never has a blank last line beyond that terminator. AND (6) CLASS TOKEN -- the decimal integer class_order[channel] (yolo_detection.py:28), i.e. the original class id, never the 0..N-1 channel index and never the alias/label string. AND (7) WRITING NEVER DELETES -- export_all only writes (exporters/__init__.py:189-206, docstring at 190-197); other formats' files sitting beside the image are left untouched.  
**Parameters:** * image_size: tuple (height, width) -- height first (exporters/__init__.py:102; save_export_manager.py:397, save_worker.py:86). h is the divisor for cy and nh, w for cx and nw (yolo_detection.py:20, 29-32). * Output path: os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + '.txt' (yolo_detection.py:45-46) -- only the LAST extension is stripped, so a/b.tar.png -\> a/b.tar.txt and a/b (no extension) -\> a/b.txt. * Guards: return None (write nothing) if h \<= 0 or w \<= 0 (yolo_detection.py:21-22) -- '\<= 0', not '== 0' -- and if no contour produced any annotation (yolo_detection.py:35-36). * File mode: open(path, 'w') -- truncate and overwrite; parent dir created via os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path) or '.', exist_ok=True) (yolo_detection.py:39-40). * Line: f'{class_id} {cx} {cy} {nw} {nh}' + '\\n' -- exactly five fields, one U+0020 between each, no leading or trailing spaces, no header, no comments, no blank lines (yolo_detection.py:33, 41-42). Text mode maps '\\n' to os.linesep, so CRLF on Windows and LF elsewhere. No encoding is passed; content is pure ASCII, so the locale default is irrelevant. * class_id: class_order[channel] as a decimal integer. class_order = sorted unique class ids present IN THIS IMAGE (segment_manager.py:87-95); channel = its index in that list (segment_manager.py:221, 276). * Box: cv2.boundingRect of the contour -- x,y = min column/row, bw,bh = inclusive pixel span (yolo_detection.py:27). A single pixel at (0,0) gives (0,0,1,1). * Math, in exactly this operation order (IEEE-754 doubles then match bit for bit): cx = (x + bw/2)/w; cy = (y + bh/2)/h; nw = bw/w; nh = bh/h (yolo_detection.py:29-32). Do NOT algebraically regroup to (2*x + bw)/(2*w). No rounding, no clamping, no epsilon. * Value ranges: cx and cy are strictly in (0,1); nw and nh are in (0,1] and are exactly 1.0 when the box spans the full width/height. * NUMBER FORMATTING (the part a TS port gets wrong by default). The legacy writer interpolates a Python float with str()/repr(): the shortest decimal digit string that round-trips to the same double, laid out by this rule -- let D be that digit string and decpt the decimal-point position (value = 0.D x 10^decpt). If decpt \<= -4 or decpt \> 16, Python emits exponential form d[.ddd]e followed by an ALWAYS-PRESENT sign and at least two zero-padded exponent digits ('1e-05', '6.103515625e-05', '1e+16'); otherwise positional form, with '.0' appended when there is no fractional part ('1.0', '0.0001'). JavaScript's String(v) uses the same digit string D but different layout rules: no '.0' on whole numbers, and exponential only below 1e-6 (and then with an unpadded exponent, '1e-7' vs Python '1e-07'). Measured over 8013 values produced by real (x+bw/2)/w and bw/w computations across image widths 1..1e7, String(v) differed from Python on 421 of them (416 whole numbers such as nw=1.0, 5 scientific such as 1e-05 and 9.999e-05). Never use toFixed, toPrecision or String(v). Use this function -- it reproduced Python's repr on all 8013 sampled values with zero mismatches: function pyRepr(v: number): string { if (!Number.isFinite(v)) return Number.isNaN(v) ? 'nan' : (v \> 0 ? 'inf' : '-inf'); const neg = v \< 0 || Object.is(v, -0); const a = Math.abs(v); if (a === 0) return neg ? '-0.0' : '0.0'; const [mant, expStr] = a.toExponential().split('e'); // no arg =\> shortest round-trip digits const digits = mant.replace('.', ''); const decpt = parseInt(expStr, 10) + 1;             // Python's decpt let out: string; if (decpt \<= -4 || decpt \> 16) { const m = digits.length \> 1 ? digits[0] + '.' + digits.slice(1) : digits[0]; const e = decpt - 1; out = m + 'e' + (e \< 0 ? '-' : '+') + String(Math.abs(e)).padStart(2, '0'); } else if (decpt \<= 0) { out = '0.' + '0'.repeat(-decpt) + digits; } else if (decpt \>= digits.length) { out = digits + '0'.repeat(decpt - digits.length) + '.0'; } else { out = digits.slice(0, decpt) + '.' + digits.slice(decpt); } return neg ? '-' + out : out; } Realistic triggers: nw/nh = '1.0' whenever a mask touches both edges of the image (common: a loaded NPZ whole-class mask); scientific notation whenever a coordinate drops below 1e-4, i.e. cx for a 1px box at x=0 needs w \> 5000 and nw for a 1px box needs w \> 10000 (an 8192x8192 tile writes '0 6.103515625e-05 6.103515625e-05 0.0001220703125 0.0001220703125').  
**Edge cases handled:** h \<= 0 or w \<= 0: nothing is written and export returns None (yolo_detection.py:21-22). Note the single-view UI never reaches this guard -- _build_export_context raises ValueError('No image loaded (pixmap is empty)') first (save_export_manager.py:398-399) -- so it is reachable only via the worker/multi-view paths or a directly constructed ExportContext.; No contours at all (every channel empty, or every segment's pixels removed by crop/pixel priority): export returns None WITHOUT writing and WITHOUT deleting, so a stale \<base\>.txt from an earlier save survives with outdated boxes (yolo_detection.py:35-36; pinned by test_bbox_roundtrip.py:438-452). Outputs are deleted only when the image has no segments at all, and then for every format at once (save_export_manager.py:106-109 -\> 523-529 -\> exporters/__init__.py:209-215).; ctx.instances empty triggers the merged-channel fallback (exporters/__init__.py:122 tests truthiness). This is not only the no-instance-data case: if every segment is skipped by create_instance_contours -- mask is None or mask.shape[:2] != image_size (segment_manager.py:294-295), or the AND with the final tensor leaves nothing (segment_manager.py:300-301) -- the list comes back empty and the exporter silently switches to contouring the merged channels, changing both line order (ascending class id) and granularity (touching same-class objects fuse).; A segment made of two disconnected blobs produces two lines, each with its own box; the lower blob came first in the verified case, so line order must not be assumed top-to-bottom (segment_manager.py:303-305, exporters/__init__.py:122-126).; Crop does not change the coordinate frame: crop zeroes pixels outside the rectangle (file_manager.py:712-739) but image_size stays the FULL image (save_export_manager.py:397, 412-421), so coordinates are still divided by full width/height and are not shifted to the crop origin. A crop can shrink a box, or split one segment into several lines.; Pixel priority (segment_manager.py:247-250) can likewise shrink or split a box, because each segment's mask is ANDed with the post-priority channel (segment_manager.py:297-299).; Non-contiguous class ids are written as-is: class_order=[3,7] writes tokens '3' and '7' (yolo_detection.py:28; pinned by tests/unit/core/exporters/test_exporters.py:301-320 and test_bbox_roundtrip.py:513-523).; A box spanning the full width or height writes nw/nh as '1.0' (Python), where JS String() would write '1'. cx and cy can never be exactly 0.0 or 1.0 because bw, bh \>= 1.; Any coordinate below 1e-4 is written in Python scientific notation with a two-digit exponent, e.g. a 1x1 px box at (0,0) of an 8192x8192 image writes '0 6.103515625e-05 6.103515625e-05 0.0001220703125 0.0001220703125'. JS String() would write '0.00006103515625' for the first two fields.; Line terminator is platform-dependent in the legacy writer (text mode, yolo_detection.py:40): CRLF on Windows, LF on Linux/macOS. Every line ends with one, including the last.; DO NOT port FileManager.save_bb_txt (file_manager.py:74-123). It writes to the same \<base\>.txt path but emits class_labels[channel] -- the alias NAME, e.g. 'dog' -- instead of the id (line 105), always uses the merged-channel path (lines 96-103), and calls os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_path)) without the `or '.'` fallback (line 118), which raises FileNotFoundError for a bare relative filename. Nothing in the app calls it; only tests do.; The loader reads this file back last in the priority chain (exporters/__init__.py:73-81; file_manager._LOAD_CHAIN:128-136) via load_bb_txt (file_manager.py:412-454): lines with other than 5 whitespace-separated fields or unparseable floats are skipped (lines 432-440), and corners are recovered as int(round((cx -/+ bw/2) * w)) (lines 446-449), i.e. the reload is round-trip-exact for integer pixel boxes but is not the inverse of boundingRect's inclusive convention in general.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** No -- YOLO class indices must NOT be renumbered to a contiguous 0..N-1 range, and a TS port that does so is a data-corruption bug. The legacy writer emits class_order[channel] (yolo_detection.py:28), where class_order is the sorted unique class ids present in that ONE image (segment_manager.py:87-95) and channel is merely the position in that list (segment_manager.py:221, 276); the contiguous 0..N-1 value exists only as the channel index and is never written. That matches the approved decisions (keep legacy per-image class ids and aliases; exports byte-identical to legacy): ids are per-image, therefore sparse and not dataset-global -- the same dataset can legitimately contain img_a.txt with tokens 3 and 7 and img_b.txt with token 0 -- and the app reloads the token as an int or as an existing alias name (file_manager.py:345-376, 436), so renumbering would break the app's own reload; two tests pin it (tests/unit/core/exporters/test_exporters.py:301-320, test_bbox_roundtrip.py:513-523). What the card got wrong: (1) 'One line per object' -- the code writes one line per OUTER CONTOUR; iter_object_contours yields every contour of every instance record (exporters/__init__.py:122-126) and create_instance_contours calls findContours with RETR_EXTERNAL per segment (segment_manager.py:303-305), so a segment split into two islands writes two lines (I reproduced this: rects (300,100,40,20) and (10,10,10,10)). The phrase survives only in a docstring (yolo_detection.py:16) and must not be ported. (2) The 'then' line was truncated ('0.114583...', '0.020833...'); the contract strings are '3 0.1640625 0.11458333333333333 0.015625 0.020833333333333332', which I re-derived by running the exporter's arithmetic against OpenCV 4.12.0 (yolo_detection.py:27-33). (3) 'floats written unrounded (full precision)' is too vague to implement: the f-string at yolo_detection.py:33 is Python float repr, which appends '.0' to whole numbers and switches to two-digit-exponent scientific notation below 1e-4, where JavaScript's String() writes '1' and '0.00006103515625'. Over 8013 values generated from real (x+bw/2)/w and bw/w computations, String(v) differed from Python on 421; the pyRepr function given in parameters matched all 8013. (4) The card stated no line order, no line terminator and no overwrite semantics: order is segment-list order then OpenCV contour order (lower island first in my test), never sorted by class in the instance path, while the fallback path (exporters/__init__.py:128-136) orders by ascending class id and fuses touching same-class objects; every line ends with a terminator that Python text mode writes as CRLF on Windows (yolo_detection.py:40-42, verified byte-for-byte); open(...,'w') truncates and line 39 creates the parent directory. (5) The guard is h \<= 0 or w \<= 0, not just 0 (yolo_detection.py:21-22), and the single-view UI raises before that guard is ever reached (save_export_manager.py:398-399). Two further traps for the implementer: ctx.instances being an EMPTY list silently selects the merged-channel fallback (exporters/__init__.py:122), which can happen in production when a segment's mask shape does not match image_size (segment_manager.py:294-295); and FileManager.save_bb_txt (file_manager.py:74-123) is a dead second writer for the same .txt path that emits the alias NAME instead of the class id (line 105) -- do not use it as the reference implementation. **Still open for the owner:** Two items need the product owner. (1) Line endings: 'byte-identical to legacy output' is ambiguous because the legacy bytes are platform-dependent -- Python text mode (yolo_detection.py:40) writes CRLF on Windows and LF on Linux/macOS. The TS writer must be told whether to mirror the host OS (matching whichever machine the legacy app ran on) or to pin one terminator; golden files captured on Windows will fail an LF writer and vice versa. (2) Byte-identity implies reproducing OpenCV's cv2.findContours (Suzuki-Abe, RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) contour ENUMERATION ORDER, not just the boxes, because line order is observable in the file. The PO should decide whether the TS stack takes an OpenCV binding/WASM build or accepts a hand-ported tracer whose ordering must then be characterised against the legacy output.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Must YOLO class indices be contiguous 0..N-1 across the dataset? Panel: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. | The compliance judge rated this not P0 while the fidelity judge rated it P0. Decide whether it guards annotation data integrity and belongs in the behavior contract. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-006: YOLO Segmentation export polygon simplification
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/yolo_segmentation.py:25-56; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/__init__.py:139-157`  
**Plain English:** One line per object: class ID followed by outer-contour vertices simplified with tolerance 0.1% of the contour perimeter, x divided by width and y by height.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 640x480 image; class 0 object = filled 10x10 square with top-left (100,50)  
  When  Saving YOLO Segmentation  
  Then  img_seg.txt line: '0 0.15625 0.104166... 0.15625 0.122916... 0.1703125 0.122916... 0.1703125 0.104166...' (vertices (100,50),(100,59),(109,59),(109,50))  
**Parameters:** epsilon = 0.001 x contour arc length; output \<base\>_seg.txt; Simplification epsilon = 0.001 x closed arc length (Douglas-Peucker); normalization by width/height; suffix '_seg.txt'  
**Edge cases handled:** A 1-pixel object is written as the same point repeated 4 times; a 1-pixel-wide line as a there-and-back 4-point ring (ARCHITECTURE.md:179-180 says a bounding-box outline is used instead); Holes are not exported (external contours only); Disconnected parts of one segment become separate lines; Holes inside objects are not exported (external contours only); Axis-aligned shapes round-trip to the exact pixel set on reload  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-007: Label text to class ID resolution on import
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:345-379; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:20-26; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2138-2141`  
**Plain English:** For YOLO, VOC and CreateML files a label equal to an existing alias uses that class, a numeric label is used as the class ID, and any other name gets the lowest ID not taken by an alias or numeric label in the file and is registered as an alias; aliases are reset for every image.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A newly opened image; VOC objects in order 'dog', '0', 'cat'  
  When  The file is loaded  
  Then  '0' is class 0, 'dog' class 1, 'cat' class 2; aliases {1: 'dog', 2: 'cat'}  
**Parameters:** New ids assigned starting at 0, skipping ids used by aliases or numeric labels, in first-appearance order  
**Edge cases handled:** Image A lists dog then cat (dog 0, cat 1) and image B lists cat then dog (cat 0, dog 1); re-exporting to YOLO or COCO writes different IDs for the same name; Two classes sharing an alias merge into one on reload; Label '07' becomes class 7; Name-based formats do not preserve numeric ids: classes saved as 3 'dog' and 7 'cat' reload as 0 and 1, so a later YOLO/NPZ export renumbers them; Labels without aliases export as the id text and do round-trip  
**Suspected defect:** Name-based formats cannot round-trip class IDs consistently across a dataset because alias memory is per image.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** ANSWERED BY DECISION 6, which the owner approved: keep legacy per-image class ids and aliases. No project-wide class list. Both questions on the card therefore get the same answer. Class names do NOT map to a stable project-wide list, and per-file renumbering IS accepted for a dataset labelled only in Pascal VOC or CreateML. The reason is not that the defect is imaginary - it is real and the card describes it correctly - but that fixing it would break the thing decision 6 exists to protect. Exported ids must match legacy exports byte for byte, and a project label map changes which id a name resolves to, so every export in the dataset would shift. WHAT THIS COSTS, stated plainly so it is a known cost rather than a surprise. A dataset whose only annotations are name-based (VOC or CreateML) has no stable id space: 'car' can be class 1 in one image and class 2 in the next, depending on what else appears in that file and in what order. Training on such a dataset without re-mapping produces silently wrong labels. That is legacy behaviour and the port reproduces it. WHAT MITIGATES IT, and belongs in Phase 4. The alias table the user has already built is consulted BEFORE the fallback, so a name that matches a known alias keeps its id. The gap only opens for names the session has never seen. So the dataset browser should carry the alias table across images within a session rather than rebuilding it per file, which costs nothing and closes most of the instability in practice. A project label map may be layered on later as an optional VIEW, exactly as decision 6 says: a mapping applied when reading for training, leaving the files untouched. That is a feature, not a fix to this rule.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-008: Detection and polygon exports keep same-class objects separate
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0
