**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:254-315; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/__init__.py:83-136; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:432-452; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ARCHITECTURE.md:212-218`  
**Plain English:** For YOLO Detection/Segmentation, COCO, VOC and CreateML each segment is contoured on its own after crop and pixel priority, so touching objects of one class stay separate and each disconnected part of a segment becomes its own object.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Two touching class-1 segments: A covers x 0-49, B covers x 50-99, both rows 0-49  
  When  Saving YOLO Detection  
  Then  Two lines with 50-px-wide boxes, not one 100-px box  
**Parameters:** Instance-aware formats: YOLO_DETECTION, YOLO_SEGMENTATION, COCO_JSON, PASCAL_VOC, CREATEML; contour mode external  
**Edge cases handled:** Segments reloaded from NPZ or Class Map are one per class, so re-exporting them fuses touching objects (documented as intended); Sequence frames are merged by class on display, so their exports fuse touching objects; A segment whose mask size differs from the image is silently omitted from detection exports; A segment with two separate blobs yields two objects; Identical overlapping segments yield duplicate boxes  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-009: Eraser splits segments and drops pieces of 10 pixels or less
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:646-707; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:752-814; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:509-517; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/polygon_drawing_manager.py:175-198`  
**Plain English:** Erasing removes the erase shape's pixels from every overlapping segment; the remainder is split into 8-connected pieces and only pieces larger than 10 pixels survive as new mask segments of the same class.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A class-2 polygon cut by a Shift+box erase into a 500-pixel piece and an 8-pixel sliver  
  When  The erase is applied  
  Then  The polygon is removed, one 500-pixel class-2 mask segment (type AI) is appended at the end of the list, and the sliver is discarded  
**Parameters:** Minimum surviving piece \> 10 pixels; connectivity 8; minimum kept component \> 10 px  
**Edge cases handled:** Erased polygons and circles lose their vertices and are no longer editable; Affected segments move to the end of the segment order; Erased polygons and circles become masks and lose vertex editing; Segment order changes (survivors move to the end); A mask of different size than the image is nearest-neighbour resized first; Multi-view mirrored erase keeps only the largest contour for polygon views  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; the only P0 judge that returned a verdict rated it P0 and faithful (the other judge failed); 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-010: Final per-class mask composition
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:212-252; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:174-203`  
**Plain English:** Each class gets one binary channel that is the union of all its segments; polygons and circles are rasterized at save time; segments whose class is not in the save order are excluded.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 640x480 image; class 1 has a polygon with corners (100,100),(199,100),(199,199),(100,199) and a separate 50-pixel AI mask  
  When  The image is saved  
  Then  Class 1 channel = 10,000 filled polygon pixels (boundary inclusive) OR 50 mask pixels = 10,050 pixels  
**Parameters:** None  
**Edge cases handled:** Polygon vertices are truncated to integers before filling; A circle whose rounded radius is 0 contributes nothing; A stored mask whose size differs from the image raises; save fails with 'Error saving: ...' and nothing is written  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful

### RULE-011: New segments take the active class, otherwise the next free class id (highest + 1)
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:28-85; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:375-391; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:816-826; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:409-419`  
**Plain English:** A segment added without a class gets the active class, otherwise one more than the highest class id in use. Merging selected segments gives them all the lowest class id among them. Reordering classes renumbers them 0..n-1 in the chosen order.  
**Specification:**  
  Given One image is open in single view. Its SegmentManager holds three segments whose class ids are [0, 0, 3]; class_aliases is {3: "stop sign"}; active_class_id is null (no class is toggled on in the class table); and the cached field next_class_id is 4, because _update_next_class_id ran after the last mutation and max({0,3}) + 1 = 4 (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:816-826). This state is per image and per viewer: opening another image calls clear(), which empties segments and class_aliases and resets next_class_id to 0, active_class_id to null and last_toggled_class_id to null (segment_manager.py:20-26, called from ui/managers/file_navigation_manager.py:281); multi-view keeps one independent SegmentManager per viewer (ui/managers/ai_segment_manager.py:339).  
  When  The user creates one new annotation through any single-view creation path that does not carry a class_id - accepting an AI mask (ai_segment_manager.py:283-284, building the dict at ui/main_window.py:1818-1829), finalizing a polygon (ui/managers/polygon_drawing_manager.py:201-206; ui/modes/single_view_mode.py:279-285), dragging a bounding box (ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:426-432; single_view_mode.py:157-163) or dragging a circle (single_view_mouse_handler.py:517-525) - and then selects the pre-existing class-3 segment together with the new segment in the segment table and presses M ("Merge Selected Segments", config/hotkeys.py:83 -\> main_window.py:1006 -\> main_window.py:2000-2005 -\> ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:236-239 -\> ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:53-57 -\> segment_manager.assign_segments_to_class).  
  Then  Add: the incoming object has no "class_id" key, so add_segment fills one in (segment_manager.py:34). active_class_id is null, so the else branch applies and class_id = next_class_id = 4 (:38-39). last_toggled_class_id is set to 4 (:42), the segment is appended (:55), and only after the append is next_class_id recomputed (:56) to max({0,3,4}) + 1 = 5. Segments are now [0, 0, 3, 4] and next_class_id is 5. Merge: assign_segments_to_class([2, 3]) collects the non-null class ids of the selection, [3, 4], and takes their minimum, 3 (:70-77); it writes class 3 onto both selected segments (:81-83) and recomputes next_class_id (:85) over {0, 3} to max + 1 = 4. Final state: segments [0, 0, 3, 3], next_class_id back down to 4, active_class_id still null, last_toggled_class_id still 4 (assign_segments_to_class never touches it). Both merged segments now export under alias "stop sign", and the id 4 that was just handed out is free again and will be handed to the next unclassed annotation.  
  And   The order of checks in add_segment is exactly: (1) if the incoming object already has a class_id key, use its value verbatim and skip steps 2 and 3 - this holds even when the value is null (segment_manager.py:34); (2) otherwise, if active_class_id !== null, use active_class_id - the test is an explicit null check, not a truthiness check, so an active class 0 is honored (:36-37); (3) otherwise use the cached next_class_id (:38-39). Then always set last_toggled_class_id to the class actually used, defaulted or supplied (:42), append the segment (:55), and only then recompute next_class_id (:56). next_class_id is a cached field, never derived on demand at read time: _update_next_class_id runs after add (:56), delete (:63), assign/merge (:85), merge_segments_by_class (:172), reassign_class_ids (:391), and both erase paths - the erase paths only when at least one segment was actually modified (:581, :705). No other code writes class_id, so the cache is never stale. This is why the assigned id is annotation data and not a UI convenience: get_unique_class_ids() returns the sorted distinct ids (:87-95); every save path passes that list as class_order (ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:400 single view, :494 multi-view, main_window.py:6602); create_final_mask_tensor maps class_order[i] to output channel i and drops any segment whose id is not in the list (:221-223, :227-228); and class_labels is the alias of each id in the same order (save_export_manager.py:401-403, :495). core/exporters/npz.py:23-25 writes exactly that mask tensor, class_order and class_aliases. A different id therefore changes which channel a mask lands in, which name it is exported under, and whether two objects collapse into one class.  
**Parameters:** next_class_id = max({ s.class_id : s in segments, s.class_id !== null }) + 1, or 0 when that set is empty (segment_manager.py:816-826). Initial state and clear(): segments [], class_aliases {}, next_class_id 0, active_class_id null, last_toggled_class_id null (:13-26). active_class_id: int | null. toggle_active_class(id) always records last_toggled_class_id = id first, then clears active_class_id and returns false if it already equals id, otherwise sets it and returns true (:409-419); this toggle is reached only from the single-view class table (main_window.py:2699). set_active_class(id) assigns unconditionally and is what multi-view class clicks and the reassign flow use (:401-403; main_window.py:6446, 6456, 7211). Merge (assign_segments_to_class) target = min of the non-null class ids among the selected indices, else next_class_id when the selection has none (:70-79); an empty index list returns immediately and recomputes nothing (:67-68); indices past the end of the list are skipped (:73, :82). reassign_class_ids(new_order) builds id_map = {new_order[i]: i}, rewrites every segment whose id is a key of that map (:379-382), then rebuilds class_aliases as {id_map[old]: alias} for old ids that currently have an alias, which drops aliases for ids absent from new_order (:385-390), and finally recomputes next_class_id (:391). new_order comes from the class table, which lists only ids that have segments, in the user's drag order (ui/right_panel.py:361-371 via segment_table_manager.py:96-100).  
**Edge cases handled:** Empty image: with no segments next_class_id is 0, so the first new annotation gets class 0 (segment_manager.py:823-824; pinned by tests/unit/core/test_segment_manager.py:108-119).; Active class 0 must be honored: the guard is active_class_id !== null, not a truthiness test (segment_manager.py:36). A TypeScript port written as `if (activeClassId)` would wrongly start a new class whenever class 0 is active.; The default fires only when the class_id key is ABSENT (segment_manager.py:34). An explicit class_id of null is preserved as null; such a segment is skipped by _update_next_class_id (:819-822) and dropped from every export because its id is not in class_order (:227-228, :91-93).; Gaps are never filled by this rule: ids {0, 3} give 4, not 1; ids {0, 4} give 5 (segment_manager.py:826).; next_class_id can go DOWN and ids are reused: with classes {0, 1, 4}, deleting every class-4 segment leaves max 1, so next_class_id becomes 2 (delete_segments :58-63 then :826). It also goes down after a merge, as in the worked example above (5 back to 4).; Alias leak on id reuse (likely defect): delete_segments and assign_segments_to_class never prune class_aliases - only clear() (:23) and reassign_class_ids (:385-390) touch it. With classes {0, 3} and alias {3: "stop sign"}, deleting every class-3 segment sets next_class_id to 1; the next three new annotations get 1, 2, then 3 - and that third one silently inherits the name "stop sign" in exports (save_export_manager.py:401-403, :495).; Reassign Class IDs renumbers the in-use classes to 0..n-1 in class-table order (segment_manager.py:375-391). It does NOT update active_class_id, so after a reassign the active class can point at a different class or at one that no longer exists. The `str(old_id)` fallback at :386 is unreachable dead code, guarded out by `if old_id in self.class_aliases` at :388 - do not port it as a behavior.; Merge (M) does NOT use the active class, despite the docstrings at keyboard_event_manager.py:237 and segment_manager.py:66 saying so. It uses the minimum of the selected segments' ids (:77), and only falls back to next_class_id when none of the selected segments has a non-null id (:79).; Aliases never reserve ids: _update_next_class_id reads segments only (segment_manager.py:818-822), so a named class with no segments does not hold its number.; Multi-view AI accept duplicates the active-else-next logic in the CALLER and then sets class_id explicitly, so add_segment's own default is bypassed (ai_segment_manager.py:366-371; main_window.py:6879-6885). Each viewer uses its own manager, so in linked mode with no active class the two viewers pick ids independently and can diverge.; Linked polygon finalize is the one exception: a single shared id is computed from the primary viewer BEFORE finalizing and passed to both viewers (main_window.py:5619-5650, 6990-7006). Multi-view bbox and circle set no class_id and fall through to add_segment's per-viewer default (main_window.py:5815, 5909).; "Click the same class again to deactivate" is single-view only: multi-view class clicks call set_active_class, not toggle_active_class (main_window.py:6446, 6456 vs :2699).; active_class_id is never validated against existing classes and is not cleared when its last segment is deleted, so a new annotation can join a class that currently has no segments.; Load paths cannot be affected: every NPZ/JSON/annotation load passes an explicit class_id (core/file_manager.py:259, 273, 324, 403, 590, 672, 701), so a file whose highest id is 250 simply leaves next_class_id at 251 after the last add_segment recompute (:56).; Annotation IMPORT uses a DIFFERENT allocator - the lowest free id not already taken by an alias or by a numeric label (file_manager.py:370-377) - and must not share an implementation with this rule.; Undo/redo never triggers the default: undo of an add calls delete_segments, which recomputes (core/undo_redo_manager.py:155), and redo re-adds the saved dict, which already carries class_id (:367, :373, :591, :597, :647, :652). AI-to-polygon conversion also preserves class_id (segment_manager.py:882, :900).  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** Answered: yes, this rule guards annotation data integrity, and the approver restored it to P0 on 2026-09-17 - the id assigned here IS the id written to every export. get_unique_class_ids() returns the sorted distinct ids (segment_manager.py:87-95); every save path hands that list to create_final_mask_tensor as class_order (save_export_manager.py:400, :494; main_window.py:6602); the function maps class_order[i] to output channel i and silently drops any segment whose id is missing from the list (segment_manager.py:221-223, :227-228); and the parallel class_labels list is the alias of each id in the same order (save_export_manager.py:401-403, :495), which npz.py:23-25 persists alongside the mask. So reusing an existing id merges two distinct objects into one channel, and choosing a different free id shifts every higher class's channel and changes the exported name - the compliance judges' "the user can see and fix it" argument does not survive the fact that the save happens without re-validating the id. Three things in the card were wrong. (1) The When/Then were internally inconsistent: the Given establishes classes {0, 3}, so after the add the classes are {0, 3, 4} and there is no class 5 to select; the corrected example selects the class-3 and class-4 segments and lands on 3 via min() at segment_manager.py:77. (2) The Then ended on the vague phrase "recalculated as the highest remaining class + 1" instead of a number; it is 4, because _update_next_class_id runs over the post-merge set {0, 3} (segment_manager.py:85, :816-826). (3) The parameters line "next_class_id = max(existing ids) + 1; 0 when empty" omitted that the None-valued ids are filtered out (:819-822) and, more importantly, that next_class_id is a CACHED field recomputed only at :56, :63, :85, :172, :391, :581 and :705 - an implementer who derives it lazily on read will diverge on the erase paths, which recompute only when modified_indices is non-empty. The card also duplicated an edge case ("gaps are never reused" twice, lines 18 and 20) and stated "deleting all class-4 segments makes the next new class 2" without the surrounding classes that make it true. What an implementer must watch for: the active-class test is `!== null`, not truthiness, so class 0 works as an active class (:36); the default applies only when the class_id KEY is absent, not when its value is null (:34); the id is picked BEFORE the append and the recompute, so the new segment's own id never influences its own assignment (:39, :55-56); merge ignores the active class despite its docstring (keyboard_event_manager.py:237); next_class_id can go down and ids are reused, and because delete never prunes class_aliases (only :23 and :385-390 do) a reused id silently inherits the old class name in exports; reassign_class_ids does not update active_class_id (:375-391); multi-view keeps one manager per viewer and reimplements the fallback in the caller (ai_segment_manager.py:366-369; main_window.py:6879-6882), except for linked polygon finalize which shares one id (main_window.py:5619-5630); and the annotation-import allocator at file_manager.py:370-377 is a different algorithm (lowest free id not taken by an alias) that must not be folded into this one. **Still open for the owner:** Two product-owner calls, both outside the scope of a faithful port. (1) Alias reuse: because delete_segments never prunes class_aliases (segment_manager.py:58-63; only :23 and :385-390 touch the table), an id that is freed and later re-handed-out silently inherits the deleted class's name and exports under it (save_export_manager.py:401-403, :495). The contract above preserves this legacy behavior, but the owner should say whether it is intended or a defect to fix in the rewrite. (2) Linked multi-view inconsistency: an AI accept picks a class id independently per viewer (ai_segment_manager.py:366-369), while a linked polygon finalize shares one id computed from the primary viewer (main_window.py:5619-5630, 6990-7006), so the same user gesture can yield matching or diverging ids depending on the tool used. The owner should decide which of the two is the intended contract.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The compliance and fidelity judges rated this not P0. Decide whether it guards annotation data integrity and belongs in the behavior contract. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-012: Pixel priority resolves overlapping classes
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:317-373; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/settings_widget.py:68-102; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:60-62; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2721-2728`  
**Plain English:** With Pixel Priority enabled, a pixel claimed by several classes keeps only the lowest channel (Ascending) or highest channel (Descending); pixels with a single class are untouched.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Classes 1 and 4 present (channels 0 and 1); pixel (10,10) covered by both; Pixel Priority enabled  
  When  Saving with Ascending / with Descending  
  Then  Ascending: pixel (10,10) stays only in class 1; Descending: only in class 4  
**Parameters:** pixel_priority_enabled default False; pixel_priority_ascending default True; Default enabled False, ascending True; priority by channel index = ascending class id  
**Edge cases handled:** Ranking is by channel index = ascending class ID, while the recent-class hotkey uses the table order when priority is on; Disabled: NPZ keeps overlaps, NPZ Class Map gives overlap to the lowest class ID; Detection exports use the prioritized tensor, so a lower-priority object can shrink or split into several boxes; Detection formats intersect each object with the prioritized tensor, so an object fully covered by a higher-priority class disappears from YOLO/COCO/VOC/CreateML; 'Reassign Class IDs' changes ids and therefore priority  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-013: Reassign class ids from class table order
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:375-391; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:96-100; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:338-366; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/right_panel.py:361-371`  
**Plain English:** 'Reassign Class IDs' renumbers classes 0..N-1 in the current table row order and carries aliases along.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Class table rows ordered 7, 2, 5 after dragging; aliases {7:'car', 2:'person'}  
  When  The user clicks 'Reassign Class IDs'  
  Then  7 -\> 0, 2 -\> 1, 5 -\> 2; aliases become {0:'car', 1:'person'}  
**Parameters:** new id = row position  
**Edge cases handled:** The table is rebuilt in ascending id order on every refresh, so a dragged order is lost unless reassigned first; Aliases for classes with no segments are discarded; Aliases for classes not in the table are dropped; A segment whose class is missing from the order keeps its old ID and can collide with a new one; Renumbering is per image, so IDs diverge across images; Not undoable  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; the only P0 judge that returned a verdict rated it P0 and faithful (the other judge failed); 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-014: Saved class channel order is ascending class ID, not the Class Order table
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:87-95; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/right_panel.py:180-198; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:338-368; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:394-430; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/npz.py:15-37`  
**Plain English:** The NPZ channels and every exporter's class list are the distinct class IDs present in the image sorted ascending; dragging rows in the Class Order table has no effect on saving unless 'Reassign Class IDs' is pressed.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Segments with class IDs 5, 0 and 2; the Class Order table dragged to 5, 2, 0  
  When  The image is saved as NPZ  
  Then  mask has 3 channels in order class 0, class 2, class 5 and class_order = [0, 2, 5]  
**Parameters:** Keys mask/class_order/class_aliases; dtype uint8; compressed; class_order = sorted unique class ids of current segments  
**Edge cases handled:** Channels include only classes present in this image, so class 5 is channel 2 here but may be channel 0 in another image; consumers reading channel index as class ID mislabel; Any list refresh rebuilds the table sorted by ID, discarding the drag order; Class ids with no pixels (e.g. phantom segments) still get an empty channel; Written even when every channel is empty after crop (only a zero-size tensor is skipped); Channel count varies per image  
**Suspected defect:** Class table tooltip says 'drag to reorder channels for saving' (right_panel.py:181-183) but save ignores table order.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** TWO QUESTIONS, TWO ANSWERS, and the second is decision 6. SHOULD NPZ USE A PROJECT-WIDE CLASS LIST? No. Decision 6, approved: per-image class ids. Channels are the distinct class ids present in THIS image, ascending. Same reasoning as RULE-007 - a project-wide list changes the exported bytes. SHOULD CHANNEL ORDER FOLLOW THE CLASS TABLE? No, and this half is a specification correction rather than a product choice. The save path sorts the ids ascending and ignores the table entirely; dragging rows changes nothing until 'Reassign Class IDs' is pressed, which actually renumbers the ids. Making the order follow the table would change every NPZ channel order and every exporter's class list, which decision 10's byte-identity requirement forbids. WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG HERE IS THE TOOLTIP. `right_panel.py:181-183` tells the user 'drag to reorder channels for saving', and that is simply false. The rewrite must not carry the sentence over. Dragging reorders the DISPLAY; renumbering is a separate, explicit action with a button, and the UI should say so. Shipping a corrected label is not a behaviour change - the behaviour was always this - it is the removal of a lie about it. So: the channel rule is preserved exactly (ascending class id, per image), the table stays a display ordering, and the wording is fixed. Phase 4 owns the wording; Phase 1 already implements the channel rule and proves it byte-identical.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-015: Shape rasterization before export (truncated vertices, rounded circles)
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:174-252; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:384-438`  
**Plain English:** Before export, polygons and boxes are filled using vertex coordinates truncated toward zero with edges included, circles use a rounded center and rounded radius, and all segments of a class are OR-ed into one channel.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A user drags a box from (10.7, 10.2) to (20.9, 30.8)  
  When  The image is saved  
  Then  Vertices become (10,10)-(20,30); 11 x 21 = 231 pixels are filled; COCO bbox [10,10,11,21] and YOLO width 11/image width  
  And   A circle with center (20.4, 20.6) and radius point 5.4 px away becomes center (20,21), radius 5, 81 pixels; a radius that rounds to 0 produces no pixels  
**Parameters:** Vertex cast to int32 (truncation); cv2.fillPoly boundary inclusive; circle radius = round(distance); center = round(x), round(y)  
**Edge cases handled:** Boxes export 1 px larger than the drawn span; Out-of-image vertices are clipped by the rasterizer  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-016: Propagation confidence score per object
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:1029-1044; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:893-909; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:683-690`  
**Plain English:** For each object in each frame the mask is the pixels with logit \> 0, and confidence = sigmoid(mean of those positive logits); an object with no positive logits scores 0.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An object's positive logits average 4.0 (or 5.0)  
  When  The frame is propagated  
  Then  Confidence 0.982 (or 0.9933)  
**Parameters:** Threshold 0.99 corresponds to a mean positive logit of about 4.595; Logit threshold 0; predictor overrides: dynamic multimask stability delta 0.05, stability threshold 0.98, fill_hole_area 8, binarize_mask_from_pts_for_mem_enc true  
**Edge cases handled:** Score ignores mask size; a tiny confident fragment can score high; Confidence ignores mask size, so tiny confident masks score high; Empty masks are skipped before flagging  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** P0, OVERRIDING THE COMPLIANCE JUDGE. The panel split on whether this rule guards annotation data integrity, and the disagreement comes from reading the card as being about a SCORE when half of it is about a MASK. The card states two things in one sentence: the propagated mask for an object in a frame is the pixels whose logit is greater than 0, and the confidence reported for that object is sigmoid(mean of those positive logits), with 0 when there are none. The first clause decides which pixels are written into the user's annotation files. There is no weaker reading of that: change the threshold and a propagated sequence exports different polygons, different boxes and different NPZ planes, for every frame the user did not hand-correct. That is the same class of rule as every P0 already in the contract, and it is P0 for the same reason. The second clause is triage rather than content - the score does not change a pixel - but it is not merely cosmetic either, because decision 9 persists the confidence threshold with the other settings and the sequence review flags frames against it. A score computed differently flags a different set of frames, so a user reviews a different subset and ships the unreviewed remainder. That is a P1-strength concern riding on a P0 clause, which is the shape that makes splitting the card worse than keeping it whole. So: the whole card is P0, with the understanding that the binding part is the mask derivation and the score must be reproduced because the flagging threshold is user-visible and persisted. This is exactly the revisit decision 14 scheduled. Decision 14 restored the split-panel rules whose work lands in Phase 1 or Phase 4 and left the P3-and-P6-only ones at P1 to be reconsidered at their phase entry, precisely so that the compliance lens's 'no money, no regulation' bias - which `P0_PANEL.md` records being applied to 37 of 74 cards - would not quietly settle them. This card lands in Phase 3. This is the entry. The fidelity judge was right. FOR PHASE 3. Both halves are testable without matching floating point bit for bit. The mask is `logits \> 0`, compared by IoU against the legacy mask under decision 10's 0.98 tolerance. The score is `sigmoid(mean(logits[logits \> 0]))` with the documented 0 for an all-negative object, compared numerically; an object whose mask is empty must score 0 rather than NaN, which is what `mean` of an empty selection would otherwise produce and what the legacy code's own guard exists to prevent. Reproduce that guard rather than relying on the arithmetic to be kind.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; the only P0 judge that returned a verdict rated it P0 and faithful (the other judge failed); 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-017: SAM 2 frame staging numbering gap
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:759-803; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:778-781`  
**Plain English:** Frames are staged as sequentially numbered JPEGs named by list position (00000.jpg, 00001.jpg...), and an unreadable non-JPEG frame is skipped without renumbering.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Frames [a.png, b.png (unreadable), c.png] and no reference size filter  
  When  Propagation initializes the video predictor  
  Then  Staged files are 00000.jpg and 00002.jpg; if SAM 2 indexes frames by sorted position, its frame 1 is c.png while LazyLabel maps index 1 to b.png  
**Parameters:** JPEG quality 95 for converted or cached frames; existing JPEGs are symlinked or copied  
**Edge cases handled:** When the reference size is known, the size filter already drops unreadable frames, reducing exposure  
**Suspected defect:** Masks for every frame after an unreadable image may be attributed to the wrong image.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** ANSWERED BY READING THE INSTALLED SAM 2, AND THE DEFECT IS REAL. The question was whether the SAM 2 video loader indexes frames by sorted list position or by the number in the file name. It does BOTH, in the order that makes the gap dangerous: `sam2/utils/misc.py::load_video_frames_from_jpg_images` collects every .jpg/.jpeg in the staging folder and sorts them with `frame_names.sort(key=lambda p: int(os.path.splitext(p)[0]))` - so the file NAME decides the order - and the predictor then addresses frames by their POSITION in that sorted list, 0, 1, 2, and so on. The number in the name is used to sort and then thrown away. So with legacy's staging, which names each frame by its position in the app's own list and skips an unreadable frame WITHOUT renumbering, a sequence whose third image is a corrupt PNG stages as 00000.jpg, 00001.jpg, 00003.jpg, 00004.jpg. SAM 2 sorts those to [00000, 00001, 00003, 00004] and calls them frames 0, 1, 2, 3. Mapping SAM 2's frame 2 back to the app's list position 2 attributes the mask for image 00003 to image 00002. EVERY frame after the gap is off by one, and the shift grows by one for each additional unreadable frame. What the user sees is not an error. It is a propagation that looks like it worked, with masks on the wrong images from the gap onward, saved to the wrong sidecars by auto-save. Nothing in the legacy code notices, because both sides of the mapping are plausible integers. PRIORITY: P0, overriding the compliance judge. The panel split on whether this 'guards annotation data integrity'. It does, in the most direct sense available: it decides which image a mask is written next to. A rule that can silently attach one frame's annotations to a different frame's file is data integrity whether or not money or regulation is involved, and decision 14 already records that the compliance lens under-rated exactly this class of rule. Decision 14 deferred the P3-and-P6-only rules to their phase entry; this is that entry, and the answer is to restore it. THE FIX, for Phase 3. Do not encode identity in the staged file name and then recover it by position. Stage frames under dense, gapless indices 0..n-1 and keep an explicit `staged_index -\> source_image_key` map alongside; every result coming back from SAM 2 is resolved through that map, never by arithmetic on the index. An image that cannot be staged is a typed, reported failure that either excludes the frame from the job (with the map reflecting that) or fails the job, never a silent skip. The map also makes the round trip testable without a model: stage a sequence with a deliberate hole and assert that every returned index resolves to the image it came from. **Still open for the owner:** Whether a sequence containing an unreadable frame should propagate at all (excluding that frame) or refuse until the file is fixed. Excluding is friendlier and is what the fix above assumes; refusing is safer if a user would rather know immediately. Either is sound once the index map exists - the defect is the silent renumbering, not the choice between these two.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Does the SAM 2 video loader index frames by sorted list position (making numbering gaps shift frames), or by the number in the file name? Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-018: Crop is clamped to the image and blanks everything outside it on save, including the last row and column
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/crop_manager.py:47-57; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/crop_manager.py:102-153; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:712-739; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:412-417; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:539-561; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2148-2156; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/file_navigation_manager.py:227-238`  
**Plain English:** Crop corners are rounded, clamped to 0..width-1 and 0..height-1, and put in order. On save, mask pixels outside rows y1..y2-1 and columns x1..x2-1 are cleared. The crop applies only to the current image.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A 1000x800 image (width=1000, height=800) is open in single view (sequence view behaves identically; multi-view has no crop at all). At least one segment exists, so a save will write files. The Border Crop panel takes two text ranges rather than two corners: X = "-10:1200" and Y = "50:700", i.e. corners (-10, 50) and (1200, 700). Each half is parsed as a plain integer with no range validation, and the widget swaps a reversed pair before handing (x1, y1, x2, y2) to the crop manager (border_crop_widget.py:115-135).  
  When  The crop is applied - either typed into the panel, or drawn by dragging a rectangle that is strictly wider than 5 AND strictly taller than 5 image pixels - and the image is then saved through the single-view/sequence save path (save_export_manager.py:97-122).  
  Then  The crop is stored as (x1, y1, x2, y2) = (0, 50, 999, 700). x is clamped to [0, width-1] = [0, 999], so -10 becomes 0 and 1200 becomes 999; y is clamped to [0, height-1] = [0, 799], so 50 and 700 pass through unchanged; no swap is needed (crop_manager.py:131-140). The X field is rewritten to "0:999", the Y field to "50:700", and the toast reads exactly "Crop applied: 0:999, 50:700" (crop_manager.py:148, 152). On save, the export mask tensor of shape (800, 1000, numClasses), dtype uint8, is COPIED, and in the copy every pixel outside rows [50, 700) x columns [0, 999) is set to 0 in every channel: rows 0-49 cleared across the full width, rows 700-799 cleared across the full width, and, within the surviving rows 50-699, column 999 cleared (the left band [0, x1) is empty here because x1 = 0). Exactly 650 rows x 999 columns = 649,350 pixel positions per channel survive. The row at index y2 (700) and the column at index x2 (999) are themselves cleared: the END COORDINATES ARE EXCLUSIVE, the start coordinates inclusive.  
  And   Because the clamp is inclusive (x2 \<= width-1, y2 \<= height-1) while the slice ends are exclusive, the image's last column (index 999) and last row (index 799) can NEVER survive while any crop is active - not even for the maximum crop (0, 0, 999, 799), which keeps only rows 0-798 and columns 0-998. The blanking runs on a copy of the tensor (file_manager.py:720), so the in-memory segments are untouched and clearing the crop restores full-image saves. All exported coordinates stay in the FULL-image frame: the tensor keeps its full (height, width) shape, exporters normalize by the full width/height (e.g. yolo_detection.py:20, 29-32), and no exporter ever reads the crop rectangle itself (it is only a dead field, exporters/__init__.py:107). Per-object exports intersect each segment with the already-cropped tensor (segment_manager.py:297), so YOLO/COCO/VOC/CreateML boxes shrink to the kept region. The crop belongs to the current image only: loading any other image clears it, and it must NOT be restored for a later image of the same dimensions.  
**Parameters:** Input: x1, y1, x2, y2 in image pixels, integers. TEXT PATH - each field is "start:end"; each half is parsed as an integer with no range check (negatives and values past the image edge are accepted), then the pair is swapped if reversed (border_crop_widget.py:115-135). DRAG PATH - the rectangle is built from normalized scene coordinates, which are image pixels, not screen pixels (single_view_mouse_handler.py:264); the press must land inside the image but the drag may leave it (:106-110); it is accepted only if rectWidth \> 5 AND rectHeight \> 5 (strictly greater, both axes, evaluated on the FLOAT rectangle BEFORE truncation and clamping, single_view_mouse_handler.py:553); its edges are then converted by TRUNCATION TOWARD ZERO, not rounding (Python int(), :554-555), so a drag edge at 999.7 becomes 999. Typed coordinates bypass the \>5 check entirely. CLAMP - x1, x2 = max(0, min(x, width-1)); y1, y2 = max(0, min(y, height-1)) (crop_manager.py:131-134). ORDER - swap so x1 \<= x2 and y1 \<= y2 (crop_manager.py:137-140); a no-op on every live path because both entry paths already order the pair. KEPT REGION in the mask - rows [y1, y2) x columns [x1, x2), both ends EXCLUSIVE, across all channels (file_manager.py:724-737). DO NOT PORT the legacy per-image-size crop memory (crop_manager.py:144-145) or its restore-on-load (file_navigation_manager.py:227-238): both are unreachable in the shipping app and the decision is that a crop never carries over. Implement truncation toward zero on the drag path and integer parsing on the text path; never a round-half rule - the legacy round() at crop_manager.py:127 is a documented no-op because both callers already pass integers, and Python's round() is half-to-even, which JavaScript's Math.round does not match.  
**Edge cases handled:** Maximum crop: (0, 0, 999, 799) on a 1000x800 image still keeps only rows 0-798 and columns 0-998. There is no way to express a crop that keeps the whole image; preserve this exactly (crop_manager.py:131-134 plus file_manager.py:728, 736).; Degenerate crop: if clamping collapses the rectangle so x1 == x2 or y1 == y2, the kept region is empty and the ENTIRE saved mask becomes zero, wiping every annotation from the export. Reachable from a drag: on a 100-px-wide image, press at x=99.4 and release at x=110 passes the \>5 gate (width 10.6) but clamps to x1 = x2 = 99 (single_view_mouse_handler.py:553-556, crop_manager.py:132).; A drawn rectangle that is \<= 5 px on either axis is silently discarded: no crop is set, no message is shown, the app STAYS in crop mode, and any previously active crop is left untouched (single_view_mouse_handler.py:548-558).; A successful drawn crop switches the app out of crop mode into the AI point mode (legacy mode name 'sam_points') (single_view_mouse_handler.py:557-558).; The active crop is cleared when another image is loaded (file_navigation_manager.py:279 -\> main_window.py:2148-2156) and also when the SAM model is switched or unloaded (main_window.py:1261, 1287 -\> 2956, 3003). It is never restored for a later image of the same size - that legacy restore is not being ported.; Multi-view has no crop. Applying, clearing or resetting a crop in multi-view calls _apply_multi_view_crop_coordinates / remove_multi_view_crop_visual (crop_manager.py:107, 88, 426), which are defined nowhere in the source and raise AttributeError, and the multi-view save builds its export context with no crop field at all (save_export_manager.py:504-514). Port as: crop unavailable in multi-view, multi-view saves never crop.; Sequence 'Save All' writes every propagated frame through the single-view save path while the on-screen frame's crop is still active, so the one active crop is applied to all of the frames it writes, and the mask dimensions come from the on-screen pixmap (main_window.py:4810-4813 -\> save_export_manager.py:396-397, 412-417).; Clearing the crop drops it from the active state and from the size-keyed memory for the current image size, and restores full-image saves; it does not repair anything already written to disk (crop_manager.py:70-100).; If a crop removes every object, the NPZ is still rewritten as an all-zero tensor (npz.py:15-27 writes whenever mask_tensor.size != 0), while the other format files already on disk are left untouched because export_all never deletes (exporters/__init__.py:189-206). Stale labels are then left beside an empty mask.; Channel threshold, FFT threshold and rescale operate on image[y1:y2, x1:x2] when a crop is active - the same exclusive convention - and leave pixels outside the crop untouched in the displayed image (image_adjustment_manager.py:645-663; rescale via :393-394 and :622-623).; The crop blanking is never applied to the image fed to the AI/SAM model, and the crop rectangle is not part of the embedding cache key (image_adjustment_manager.py:637-638). Caveat: with 'operate on view' enabled the key is a hash of the displayed pixels (main_window.py:1580-1587), so changing the crop while a channel threshold, FFT or rescale is active does change the key indirectly.; Do not port or test against FileManager.save_npz / save_bb_txt (file_manager.py:47-49, 89-91) or SaveWorker (save_worker.py:76-79). They repeat the crop logic but nothing calls them; the only live application point is save_export_manager.py:412-417.  
**Suspected defect:** Off-by-one: clamping to width-1/height-1 combined with an exclusive end drops the final column and row. | Clamping to width-1/height-1 combined with exclusive x2/y2 means no crop can ever keep the last pixel column and row, even a full-image crop. | Inclusive clamping combined with exclusive slicing makes it impossible to keep the image's last row/column once any crop is active.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** Both open questions were decided on 2026-09-17. (1) SCOPE: a crop applies to the current image only and must NOT carry over to another image of the same size. Legacy does keep a size-keyed memory (crop_manager.py:144-145) and a restore block (file_navigation_manager.py:227-238), but I confirmed that block sits inside load_selected_image, whose only caller is main_window._load_selected_image (main_window.py:947, 1461-1463) bound to right_panel.image_selected, emitted only by file_tree.doubleClicked on a QTreeView that is constructed hidden 'for backward compatibility' (right_panel.py:117-122, 206); the shipping loader is load_image_by_path (file_navigation_manager.py:254-279), which calls _reset_state and nulls the crop (main_window.py:2148-2156) and never restores, and crop_manager.get_crop_for_image_size (crop_manager.py:408-413) has no callers at all - so neither the dictionary nor the restore is ported. (2) GEOMETRY: the inclusive clamp (crop_manager.py:131-134: x2 \<= width-1, y2 \<= height-1) combined with exclusive slice ends (file_manager.py:724-737) is preserved verbatim, including the unconditionally cleared last row and column, because exports must stay byte-identical. What was wrong in the card: the 'then' sentence 'so column 999 and row 700 are always cleared' conflates two different facts - in this example column 999 happens to be BOTH the crop's excluded end column and the image's last column, whereas row 700 is only the crop's excluded end row (the image's last row is 799, also cleared, by the bottom branch at file_manager.py:728-729); the always-true generalization is that the column at index x2 and the row at index y2 are excluded, and therefore the image's final column (width-1) and final row (height-1) can never survive an active crop (crop_manager.py:131-134 + file_manager.py:728, 736). The plainEnglish/parameters claim that 'crop corners are rounded' is misleading: round() at crop_manager.py:127 only ever sees integers, so the real conversions are int() truncation toward zero on the drag path (single_view_mouse_handler.py:554-555) and integer parsing on the text path (border_crop_widget.py:119, 126) - an implementer who writes Math.round will diverge on any sub-pixel drag edge, and Python's round() is half-to-even anyway. The 'parameters' string also duplicated 'x2/y2 exclusive in the mask' and buried the dead-loader nuance in a way that reads like a portable requirement; the \>5x5 gate needed to be stated as strictly greater on BOTH axes, measured on the float rectangle in image pixels before truncation and clamping (single_view_mouse_handler.py:553); and 'and' was null though the full-image coordinate frame (yolo_detection.py:20, 29-32; exporters/__init__.py:107 never read), the copy-not-mutate semantics (file_manager.py:720) and the per-object intersection (segment_manager.py:297) all belong in the contract. The edge-case list carried three duplicates ('Multi-view saves ignore the crop' / 'Multi-view saves never crop', and 'Every crop drops its own last column and row' restating the full-image case) and was missing the reachable failure modes. What an implementer must watch for: the degenerate crop - once clamping collapses x1 == x2 or y1 == y2 the kept region is empty and the whole exported mask is zeroed, which a drag from x=99.4 to x=110 on a 100-px-wide image reaches despite the \>5 gate; the silent no-op when a drawn rectangle is \<= 5 px (crop mode is retained and the previous crop survives); the fact that a crop which empties the mask still rewrites the NPZ as zeros while leaving other-format label files on disk (npz.py:15-27, exporters/__init__.py:189-206); sequence 'Save All' applying the one on-screen crop to every propagated frame (main_window.py:4810-4813); and that multi-view must simply have no crop, since the legacy multi-view paths call methods that do not exist (crop_manager.py:88, 107, 426). **Still open for the owner:** Nothing blocks implementation. Two product decisions remain outside this card: (a) whether the inclusive-clamp/exclusive-slice off-by-one should be fixed in some later release that is allowed to break byte-identical exports, and whether datasets already exported with a crop would then be regenerated; (b) whether multi-view should ever gain a working crop, since the legacy multi-view apply/clear/reset paths call methods that do not exist and raise AttributeError.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should a crop carry over to other images of the same size, as the unreachable loader does, or reset for each image? | Is crop end (x2, y2) meant to be inclusive or exclusive, and should a full-frame crop keep the entire image? Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-019: Merge selected segments into the lowest selected class
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:65-85; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:236-239; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:53-57`  
**Plain English:** The Merge action (M) moves all selected segments to the lowest class id among them, or to a new class id when none has a class; masks stay separate.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Selected segments with classes 5, 2 and 9 while class 7 is active  
  When  The user presses M  
  Then  All three segments become class 2; the active class 7 is ignored  
**Parameters:** target = min(selected class ids) else next_class_id  
**Edge cases handled:** Selecting a single segment leaves it unchanged; If no selected segment has a class, the next new class ID is used; Indices are only upper-bound checked; Not undoable  
**Suspected defect:** Docstrings say merge assigns 'to the active class', but the executable code ignores the active class. | Handler docstrings say 'assign selected segments to active class' but the active class is never used.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** NO - Merge must NOT use the active class. Keep the executable behaviour, target = min(selected class ids), or next_class_id when none of the selected segments has a class; the docstrings that say 'assign selected segments to active class' are the defect and should not be ported. THE REASON IS WHICH STATE A USER IS LOOKING AT. Merge acts on segments the user has just selected and can see highlighted. The active class is a mode set somewhere else, possibly minutes ago, and possibly not on screen at all. Reading it here makes the same visible action produce different results depending on invisible state, which is the property that makes a destructive operation feel random. min(selected) is derivable from exactly what the user selected. IT IS ALSO THE LESS DESTRUCTIVE OF THE TWO. Merging classes 2 and 3 with class 7 active would move both to 7 - a class that took no part in the selection - so the operation would introduce a classification the user never asked for while removing two they had. min(selected) can only collapse toward a class that was already in the selection, so no new class appears and at least one segment keeps the class it had. THE ACTIVE CLASS ALREADY HAS ITS JOB. It governs CREATION: a new polygon, box, circle or accepted AI mask takes it. Merge is a reclassification of existing annotations, and reclassification already has a deliberate, explicit action in RULE-013 (reassign class ids from the class table order). Wiring the active class into merge as well would give two different controls for the same intent, one of them implicit. PRIORITY: not P0, and the panel was right. Nothing is deleted and no mask changes - masks stay separate, which the card states and the code confirms; only the class id moves, and it is recoverable both by the undo stack (RULE-052) and by an explicit reassign. It stays P1 because getting it wrong silently mislabels every merged object, which survives into every export. WHAT THE PORT MUST DO BESIDES: the docstring divergence is itself worth recording rather than quietly fixing, because someone reading legacy to check the port will hit it. The TypeScript implementation should name the rule in a comment - target is the lowest SELECTED class, not the active one - so the next reader does not resolve the contradiction the other way.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should Merge assign to the active class when one is set? Panel: The compliance and fidelity judges rated this not P0. Decide whether it guards annotation data integrity and belongs in the behavior contract. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-020: SAM best-mask selection and click coordinate mapping
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam_model.py:217-255; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:371-411; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/coordinate_transformer.py:33-143; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/workers/sam_update_worker.py:42-43; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/ai_segment_manager.py:487-515`  
**Plain English:** SAM returns three candidate masks and the highest-scoring one is used; click positions are truncated to whole pixels, float masks are binarized above 0.5, and a preview needs at least one positive point.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A positive click at (120.8, 45.6) and SAM candidate scores [0.71, 0.93, 0.88]  
  When  The preview is computed  
  Then  The point is sent as (120, 45) with label 1 and the second candidate (0.93) is shown; with only negative points no preview is made  
**Parameters:** multimask_output True; winner = argmax score; scale factor fixed at 1.0; binarize \> 0.5; labels 1 positive, 0 negative  
**Edge cases handled:** With Operate On View off, coordinates are scaled by original/display size (1.0 in practice); Masks are resized with nearest neighbor if the scale factor is not 1.0  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-021: Auto-convert AI masks to polygons
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1788-1829; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:907-954; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/control_panel.py:431-436; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:29-31; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/control_panel.py:892-939; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1073-1078`  
**Plain English:** With Auto-Convert on, an accepted AI mask is replaced by a polygon traced from its largest outer contour and simplified by tolerance factor x perimeter; if fewer than 3 vertices remain, the mask is kept as is.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Auto-Convert ON; resolution slider 80; accepted mask has blobs of 4,000 px and 900 px  
  When  The user accepts the mask  
  Then  One Polygon segment outlining the 4,000-px blob; the 900-px blob is dropped  
**Parameters:** epsilon factor = 0.005 x 0.02^(slider/100); slider 1-100, default 80 gives about 0.000219; slider 41 gives about 0.001; slider 100 gives 0.0001; Auto-Convert default OFF; factor 0.00481 at 1, 0.000707 at 50, 0.000219 at 80, 0.0001 at 100 (0.001 at about 41)  
**Edge cases handled:** Holes are lost; Converted segment becomes vertex-editable; Holes are lost because only the external contour is used  
**Suspected defect:** Comment says default slider 80 equals epsilon 0.001 (control_panel.py:434-436); the formula yields about 0.000219. Secondary blobs are silently discarded. | Code comments claim slider 80 gives epsilon 0.001, but the executable mapping gives 0.000219; disconnected parts of the AI mask are silently dropped.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-022: Find Archetypes suggests about 2% of frames (between 5 and 50) as references
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:5039-5139; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/workers/reference_finder_worker.py:199-289; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/modes/sequence_view_mode.py:486-509; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/workers/reference_finder_worker.py:122-136`  
**Plain English:** The app embeds every frame, clusters them, and picks max(5, min(50, 2% of frames)) representative frames, split across clusters by size with at least one per cluster. Only pending frames change status to suggested.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 1,000-frame built timeline; clustering finds clusters of 600, 300 and 50 frames, plus 50 noise frames  
  When  The user clicks Find Archetypes (Ctrl+H)  
  Then  Budget = 20 and minimum cluster size = 10. The initial split 12/6/1 is topped up to 13/6/1, giving the extra slot to the cluster with the most spare frames. The 13, 6 and 1 frames closest to each cluster's center become 'suggested' (purple) if they were pending, and are queued first for AI embedding when a model is loaded. The user sees 'Found 20 suggested reference frames'.  
**Parameters:** budget = max(5, min(50, int(N x 0.02))); min_cluster_size = max(5, N // 100); at least 5 frames required; batch size 32 on CPU, 128 on GPU; MobileNetV3-small embeddings, L2-normalized; Minimum 5 frames; budget = max(5, min(50, int(0.02 n))); resize 256, center crop 224, ImageNet mean/std; L2-normalized 576-d features; min frames 5; expected = max(5, min(50, int(frames x 0.02)))  
**Edge cases handled:** Fewer than 5 frames: 'Need at least 5 frames to find archetypes'.; No timeline built: 'Build a timeline first'.; Clicking again while running aborts.; Earlier suggestions are cleared first.; All frames are noise: 'No diverse reference frames found'.; More clusters than the budget: each still gets 1, so suggestions can exceed the budget.; Frames already reference, saved or propagated keep their status but still count as suggestions for navigation.; More clusters than budget still gives each cluster 1, exceeding the budget; Noise frames are never suggested; No clusters means no suggestions; Clicking again while running aborts the analysis  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-023: Propagation seeds come only from mask segments on reference frames
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:3640-3668; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4257-4298; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:426-431; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/workers/propagation_worker.py:254-287; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4475-4481`  
**Plain English:** Every non-empty mask segment on every reference frame becomes one tracked object (IDs 1..N); the open frame contributes its unsaved in-memory segments, other reference frames load from their annotation files.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Reference frame 0 is open with 1 AI mask and 1 drawn polygon (unsaved); reference frame 40 has a saved NPZ with 2 classes  
  When  Propagation starts  
  Then  3 objects are tracked: the AI mask from frame 0 and 2 class masks from frame 40; the polygon is ignored  
**Parameters:** Object ids assigned sequentially from 1 in reference-frame order  
**Edge cases handled:** Polygons, boxes and circles on the open frame have no mask and are silently excluded until saved and reloaded; Class names for all objects come from the open frame's aliases; No usable masks: 'No valid segments in reference frames'; The same polygon would be included after saving and reloading from NPZ, YOLO Seg or COCO, which carry masks; If no reference yields a non-empty mask: 'No valid segments in reference frames' and the run ends.; Aliases stored in a reference frame's own file are ignored when naming objects.  
**Suspected defect:** Shape annotations on the open reference frame are silently left out of propagation. | Polygons, boxes, circles and auto-converted AI polygons (mask None) on the open reference frame are silently excluded from propagation.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** YES - rasterize drawn polygons, boxes and circles and use them as propagation seeds. Legacy excludes them silently, and that is the defect, not the design. THE USER'S ACT ALREADY SAID WHAT THEY MEANT. Marking a frame as a reference means 'propagate from what is on this frame'. Drawing a polygon on it means 'this is an object'. Legacy honours the first and ignores the second, so a user who carefully outlines four objects by hand on a reference frame and starts propagation gets nothing from them and is never told - the frames come back tracked for the mask segments only, which looks like the model failing to find objects rather than the objects never having been offered. That is the silent-loss family decision 7 exists to remove: the work is not destroyed, but its entire effect is, invisibly. THERE IS NO TECHNICAL REASON FOR THE GAP. The distinction legacy draws is between a segment carrying a mask and one carrying vertices, which is an internal representation difference, not a difference in what the user drew. The format library already rasterizes shapes on the way to export (RULE-015), so the capability exists and is proven byte-identical; and SAM 2's add_new_mask takes a mask, which a rasterized polygon is. The seed path can call the same rasterizer the exporter calls, which also guarantees the thing that propagates is the thing that would have been saved. RASTERIZE THROUGH THE EXPORT PATH, NOT A SECOND ONE. If seeding rasterized differently from exporting - a half-pixel offset, a different fill rule - a propagated sequence would disagree with the reference frame it came from, and the difference would appear only on the frames the user did not check. RULE-015's cv2.fillPoly semantics (edge rows and columns included) are the contract for both. WHAT MUST STILL BE REPORTED. Object ids stay sequential from 1 in reference-frame order, as the card says, with rasterized shapes taking their place in that order rather than being appended. Any segment that rasterizes to zero pixels - a degenerate polygon, a circle of radius 0 - is skipped AND named in the result, because 'we could not seed from this one' is exactly the sentence legacy never says. PRIORITY: not P0, and the panel was right. No annotation is deleted or altered; the shapes remain on the frame and export normally. It is P1 because the failure is invisible and the user's natural conclusion is that propagation does not work.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should drawn polygons, boxes and circles be rasterized and used as propagation seeds? | The compliance and fidelity judges rated this not P0. Decide whether it guards annotation data integrity and belongs in the behavior contract. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-024: Grayscale detection tolerance and 16-bit display conversion
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/image_adjustment_manager.py:451-480; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/image_adjustment_manager.py:546-567`  
**Plain English:** A 3-channel image is treated as grayscale when adjacent channels never differ by more than 3 (8-bit) or 768 (16-bit); 16-bit results are shown and sent to SAM as value / 256, truncated.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A JPEG whose R, G and B values differ by at most 2 per pixel  
  When  The image is loaded  
  Then  It is processed as one channel (Gray slider, rescale and FFT enabled); a 16-bit pixel of 1000 displays as 3  
**Parameters:** Tolerance 3 (8-bit), 768 (16-bit)  
**Edge cases handled:** A nearly gray image with a maximum difference of 4 is treated as RGB  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-025: Propagation direction and range
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:577-686; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:629-647; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4350-4378; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:497-529; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:667-679; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:851-874`  
**Plain English:** Propagate always runs both ways: forward from the lowest reference frame to the range end, then backward from the lowest reference frame to the range start; the range defaults to the whole timeline.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 100 frames; references at 20 and 60; range 1-100  
  When  The user clicks Propagate  
  Then  Forward pass frames 21-99 (60 skipped as a reference), then backward pass 19-0  
**Parameters:** UI range is 1-indexed; full range treated as unbounded; streaming used only if enabled (default on) and frames exceed the window (default 250, allowed 50-1000); UI range is 1-based, converted to 0-based; Direction is always 'both'. Range spinboxes are 1-based and reset to 1..N whenever the frame count changes. A start of 0 or an end of N-1 means no limit.  
**Edge cases handled:** Start greater than end is not validated; forward pass is empty if end is below the lowest reference; A start above the lowest reference is ignored by the forward pass; Range ends on size-skipped frames snap to the nearest valid frame (start up, end down); The forward pass ignores the requested start and always begins at the earliest reference; No backward pass from later references is ever run; A range bound that lands on a skipped frame snaps to the nearest usable frame (start moves up, end moves down).; Reference after the range end (reference 90, range 11-50): the backward pass covers frames 89 down to 10.  
**Suspected defect:** The user's range is not honored on the near side of the earliest reference.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-026: Streaming (chunked) propagation windows
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:69-76; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:594-598; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:876-1085; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4203-4210; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:356-381; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:610-627`  
**Plain English:** When Streaming is on and there are more valid frames than the window size, frames are processed in windows overlapping by 5; each window re-loads all reference images, and frames already completed by an earlier window are skipped.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 600 valid frames, a reference at frame 0, window 250  
  When  Propagation runs forward  
  Then  Windows cover frames 0-249, 245-494 and 490-599; overlap frames keep the earlier window's results  
  And   Turning Streaming off with more frames than the window warns with an estimate of frames x 12.6 MB (1000 frames about 12 GB)  
**Parameters:** Window 50-1000 step 50, default 250; overlap 5 frames; memory estimate 12.6 MB per frame; streaming default on; chunk_size default 250 (UI 50-1000, step 50, saved as stream_window_size)  
**Edge cases handled:** Flagged frames in an overlap are re-processed by the next window; Masks do not carry between windows; only reference masks seed each window; Exactly 250 usable frames uses the all-at-once mode, since the rule is strictly greater than.; The overlap skip only knows about frames recorded as propagated, so frames that were only flagged in an earlier chunk are processed again.  
**Measured (2026-09-26):** `inference/tests/goldens/propagation/synthetic-shapes-streaming` is legacy's streaming mode on 34 frames, the reference on frame 12, window 10. It walked 12-21, 17-26, 22-31 and 27-33 forward and 3-12 and 0-7 backward, as this card says. Two things the card does not say. First, a backward window whose reference is outside it answers nothing: the reference is staged first, and SAM 2 does not walk backward from index 0, so frames 0-2 stayed pending (SEQUENCE_PARITY SP-09). Second, "overlap frames keep the earlier window's results" holds only where the engine stored an object: frame 25, only flagged by window 2 with Keep Flagged Masks off, was answered again by window 3 and kept that window's mask, a distractor, while still flagged (SP-36). The web propagates the first and keeps the earlier answer in the second, pending the owner's ruling. Each window seeds only from the reference, so a window far from it can lose an object and track a look-alike: window 3 tracked the purple distractor rectangle as the square on frames 22-31, and both apps' Save All write it on frames 27-31.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-027: The fragment filter keeps only regions at least X% of the largest region
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:285-344; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1320-1324; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/fragment_threshold_widget.py:46-51; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:160-166`  
**Plain English:** Before an AI mask is accepted, each separate outer region is kept only if its contour area is at least threshold% of the largest region's area. A threshold of 0 turns the filter off, and Z toggles between 0 and the last non-zero value.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Fragment threshold 30; an AI mask with regions of contour area 2,000, 700 and 500 square px  
  When  The user accepts the preview  
  Then  Minimum area = 30% x 2,000 = 600. The 2,000 and 700 regions are kept, the 500 region is dropped, and the new mask is the filled outlines of the kept regions.  
**Parameters:** Range 0-100, default 0, saved in settings. Z toggle memory starts at 100 when the saved value is 0. Area is the contour polygon area, not a pixel count.; Range 0-100, default 0, toggle default 100; keep if area \>= threshold; area = contour polygon area  
**Edge cases handled:** Threshold 100 keeps only the largest region and any ties.; One-pixel-wide regions have contour area 0; if the largest region has area 0, everything is dropped.; Holes inside kept regions are filled whenever the threshold is above 0.; One-pixel-wide masks have contour area 0 and are always rejected when filtering is on  
**Suspected defect:** Any threshold above 0 fills interior holes, because outer contours are drawn filled, so mask shape changes beyond fragment removal. | With filter \> 0 the kept pieces are redrawn as filled outer contours, so holes inside the AI mask are filled; with filter 0 holes remain. Contour area undercounts small pieces relative to pixel counts.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-028: Display image adjustments (saturation, brightness/contrast, gamma)
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/photo_viewer.py:95-152; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/image_adjustment_manager.py:88-107; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/adjustments_widget.py:65-97; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1095-1097`  
**Plain English:** Displayed pixels are adjusted in order: saturation blend with BT.601 gray, then |p x (1 + contrast/100) + brightness| saturated to 0-255, then gamma LUT p' = trunc(255 x (p/255)^(1/gamma)).  
**Specification:**  
  Given Pixel values [0, 30, 100, 200, 255]  
  When  Contrast 50 and brightness 20 are applied  
  Then  Result [20, 65, 170, 255, 255]; with gamma 2.0 a pixel of 64 becomes 127  
  And   Saturation s gives gray + s x (orig - gray) with gray = 0.299R + 0.587G + 0.114B; alpha is preserved  
**Parameters:** Brightness -100..100 default 0; contrast -100..100 default 0; gamma slider 1-200 -\> 0.01-2.00 default 1.0; saturation slider 0-200 -\> 0.0-2.0 default 1.0  
**Edge cases handled:** Gamma values 0.29, 0.57, 0.58, 1.13-1.16 drift down by 0.01 on restart because int(gamma*100) truncates; Persisted saturation is applied at startup but the slider is never restored and shows 1.00  
**Suspected defect:** cv2.convertScaleAbs takes the absolute value, so negative brightness folds instead of clipping: brightness -100 turns [0, 30, 100, 200, 255] into [100, 70, 0, 100, 155].  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-029: Channel threshold posterization
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/channel_threshold_widget.py:503-568; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/channel_threshold_widget.py:241-258; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/channel_threshold_widget.py:422-453`  
**Plain English:** N threshold markers on a channel split values into N+1 bands: below the first marker becomes 0, at or above the last becomes the maximum, and middle band i becomes int(i/N x maximum).  
**Specification:**  
  Given An 8-bit grayscale image with the Gray channel enabled and markers [50, 150]  
  When  Thresholding is applied  
  Then  Values [10, 49, 50, 100, 149, 150, 255] become [0, 0, 127, 127, 127, 255, 255]  
  And   RGB images threshold only enabled channels; 16-bit images use maximum 65535; unchecking a channel clears its markers  
**Parameters:** Minimum marker spacing 10 units; slider max 256 (8-bit) or 65536 (16-bit); lower bound inclusive, upper exclusive  
**Edge cases handled:** The 10-unit spacing is negligible for 16-bit images; Applied only inside the crop when a crop is active  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-030: FFT frequency-band thresholding for grayscale images
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/fft_threshold_widget.py:373-496; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/fft_threshold_widget.py:283-330`  
**Plain English:** The image spectrum is split into radial bands by cutoff percentages of the half-diagonal; band k of N+1 is weighted k/N (lowest band removed, highest kept), inverted, stretched to 0-255, then optionally quantized.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A grayscale image with one frequency cutoff at 10% (slider 1000) and one intensity threshold at 100  
  When  FFT thresholding is enabled  
  Then  Frequencies within 10% of the half-diagonal are zeroed (high-pass), the result is min-max stretched to 0-255, then pixels \<= 100 become 0 and \> 100 become 255  
**Parameters:** Frequency slider 0-10000 (0.01% steps); distance normalized by sqrt((H/2)^2 + (W/2)^2); quantized level = level/N x 255 truncated  
**Edge cases handled:** Only 2-D or exactly equal-channel images are processed; Output is always 8-bit, even for 16-bit input; No frequency cutoffs gives a plain contrast stretch  
**Suspected defect:** The cached spectrum is keyed only by image dimensions and is not invalidated when upstream rescale or channel-threshold settings change, so FFT output can come from a stale input.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-031: Histogram presets: contrast stretch, equalization, CLAHE
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/rescale_histogram_dialog.py:28-73; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/rescale_histogram_dialog.py:512-564; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2809-2825`  
**Plain English:** Contrast stretch sets min/max at the chosen tail percentiles; equalization builds a CDF lookup table; CLAHE applies adaptive equalization with a clip limit and tile grid.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An 8-bit grayscale image and the default 0.4% saturation  
  When  The stretch preset is applied  
  Then  min = floor(0.4th percentile) and max = ceil(99.6th percentile), clamped to the data range; 0% uses the actual data min/max  
  And   Equalization LUT = (cdf - cdf_min)/max(1, N - cdf_min) x max, clipped and truncated  
**Parameters:** Stretch 0-50% in 0.1% steps, default 0.4%; CLAHE clip 2.0 (0.5-40), tiles 8x8 (2-32)  
**Edge cases handled:** The CLAHE result is computed on the crop region and discarded when the crop changes; Dragging the rescale handles clears any preset  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-032: Linear min/max rescale for grayscale images
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/rescale_widget.py:330-393; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/image_adjustment_manager.py:388-407`  
**Plain English:** Grayscale values are clipped to [min, max] and stretched to the full output range, truncating fractions.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An 8-bit grayscale image with min 50 and max 200  
  When  Rescale is applied  
  Then  Values [30, 50, 125, 200, 250] become [0, 0, 127, 255, 255] via (clip(v,50,200) - 50)/150 x 255  
  And   Processing order is rescale, then channel threshold, then FFT, then 16-bit to 8-bit, then display adjustments  
**Parameters:** Output max 255 (8-bit) or 65535 (16-bit); inactive when handles are at full range
