**Edge cases handled:** RGB images: rescale disabled; max \<= min leaves the image unchanged; Restricted to the crop region when a crop is active  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-033: Annotation marker size, pan step and zoom
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P2  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:515-543; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1308-1314; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/annotation_settings_widget.py:69-87; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/viewport_manager.py:22-80; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/photo_viewer.py:180-185`  
**Plain English:** Point radius = 0.3 x size multiplier and line width = 0.5 x multiplier; Ctrl+Plus/Minus change the multiplier by 0.1 (not image zoom); WASD pans 10% of the view x pan multiplier; the mouse wheel zooms 1.25x in and 0.8x out.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Size slider 20 (multiplier 2.0), pan slider 15 (1.5) and an 800 px tall view  
  When  The user presses W  
  Then  Points draw with radius 0.6 and lines 1.0 wide; the view scrolls up 120 px (800 x 0.1 x 1.5)  
**Parameters:** Size slider 1-50 (0.1-5.0), pan slider 1-100 (0.1-10.0), defaults 1.0  
**Edge cases handled:** Ctrl+Plus is clamped at multiplier 5.0  
**Suspected defect:** The pan tooltip and startup tip promise 'Hold Shift for 5x boost' (annotation_settings_widget.py:84, utils/startup.py:48) but no code applies a boost.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-034: Class display color
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P2  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_display_manager.py:219-245`  
**Plain English:** Each class color is HSV with hue = int(class_id x 222.4922359) mod 360, saturation 220 and value 220; segments without a class are gray.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Class ids 0, 1, 2, 3  
  When  Segments are drawn  
  Then  Hues are 0, 222, 84 and 307  
**Parameters:** Hue step 222.4922359 degrees; S=220; V=220; no-class color HSV(0,0,128)  
**Edge cases handled:** None recorded  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-035: Confidence histogram binning
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P2  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/confidence_histogram_dialog.py:26-76; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/confidence_histogram_dialog.py:214-219; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4725-4739`  
**Plain English:** The histogram shows 50 bins from max(0, min(threshold, lowest score) - 0.02) to 1.0, counts scores strictly below the threshold as 'Below', and accepting the dialog sets Min Conf.

  Given Scores [0.95, 0.97, 0.995, 1.0] and threshold 0.99  
  When  The histogram opens  
  Then  View range 0.93-1.0; Below 2 (50%), Above 2 (50%)  
**Parameters:** 50 bins; view padding 0.02  
**Edge cases handled:** None recorded

### RULE-036: File list annotation-status indicators
**Category:** Calculation  
**Priority:** P2  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/utils/fast_file_manager.py:186-235; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/utils/fast_file_manager.py:611-692; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/utils/fast_file_manager.py:138-153`  
**Plain English:** Each image row marks which annotation files exist by base-name match: .npz (not _CM), _CM.npz, .txt (not _seg), _seg.txt, _coco.json, _createml.json, .xml.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Files img.png, img_CM.npz, img_seg.txt  
  When  The folder is scanned  
  Then  img.png shows NPZ Class Map and YOLO Seg present; NPZ and YOLO Det absent  
**Parameters:** Suffixes: .npz (not ending _CM), _CM.npz (case-sensitive), .txt (not ending _seg), _seg.txt, _coco.json, _createml.json, .xml  
**Edge cases handled:** Batch status refresh never updates the NPZ Class Map column; Image foo_seg.png shows no YOLO Det although foo_seg.txt exists and is loaded for it; The batch refresh does not re-check the Class Map column (fast_file_manager.py:670-677).; Files created outside the app appear only after a re-scan or after that row is saved.

## Validation rules (15)

### RULE-037: COCO JSON import with polygon-then-box fallback
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:602-710`  
**Plain English:** Categories become aliases ('name.supercategory' when they differ); each polygon of 6+ numbers becomes its own mask segment with category_id as class; if no polygon yields pixels, the [x, y, w, h] box is used, clamped to the image.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 640x480 image; category {id 2, name 'dog', supercategory 'animal'}; annotation category_id 2 with RLE segmentation and bbox [630, 470, 20, 20]  
  When  img_coco.json is loaded  
  Then  Alias 2 = 'dog.animal'; RLE ignored; box clamped to x 630-639, y 470-479 giving one 10x10 segment of class 2  
**Parameters:** Minimum polygon length 6 numbers; coordinates rounded with Python round()  
**Edge cases handled:** Missing category_id becomes class 0; non-numeric category_id skips the annotation; image_id is ignored; every annotation is assumed to belong to this image; A multi-polygon annotation becomes several segments; Top level not an object: error logged, nothing loaded; Annotations whose polygon and bbox are both unusable are silently dropped; NPZ loading replaces aliases wholesale while COCO merges them  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-038: NPZ import (current and legacy layouts)
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:224-280; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:335-343`  
**Plain English:** Each non-empty channel of 'mask' (or legacy 'masks') becomes one segment whose class is class_order[channel] when stored, else the channel index; a legacy N x H x W 'masks' stack with 'class_ids' becomes one segment per mask; stored aliases replace current aliases.  
**Specification:**  
  Given img.npz with mask shape (480, 640, 3), class_order [0, 3, 7], channel 1 empty  
  When  img.npz is loaded  
  Then  Two segments: class 0 and class 7  
**Parameters:** Channel-to-class: class_order[i] if present and i \< len(class_order), else i  
**Edge cases handled:** Older files without class_order treat channel index as class ID; class_order shorter than the channel count: extra channels use their index; A 2-D mask is treated as a single channel; Mask size is not checked against the image; a mismatch surfaces later as a save failure; Legacy stack entry without a class_ids value becomes class 0; NPZ mask size is not checked against the image size; a mismatched file loads and later fails at save; An NPZ with neither 'mask' nor 'masks' loads nothing and still stops the load chain  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful; 1 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-039: Pascal VOC and CreateML import rules
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:456-540`  
**Plain English:** VOC objects need both a name and a bndbox (missing coordinates default to 0, xmax/ymax exclusive); CreateML uses only the first image entry and converts center/size pixels to a box; both then use name-to-class resolution and clamp/drop rules.  
**Specification:**  
  Given VOC object name 'dog', xmin 100, ymin 50, xmax 110, ymax 60  
  When  img.xml is loaded  
  Then  One 10x10 mask segment covering columns 100-109 and rows 50-59  
**Parameters:** None  
**Edge cases handled:** VOC files from tools using inclusive xmax lose 1 px in width and height; Empty name becomes label '0' (class 0); CreateML arrays with several images: only the first is used; missing label becomes '0'  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; both P0 judges (compliance and fidelity lenses) rated it P0 and the specification faithful Confidence was lowered only by the P0 panel disagreement that decision 14 settled on 2026-09-17; every judge found the specification faithful.

### RULE-040: YOLO Detection import validation, rounding and clamping
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:381-454`  
**Plain English:** Each line must have exactly 5 tokens with numeric coordinates; boxes are converted to pixels with rounding, clamped to the image, dropped if empty, and loaded as filled rectangular mask segments.  
**Specification:**  
  Given An image whose size is supplied as the tuple image_size = (height, width) = (480, 640) — i.e. width 640, height 480 (note the order: legacy passes (h, w), NOT (w, h); file_manager.py:391, :421). h and w are plain Python ints (file_manager.py:207-222). The segment list is empty and segment_manager.class_aliases is empty, because every normal image load calls _reset_state -\> SegmentManager.clear() first (main_window.py:2141; segment_manager.py:19-26; invoked from file_navigation_manager.py:186, :279). A sidecar named \<base\>.txt sits next to the image and contains exactly three lines: '3 0.5 0.5 0.25 0.5' '2 0.5 0.5 0.1' 'cat 1.1 0.5 0.2 0.2' No .npz, _seg.txt, _coco.json, _CM.npz, .xml or _createml.json exists for that image, because '.txt' is the LAST and lowest-fidelity entry of the load chain (file_manager.py:128-136).  
  When  load_existing_mask walks the chain, finds only \<base\>.txt, and calls load_bb_txt(path, (480, 640)) (file_manager.py:173-205, :201). load_bb_txt reads all lines, denormalizes them into integer pixel boxes (file_manager.py:412-451), then hands the whole list to _add_box_segments, which assigns class ids, clamps, drops and fills (file_manager.py:453, :381-410).  
  Then  Exactly one segment is produced. Line 1 '3 0.5 0.5 0.25 0.5' -\> x1 = round((0.5 - 0.25/2) * 640) = round(240.0) = 240; y1 = round((0.5 - 0.5/2) * 480) = round(120.0) = 120; x2 = round((0.5 + 0.125) * 640) = round(400.0) = 400; y2 = round((0.5 + 0.25) * 480) = round(360.0) = 360 (file_manager.py:446-449). Clamping changes nothing. mask[120:360, 240:400] = true (file_manager.py:400-401): rows 120..359 and columns 240..399, i.e. 240 rows x 160 columns = 38,400 true pixels inside a fresh 480x640 boolean array. The segment record is exactly {mask: \<480x640 bool\>, type: 'Loaded', vertices: null, class_id: 3} (file_manager.py:403-409). Line 2 '2 0.5 0.5 0.1' has 4 tokens, so it is skipped silently, with no error and no class id (file_manager.py:432-434). Line 3 'cat 1.1 0.5 0.2 0.2' -\> in IEEE-754 doubles 1.1 - 0.2/2 is exactly 1.0, so x1 = round(1.0 * 640) = 640; 1.1 + 0.2/2 is 1.2000000000000002, so x2 = round(768.0000000000001) = 768; y1 = round(0.4 * 480) = 192; y2 = round(0.6 * 480) = 288. Clamping gives x1 = max(0, 640) = 640 and x2 = min(640, 768) = 640 (file_manager.py:395-396), so x2 \<= x1 and the box is dropped (file_manager.py:397-398). The y extent was legal; the drop is caused by x alone. Surviving segments are appended in file order, one per surviving line, with no de-duplication and no merging of overlaps.  
  And   The dropped line still leaves a class behind, and this ordering is part of the contract. _build_label_map runs at file_manager.py:392 over the labels of ALL parsed boxes ('3' and 'cat') BEFORE any clamping or dropping at :394-398. Numeric labels claim their ids first ('3' -\> 3 via int() at :365-366), then non-numeric labels take the lowest ids not already taken (:370-377). So 'cat' is assigned class id 0 and segment_manager.class_aliases[0] = 'cat' is registered permanently (:377), even though its only box was discarded. That alias table is copied into every later save (save_worker.py:89; save_export_manager.py:424), so a port that filters boxes before assigning ids would produce identical pixels but a different class id for a later 'cat' box and a different alias table on disk. And the file is all-or-nothing on arithmetic failure: segments are only added at :453, after the whole parse loop, so an exception raised at :446-449 discards every already-parsed box in the file.  
**Parameters:** image_size = (height, width); x pairs with width, y with height (file_manager.py:391, :395-396). Line splitting: line.strip().split() — Python's whitespace split, which collapses runs of any whitespace (space, tab, \\r, \\n, \\x0b, \\x0c, and Unicode spaces such as U+00A0) and yields no empty tokens. A line must have EXACTLY 5 tokens, else skip (:432-434). Blank lines yield 0 tokens and are skipped. A 6th token (e.g. a YOLO prediction confidence) makes the line 6 tokens -\> skipped. Token order: label, cx, cy, bw, bh — all four coordinates normalized to [0,1] by convention but never range-checked. Coordinate parse: Python float() (:437-440). Its grammar is NOT JavaScript's Number(): it ACCEPTS 'nan', 'inf', 'infinity', '+inf' (all case-insensitive), '.5', '1.', and underscore separators between digits ('1_0.5' -\> 10.5, '1e1_0' -\> 1e10); it REJECTS '' , '0x10', '0b101', '0o17', '1,5', '5_', '_5', '1__0', '1e'. JS Number() gets '0x10' -\> 16, '' -\> 0, 'inf' -\> NaN, '1_0' -\> NaN, all wrong. Implement a Python-float-compatible parser, e.g. match /^[+-]?(?:inf(?:inity)?|nan|(?:\\d(?:_?\\d)*(?:\\.(?:\\d(?:_?\\d)*)?)?|\\.\\d(?:_?\\d)*)(?:[eE][+-]?\\d(?:_?\\d)*)?)$/i, strip '_', then Number(). Only a parse FAILURE skips the line; a successful parse to NaN/Infinity does not (see edge cases). Denormalize, in this exact association (file_manager.py:446-449): x1 = pyRound((cx - bw/2) * w);  y1 = pyRound((cy - bh/2) * h) x2 = pyRound((cx + bw/2) * w);  y2 = pyRound((cy + bh/2) * h) Do NOT algebraically refactor (e.g. to cx*w - bw*w/2): the halving and the add/subtract happen in normalized space BEFORE the multiply, and a different association gives a different double and occasionally a different pixel. Rounding: Python's built-in round() on a float — round-half-to-EVEN applied to the exact IEEE-754 double, returning an exact integer. Math.round (half-up, and half-up toward +Infinity for negatives) is WRONG. Reference port: function pyRound(x){ if(!Number.isFinite(x)) throw new RangeError('non-finite coordinate'); const f=Math.floor(x), d=x-f; if(d\>0.5) return f+1; if(d\<0.5) return f; return f%2===0 ? f : f+1; } Verified ties: 10.5-\>10, 11.5-\>12, 30.5-\>30, 240.5-\>240, 511.5-\>512, 512.5-\>512, 2.5-\>2, -0.5-\>0, -1.5-\>-2, -2.5-\>-2. Python returns an arbitrary-precision int, but since the value is immediately clamped into [0,w] / [0,h], a double is safe once non-finite inputs are rejected. Clamp, asymmetrically (file_manager.py:395-396): x1 = max(0, x1), y1 = max(0, y1), x2 = min(w, x2), y2 = min(h, y2). There is NO upper clamp on x1/y1 and NO lower clamp on x2/y2 — that asymmetry is what makes wholly-out-of-image boxes collapse and be dropped instead of surviving as a 1-px sliver at the edge. Drop predicate (:397-398): discard the box if x2 \<= x1 OR y2 \<= y1. Evaluated AFTER clamping. Fill (:400-401): a fresh (h, w) boolean array per surviving box, with mask[y1:y2, x1:x2] = true — x2 and y2 are EXCLUSIVE, x1 and y1 inclusive; width = x2-x1, height = y2-y1. Segment record (:403-409): {mask, type: 'Loaded', vertices: null, class_id}. type is the literal string 'Loaded'; vertices is null, so loaded boxes are masks, not editable rectangles. Class id resolution (_build_label_map, file_manager.py:345-379), per distinct label string, in two passes over the labels of all parsed boxes: pass 1, in first-appearance order: (a) if the label equals an existing alias NAME, reuse that alias's id (:362-363); else (b) if Python int() accepts it, the id IS that integer (:365-366); else (c) queue it as unnamed (:368). pass 2: taken = existing alias ids UNION ids claimed in pass 1; each unnamed label in queue order takes the lowest non-negative integer not in taken, and is registered as class_aliases[id] = label (:370-377). Python int() accepts an optional sign, ASCII or Unicode decimal digits, and '_' between digits; it is arbitrary precision; it REJECTS '3.0', '0x10', '' and any float-looking token.  
**Edge cases handled:** MOST IMPORTANT, and absent from the card: a coordinate token that float() accepts but that is not finite does NOT skip its line — it aborts the entire file. 'nan', 'inf', '-inf', 'Infinity', '1e309' all parse successfully inside the try at file_manager.py:437-440, but int(round(...)) at :446-449 sits OUTSIDE that try and raises ValueError ('cannot convert float NaN to integer') or OverflowError ('cannot convert float infinity to integer'). The same happens to a finite value whose product overflows, e.g. cx=1e306 with w=640 (1e300*640 = 6.4e302 is fine; 1e306*640 overflows). The exception propagates out of load_bb_txt before _add_box_segments at :453 ever runs, so ZERO segments load from the file — every valid line before and after the bad one is thrown away. load_existing_mask catches it at :202-204, logs, and continues the chain; since '.txt' is the last entry (:135) the chain then ends with nothing loaded.; A read or decode failure is different and cleaner: open(txt_path) at :424 is called with NO encoding, so OSError/UnicodeDecodeError is caught at :426-428, logged, and load_bb_txt RETURNS normally with zero segments (no exception, chain ends at :205). Both paths end in 'nothing loaded', but only the non-finite path raises.; Encoding is platform-dependent: open() at :424 uses locale.getpreferredencoding(False), which is the ANSI code page on Windows (cp1252 on this machine) and usually UTF-8 on Linux/macOS. A UTF-8 file with a BOM therefore decodes on Windows with 'i\>?' (U+00EF U+00BB U+00BF) glued to the first token, so '0' becomes a token int() rejects, and that box silently becomes a NEW named class with a garbage alias instead of class 0.; The class token is never validated as numeric. 'dog 0.5 0.5 0.2 0.2' is fully accepted (:436 unpacks it, :438 parses only the 4 coordinates). '3' resolves to class 3 ONLY if no existing alias is literally named '3' — an alias name match wins over the integer interpretation (:362-366). '3.0' fails int() and gets the lowest free id plus a new alias named '3.0'. '-1' is accepted by int() and becomes class_id -1. A 30-digit token becomes a 30-digit class id in Python; a TS port using Number would silently lose precision there.; Class ids and aliases are assigned BEFORE clamping and dropping (:392 runs before :394-398), so a box that is discarded still consumes an id and, if its label is non-numeric, permanently registers class_aliases[id] = label (:377), which is then written into every save (save_worker.py:89, save_export_manager.py:424). In the worked example class_aliases[0] = 'cat' survives although no 'cat' pixel is loaded.; The drop at :397-398 is broader than 'collapses to zero'. It also removes: boxes with negative normalized width or height (bw = -0.2 makes x1 \> x2); boxes entirely outside the image on any side (x1 = -100, x2 = -50 clamps to x1 = 0, x2 = -50, so x2 \<= x1); and boxes exactly touching an edge from outside, as line 3 of the worked example shows.; Rounding mode is behaviour-visible, not merely a 1-pixel cosmetic difference. On a 1024x1024 image, '0 0.5 0.5 0.0009765625 0.5' gives edges 511.5 and 512.5, so Python yields x1 = 512 and x2 = 512 and the box is DROPPED, while Math.round yields 512 and 513 and the box SURVIVES with width 1. A half-up port therefore produces a segment that legacy does not. A pure 1-pixel tie: on a 512x512 image '0 0.0400390625 0.5 0.0390625 0.5' gives x1 = round(10.5) = 10 and x2 = round(30.5) = 30, i.e. columns 10..29; half-up gives columns 11..30.; Prediction files that carry a 6th confidence token are skipped line by line, silently and with no class id assigned (:432-434); pinned by tests/unit/core/test_file_manager.py:294-312.; Loaded boxes are masks, not editable rectangles: type is 'Loaded' and vertices is null (:406-407). Every surviving box allocates its own full (h, w) boolean array (:400), so N boxes cost N*h*w booleans.; Duplicate and overlapping lines are all kept as separate segments; overlap pixels are true in both masks and nothing is merged or de-duplicated (tests/unit/core/test_file_manager.py:274-288).; CORRECTION to the card: a same-named non-annotation .txt (a caption file, say) does not 'stop the load chain' — '.txt' is already the LAST entry of _LOAD_CHAIN (:135), so nothing follows it. The real consequence is elsewhere: find_annotation_file (:138-151) reports the image as annotated purely because the .txt exists, so the UI treats it as already labelled (main_window.py:3966, :4226, :4858) while zero segments actually load.; Consequence that makes the failure modes destructive rather than cosmetic: auto-save on navigation defaults to on (file_navigation_manager.py:156-160), and save_single_view_output deletes every annotation sidecar for the image, the source .txt included, whenever the segment list is empty (save_export_manager.py:106-109 -\> exporters/__init__.py:208-215). So a whole-file abort, or a file whose lines were all skipped, can erase the user's labels on the next image change. Lines that were individually skipped are likewise lost on any re-save, because the exporter rewrites the same base+'.txt' from the loaded segments only (exporters/yolo_detection.py:44-46).  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** The open question is whether this card should keep its P0 'copy Python's half-to-even rounding exactly' contract, and the approved decisions settle it: yes. 'Imported annotations must land on exactly the same pixels as legacy' makes the rounding mode, the exclusive x2/y2 bounds and the asymmetric clamp contractual, so the compliance judge's advice to demote to P1 and treat the tie rounding as a non-blocking dual-run check is overruled — and it was based on a false premise anyway, since a tie is not always a 1-pixel shift: on a 1024-wide image a half-up port keeps a box that legacy drops entirely (file_manager.py:446-449 with :397-398). 'Keep legacy per-image class ids and aliases' makes the pre-clamp ordering contractual too, so _build_label_map running over every parsed box at file_manager.py:392 before the clamp and drop at :394-398 must be reproduced, aliases for dropped boxes included (:377, copied into saves at save_worker.py:89 and save_export_manager.py:424). 'A failed or partial load must never present as no annotations' is the one place where the port must diverge from legacy's UX while matching its data: legacy raises out of :446-449, load_existing_mask swallows it at :202-204, and the user sees an empty canvas that auto-save then turns into deletion of the source .txt (file_navigation_manager.py:156-160 -\> save_export_manager.py:106-109 -\> exporters/__init__.py:208-215), so the port must load the same zero segments but surface a distinguishable load-failure state and must report skipped lines rather than silently presenting a clean empty image. What was wrong in the card: (1) it never mentions the whole-file abort at all — its 'lines without numeric coordinates are skipped' wording is only true for tokens float() rejects, since 'nan', 'inf' and '1e309' pass the try at :437-440 and then raise outside it at :446-449, discarding every valid box in the file; (2) 'Parameters' states the rounding mode twice and still omits the load-bearing details — that image_size is (height, width) (:391), that the halving happens before the multiply (:446-449), that the clamp is asymmetric with no upper bound on x1/y1 and no lower bound on x2/y2 (:395-396), and that Python's float()/int() grammars differ from JavaScript's Number()/parseInt(); (3) the edge case 'still stops the load chain' is wrong because '.txt' is already last in _LOAD_CHAIN (:135) — the real effect is that find_annotation_file (:138-151) marks the image as labelled at main_window.py:3966, :4226 and :4858; (4) 'negative coordinates are clamped to 0' is only half the rule, since the drop test at :397-398 also removes flipped boxes and boxes wholly off-image; (5) the class token's lack of validation and the alias-name-beats-integer resolution order (:362-366) were missing, so '3' is class 3 only when no alias is named '3'. What an implementer must watch for: write a Python-compatible float parser and a half-to-even pyRound before anything else, keep the arithmetic association byte-for-byte as at :446-449, assign class ids before clamping, and make the non-finite case throw and be surfaced instead of quietly yielding an empty segment list. **Still open for the owner:** One genuine product decision remains: the legacy text encoding is machine-dependent, because open() at file_manager.py:424 takes the platform locale encoding (cp1252 on the Windows machine this was verified on, UTF-8 on most Linux/macOS installs). 'Exactly the same pixels as legacy' is therefore undefined for any file that is not pure ASCII — the same UTF-8 file with a BOM loads class 0 on Linux and an alias-named garbage class on Windows. The port must pick one: read as UTF-8 and strip a leading BOM (recommended; matches Linux legacy and fixes the BOM class, but diverges from a Windows legacy install for non-ASCII label names), or replicate cp1252 for byte-fidelity with Windows. A second, smaller choice: Python's int() gives arbitrary-precision class ids, so a label like '99999999999999999999' becomes an exact 20-digit class id that JavaScript numbers cannot represent — the owner should confirm whether to use BigInt, cap the id, or treat oversized integer labels as non-numeric and give them a fresh id.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-041: YOLO Segmentation import validation
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:542-600`  
**Plain English:** A line needs a label plus at least 3 coordinate pairs (7 or more tokens, odd count, all numeric); points are scaled to pixels with rounding, filled into a mask, and dropped if no pixel is set.  
**Specification:**  
  Given A 640x480 image, so image_size is passed as (h, w) = (480, 640) -- height first (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:551). The SegmentManager is empty: segments [], class_aliases {}, next_class_id 0, active_class_id None (it is cleared before every image load, ui/managers/file_navigation_manager.py:281). On disk, \<base\>.npz is absent and \<base\>_seg.txt exists, so _seg.txt is the winning entry of the load chain (file_manager.py:128-136). Its only line is '0 0.1 0.1 0.2 0.1 0.2 0.2' terminated by a newline.  
  When  load_existing_mask walks the chain, picks _seg.txt and calls load_yolo_seg_txt(path, (480, 640)) inside a try/except Exception (file_manager.py:187-205, :542-600). The file is opened with a bare open(txt_path) and read with readlines() (:553-555).  
  Then  The line survives both gates and produces exactly one segment. (1) line.strip().split() gives 7 tokens; 7 \>= 7 and 7 is odd, so the line is kept (:562-565). (2) parts[0] = '0' is the label and is NEVER parsed as a number; only parts[1:] go through float() (:568). (3) Each pair is scaled x*w and y*h and converted with int(round(...)) -- Python round(), i.e. round-half-to-even on the IEEE-754 double product (:572-575): 0.1*640 = 64.0 -\> 64, 0.1*480 = 48.0 -\> 48, 0.2*640 = 128.0 -\> 128, 0.2*480 = 96.0 -\> 96, giving points [[64,48],[128,48],[128,96]]. (4) _build_label_map maps '0' -\> class_id 0 (:579, :345-379). (5) The polygon is rasterized by cv2.fillPoly into a bool array of shape (480, 640) that has exactly 1617 True pixels (:583-585). Concretely: filled rows are y = 48..96; row 48 spans x = 64..128 (65 px); row 95 is x = 127..128; row 96 is the single pixel x = 128. Boundary pixels are INSIDE -- (64,48), (128,48) and (128,96) are all True, while (63,48), (129,48) and (128,97) are False. Crucially (65,49) is True even though the geometric interior at y = 49 only starts at x = 65.333, because fillPoly also stamps an 8-connected Bresenham outline: a pure point-in-polygon or HTML-canvas fill of the same triangle yields only 1601 pixels and misses (65,49) plus 15 other outline pixels. (6) np.any(mask) is True so the polygon is not dropped (:587-588), and add_segment appends {'mask': \<bool (480,640) with 1617 True\>, 'type': 'Loaded', 'vertices': [[64,48],[128,48],[128,96]], 'class_id': 0} (:590-597, core/segment_manager.py:28-56). Afterwards segment_manager.next_class_id is 1 and class_aliases is still {} (a numeric label registers no alias).  
  And   RASTERIZATION CONTRACT (cv2.fillPoly, LINE_8, shift=0) -- a TypeScript port must build the mask as the UNION of two passes over an all-false (h, w) array; it is not a point-in-polygon test and not a canvas fill. PASS A, outline: for i = 0..n-1 draw the segment P[i-1] -\> P[i] (i = 0 wraps to the last vertex, so the ring is closed) as an 8-connected Bresenham line, then discard pixels outside the array. Bresenham, matching cv::LineIterator: if x1 \< x0 swap the two endpoints (the walk is always left-to-right, so the pixel set is direction-independent); dx = x1 - x0 (\>= 0), dy = |y1 - y0|, sy = sign(y1 - y0); if dy \> dx swap dx and dy and make y the major axis (minor step +1 in x) else x is the major axis (minor step sy in y); err = dx - 2*dy; repeat dx + 1 times { emit (x, y); neg = err \< 0; err += -2*dy + (neg ? 2*dx : 0); step the major axis; if neg also step the minor axis }. PASS B, interior: even-odd scanline in 16.16 fixed point. For each edge P[i-1] -\> P[i] with y0 != y1 (horizontal edges contribute nothing here, only to pass A) record ytop = min(y0,y1), ybot = max(y0,y1), xf = (x at ytop) \<\< 16, and dxf = trunc(((x1 \<\< 16) - (x0 \<\< 16)) / (y1 - y0)) using C-style truncation toward zero (NOT Math.floor -- they differ for negative slopes). If fewer than 2 such edges exist, pass B contributes nothing. For each scanline y from max(min ytop, 0) up to min(max ybot, h) - 1: collect xf + (y - ytop) * dxf for every edge with ytop \<= y \< ybot, sort ascending, and for each consecutive pair (L, R) compute xl = ceil(L / 65536) and xr = floor(R / 65536); skip the pair when xl \>= w or xr \< 0, otherwise clamp xl to \>= 0 and xr to \<= w-1 and set columns xl..xr inclusive. Pairing by parity is what makes the fill EVEN-ODD: a 5-point pentagram [[50,10],[74,82],[12,38],[88,38],[26,82]] on 100x100 fills 1410 px with its centre pixel (50,50) left False, where a canvas nonzero fill would fill it. This algorithm reproduces cv2.fillPoly bit-for-bit for every polygon whose integer vertices satisfy 0 \<= x \<= w-1 and 0 \<= y \<= h-1 (verified: 0 mismatches over 20,000+ randomised polygons plus the fixed cases in Edge cases, covering self-intersecting, collinear, single-point and thin shapes). AND the loader-level rules a test must also pin: class ids and aliases are assigned at :579 from ALL parsed lines BEFORE the empty-mask drop at :587-588, so a polygon that rasterizes to nothing still burns an id and registers its alias; a coordinate token that float() accepts but int(round()) rejects ('nan', 'inf', '1e999', '1e308') raises at :573 OUTSIDE the try at :567-570 and throws away every valid line in the file, after which load_existing_mask logs and falls through to _coco.json, _CM.npz, .xml, _createml.json and .txt (:202-204); a scaled value beyond int32 raises at :584 INSIDE the add loop, leaving a partial load onto which the fallback format is then merged; and a file that cannot be decoded returns quietly at :556-558, which is not an exception, so load_existing_mask returns at :205 and no fallback runs at all.  
**Parameters:** Minimum tokens per line: 7 (label + 3 coordinate pairs); token count must be odd; both checked together at file_manager.py:564, and a failing line is skipped with no log and no class id. Tokenisation: line.strip().split(), i.e. strip trailing CR/LF and split on any run of whitespace, so tab-separated and CRLF files parse and blank or whitespace-only lines yield 0 tokens and are skipped. Label: parts[0], any non-whitespace string, never numerically validated (:568 parses only parts[1:], :577 stores parts[0]). Coordinate parsing: Python float(), which accepts surrounding whitespace, a leading +/-, '5.', '.5', '1e-3' and the underscore form '1_0', and rejects 'abc' and '0x10' with ValueError (:567-570). Scaling: px = int(round(cx * w)), py = int(round(cy * h)), with image_size given as (h, w) (:551, :573). Rounding mode: Python round() = ROUND HALF TO EVEN on the double product, then int(); JavaScript Math.round (half up) is wrong. Worked ties on a 640x480 image: 0.16015625 * 640 = 102.5 -\> 102 (Math.round gives 103) and 0.171875 * 480 = 82.5 -\> 82 (Math.round gives 83). No clamping is applied to the scaled values. Rasterizer constants: fixed-point shift 16 (65536), line connectivity 8, fill rule even-odd, mask dtype bool of shape (h, w), fill value 1 then .astype(bool) (:583-585). Drop test: np.any(mask) (:587). Segment record: {'mask', 'type': 'Loaded', 'vertices', 'class_id'} (:590-597). The len(points) \>= 3 test at :576 is dead code -- any line that passed :564 yields at least 3 points.  
**Edge cases handled:** Even token count is rejected whole: '0 0.1 0.1 0.2 0.1 0.2 0.2 0.3' (8 tokens) produces no segment and no class id (file_manager.py:564). Verified against the legacy loader.; Fewer than 7 tokens is rejected whole, so a YOLO Detection file written under a colliding \<base\>_seg.txt name (5 tokens per line) loads zero segments; because nothing raises, load_existing_mask returns at :205 and never falls back to the real .txt, .xml or _coco.json. Verified: a 5-token _seg.txt alongside a valid .txt yields 0 segments.; A blank or whitespace-only line yields 0 tokens and is skipped silently (:562-564).; 'nan', 'NaN', 'inf', '-inf', 'Infinity', '1e999' and '1e308' all pass float() at :568, then raise ValueError/OverflowError at :573, which sits OUTSIDE the try at :567-570. Segments are appended only after the parse loop, so a single such token discards every valid line in the file. load_existing_mask catches it at :202-204 and silently degrades to the next format. Verified: a file of [valid, nan, valid] plus a .txt sidecar yields 1 bounding-box segment with no vertices. 'NaN' literally means not a number, so any port that merely 'skips non-numeric values' will diverge here.; A coordinate whose scaled value exceeds int32 (e.g. '1e7' on a 640-wide image -\> 6,400,000,000) raises OverflowError at :584, inside the add loop. Polygons already added stay, so the load is PARTIAL and the fallback format is then merged on top of it. Verified: 1 segment retained, then OverflowError.; 'abc' and '0x10' raise ValueError inside the try at :567-570 and skip only their own line; the rest of the file loads. Verified.; The label may be any string: 'dog 0.1 0.1 0.2 0.1 0.2 0.2' loads and registers class_aliases[0] = 'dog' (:345-379). Verified.; Numeric labels claim their id before names are handed out, so a file mixing '0' and 'dog' yields class ids 0 and 1 rather than merging them (:359-377). Verified: aliases {1: 'dog'}.; An existing class alias whose text equals the label beats int(label): with class_aliases = {7: '0'}, a line labelled '0' becomes class_id 7 (:362-364). Verified.; The label map is built at :579 from every parsed line, before the empty-mask drop at :587-588, so a polygon that rasterizes to nothing still consumes a class id and registers its alias. Verified: an off-image 'cat' took id 0 and alias 'cat' while the only visible polygon 'dog' got id 1.; Only polygons that rasterize to ZERO pixels are dropped; zero-area shapes that touch the image are kept. Three identical points give a 1-pixel mask; three collinear points ('0 0.0 0.0 0.32 0.0 0.64 0.0' on a 100x200 image -\> [[0,0],[64,0],[128,0]]) give a 129-pixel mask. Verified.; Coordinates are never clamped. A coordinate of exactly 1.0 maps to column w, which is off a w-wide image: on 640x480 a flat polygon at x = 1.0 fills 0 px and is dropped, while the identical shape at x = 0.0 fills 97 px and is kept. Vertices are stored unclamped even when the mask is clipped: '0 -0.5 -0.5 0.05 -0.5 0.05 0.05' stores [[-320,-240],[32,-240],[32,24]] with a 425-pixel mask. Verified.; Self-intersecting polygons fill even-odd, not nonzero: the pentagram [[50,10],[74,82],[12,38],[88,38],[26,82]] on 100x100 fills 1410 px and leaves its centre (50,50) empty. A default HTML-canvas fill would fill the centre.; Loaded segments keep their integer vertices but cannot be vertex-edited: edit mode only builds handles for type 'Polygon' or 'Circle' (ui/managers/edit_mode_manager.py:108-112; ui/main_window.py:2484), and 'Loaded' is also skipped by convert_ai_segments_to_polygons (core/segment_manager.py:853-857). Polygons over 200 vertices are additionally refused handles (edit_mode_manager.py:103, :112), which is irrelevant here because 'Loaded' never qualifies.; The file is opened with a bare open(txt_path), i.e. Python's locale default encoding (cp1252 on this Windows host), not UTF-8. A byte the locale codec cannot decode raises UnicodeDecodeError, caught at :556-558; the function logs and returns normally, so load_existing_mask sees no exception, returns at :205, and the image opens with zero segments and no fallback. Bytes that DO decode are accepted, so a UTF-16 or Latin-1 file can silently parse into garbage labels. Verified.; add_segment only defaults class_id when the key is absent (core/segment_manager.py:34-39); this loader always supplies class_id, so the user's active class never leaks into an import.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** Open question, answered from the approved decisions: KEEP the legacy whole-file stop for a non-finite or overflowing coordinate -- do not change it to per-line skipping -- but stop presenting the result as an empty or silently-degraded document. Per-line skipping would materialise polygons legacy never draws and so violates 'imported annotations must land on exactly the same pixels as legacy'; the abort itself is therefore in scope for the contract test (legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/file_manager.py:573 raises outside the try at :567-570, so a file of [valid, nan, valid] yields zero segments, and :584 raises inside the add loop so an int32 overflow yields a partial one). What must change is the presentation, because 'a failed or partial load must never present as no annotations': the port must surface an explicit import error, must NOT silently fall through to _coco.json/_CM.npz/.xml/_createml.json/.txt the way file_manager.py:202-204 does, must not merge a fallback format on top of a partial load, and must block the empty-segments delete path for that image (ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:106-109 -\> core/exporters/__init__.py:209-215 -\> core/exporters/yolo_segmentation.py:58-63, reachable unattended because config/settings.py:45 defaults auto_save to True). Class ids and aliases stay exactly as legacy computes them per image (file_manager.py:345-379), including the quirk that a dropped polygon still burns an id at :579 before the drop at :587-588, and that an existing alias literally named '0' beats int('0') at :362-364. What the card got wrong: 'all numeric' is wrong in both directions -- the label parts[0] is never parsed (:568 parses only parts[1:], so 'dog ...' loads and registers an alias), while 'nan'/'inf'/'1e999' ARE accepted by float() and then kill the whole file at :573; 'rounding Python round()' omits the mode, which is round-half-to-even on the double product (0.16015625*640 = 102.5 -\> 102 where JS Math.round gives 103), and omits that x scales by w and y by h from an (h, w) tuple (:551, :573) with no clamping; 'mask = filled triangle' hides the only thing a TypeScript port cannot guess -- cv2.fillPoly at :584 is an even-odd scanline fill UNIONed with an 8-connected Bresenham outline, giving 1617 px for the example including (65,49), which a point-in-polygon or canvas fill omits (1601 px); 'dropped if no pixel is set' is true but misleading, since zero-area polygons that touch the image are kept (1 px for three identical points, 129 px for three collinear ones) and only fully off-image shapes vanish (:587-588); Parameters listed 'min tokens 7' twice; and edge cases 3 and 4 were duplicates of each other. Added: the partial-load overflow at :584, the silent no-fallback return on an undecodable file at :556-558 (opened with the locale codec, not UTF-8), and the id-before-drop ordering at :579 vs :587. What an implementer must watch for: trunc-toward-zero (not floor) when computing the fixed-point slope, ceil on the left span edge and floor on the right, closing the ring from the last vertex back to the first, and that the specified rasterizer is verified pixel-exact only while every rounded vertex lies inside 0..w-1 by 0..h-1. **Still open for the owner:** One tolerance call for the product owner. The rasterization spec above is verified pixel-exact against cv2.fillPoly only while every rounded vertex lies inside 0..w-1 by 0..h-1. Once any vertex falls outside that box -- which a perfectly legal YOLO coordinate of exactly 1.0 does, since it maps to column w -- OpenCV first clips each outline segment with cv::clipLine and then runs a stateful active-edge walk whose pairing can differ from the fresh-sort formulation by roughly one pixel at the image border (measured: 0 mismatches in 20,000+ in-bounds cases, about 4 percent of cases with a vertex at exactly x == w or y == h, rising with how far out of range the coordinate is). Legacy's own YOLO Seg exporter never writes such a coordinate (core/exporters/yolo_segmentation.py:39-41 divides pixel indices 0..w-1 by w), so this only affects third-party files. The owner must choose: (a) port cv::FillEdgeCollection in full and gate it on a cv2 oracle test, or (b) accept a documented sub-pixel border difference for out-of-range coordinates, or (c) reject lines with coordinates outside [0, 1] at import -- which is itself a behaviour change, since legacy accepts them. Secondary: whether the loud import failure required above should merely warn or should hard-block auto-save for that image.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-042: Class alias editing is unvalidated
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:393-399; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/right_panel.py:228-240; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/right_panel.py:295-314; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/segment_table_manager.py:88-94`  
**Plain English:** Any text, including empty or duplicate text, is accepted as a class alias; classes without an alias show and export their number.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Class 3 has no alias  
  When  Exporting Pascal VOC before and after the user types 'person' as alias  
  Then  Before: object name '3'; after: 'person'  
**Parameters:** None  
**Edge cases handled:** Duplicate aliases merge classes when name-based files are reloaded; Editing the active class row can store the active-marker prefix (a diamond symbol plus space) inside the alias, which then appears in VOC/CreateML/COCO labels; Aliases are per image, not project-wide  
**Suspected defect:** Active-class display marker can leak into saved labels; no uniqueness check.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** ALIASES MUST BE NON-EMPTY AND TRIMMED; DUPLICATES ARE ALLOWED BUT WARNED; AND A DISPLAY MARKER MUST NEVER REACH A FILE. The question asks whether aliases must be unique, non-empty, and defined once per project. Three parts, and they do not all go the same way. NON-EMPTY: yes. An empty alias is indistinguishable from no alias in every export - both write the class id as the name - so accepting one lets a user believe they have named a class when they have not. An alias that is only whitespace is the same case. Trim, and treat the result as no alias rather than as an alias that happens to be blank. UNIQUE: no, but warn. Two classes called 'car' is almost certainly a mistake, and it makes the COCO categories block ambiguous and the VOC and CreateML files unreadable as a class map. But refusing the edit is worse than reporting it: a user renaming class 3 to 'car' before renaming class 1 away from 'car' would be blocked mid-way through a legitimate change. Accept it, mark both rows, and say what it costs. DEFINED ONCE PER PROJECT: no - decision 6 keeps aliases per image. Same answer as RULE-007 and RULE-014, same reason. THE DEFECT IS THE URGENT PART AND IS NOT A PRODUCT CHOICE. The card records that the active-class display marker can leak into a saved label. A marker is a UI affordance; if it can reach a file, then what the user sees and what they export have diverged, and the export is wrong in a way nothing reports. The rewrite must keep the stored alias and its presentation in separate values - the store holds what the user typed, the view decorates it - so there is no code path by which decoration can be persisted. That is structural, not a validation rule, and it is the fix worth making.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Must aliases be unique, non-empty, and defined once per project? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-043: Manual box and circle minimum sizes
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:370-449; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:451-537; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:187-203`  
**Plain English:** A drawn box must be at least 1 x 1 px and is stored as a 4-corner polygon; a circle needs radius of at least 1 px and is stored as center plus a 3 o'clock radius point; holding Shift at release erases instead.

  Given Circle mode; drag from (50,50) to (53.9,50)  
  When  Mouse released  
  Then  Circle segment with vertices [[50,50],[53.9,50]]; on save it rasterizes as a filled disc of radius round(3.9) = 4 at (50,50)  
**Parameters:** box min width and height 1 px; circle min radius 1.0 px  
**Edge cases handled:** A 0.5 x 3 px box creates nothing; Editing a circle's radius below 0.5 px keeps the segment but it saves as nothing  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee

### RULE-045: Crop coordinate validation and reuse by image size
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/crop_manager.py:111-153; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/border_crop_widget.py:104-141; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/handlers/single_view_mouse_handler.py:553-558; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/file_navigation_manager.py:227-238; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2153-2156; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/crop_manager.py:70-100`  
**Plain English:** A crop needs an open image; typed values must be integer 'start:end' pairs for both X and Y; values are rounded, clamped to 0..width-1 and 0..height-1, swapped if reversed, and remembered for images of the same width x height.  
**Specification:**  
  Given 640x480 image; user types X '700:-10', Y '20:460'  
  When  The user clicks Apply  
  Then  Crop stored as (0, 20, 639, 460) under key (640, 480); status 'Crop applied: 0:639, 20:460'  
**Parameters:** Drawn crop must exceed 5x5 px, then mode switches to AI points; memory key = (width, height)  
**Edge cases handled:** Zero-width crop (x1 = x2) is accepted and zeroes every annotation on save; Crop is reapplied by size only on the legacy tree-view load path; the main file-list/arrow-key path (load_image_by_path) clears it via reset and never restores it, although the size map still holds it; Clear Crop forgets the entry for the current image size; Missing either coordinate: 'Enter both X and Y coordinates'; non-numeric: 'Invalid coordinates. Use numbers only.'  
**Suspected defect:** Crop persistence by image size works on one of two navigation paths only.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** ANSWERED BY DECISION 9, which the owner approved: per-image-size crop restore does NOT carry over. A crop applies to the current image only. So the answer to 'should a crop automatically apply to every later image with the same resolution, and on all navigation paths?' is no to the first half, which makes the second half moot. The inconsistency the card records - that crop persistence works on the legacy file-tree path and not on the visible fast file list - is resolved by removing the persistence rather than by extending it to both paths. See RULE-067, which is the same decision seen from the other side. Removing it is the safer direction. A crop silently re-applied to a different image is a save that blanks everything outside a region the user drew for a different picture, and because the crop is applied ON SAVE the loss lands in the file rather than on the screen. WHAT IS PRESERVED is the validation, which is fidelity rather than preference and is already implemented in Phase 1's `applyCrop`: values are parsed as integer 'start:end' pairs for both axes, rounded, clamped to 0..width-1 and 0..height-1, and swapped when reversed. The off-by-one that blanks the last row and column is preserved deliberately under decision 15h, flagged for a later release that may break byte-identity. A crop needs an open image, and the rewrite keeps that: it is not a setting, it is an edit to one image's save.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should a crop automatically apply to every later image with the same resolution, and on all navigation paths? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-047: A polygon needs 3 points and closes when clicking within the join distance of its first point
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/polygon_drawing_manager.py:68-120; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/polygon_drawing_manager.py:166-224; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:87-98; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:187-230; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:26; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/annotation_settings_widget.py:89-93; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:26-26`  
**Plain English:** In polygon mode each click adds a vertex. Once there are more than 2 vertices, a click closer to the first vertex than the join threshold finishes the polygon (with Shift it erases instead), and Space also finishes it. Fewer than 3 vertices never create a segment.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Polygon mode, join threshold 2 px, vertices at (10,10), (50,10) and (50,50)  
  When  The user clicks at (11,11)  
  Then  Squared distance 2 is less than 4, so the polygon (10,10),(50,10),(50,50) is added with the active class (or the next free class) and add_segment is recorded for undo. A click at (12,10), squared distance 4, would add a fourth vertex instead.  
**Parameters:** polygon_join_threshold default 2 px, strict less-than on squared distance; Join threshold default 2 px, range 1-10; distance test strictly less than; minimum 3 points; polygon_join_threshold default 2, allowed 1-10  
**Edge cases handled:** In single view, Enter finishes the polygon and then saves.; Shift+click near the start, or Shift+Space, erases overlapping segments instead.; Each vertex click can be undone.; Enter in polygon mode does nothing in sequence view (only single and multi branches exist); Space/Enter with fewer than 3 points does nothing, silently; Threshold is in image pixels, not screen pixels  
**Suspected defect:** In the Sequence tab, Enter in polygon mode does nothing (no finish, no save), because only the single-view and multi-view branches exist.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-048: Reference frames must match the first reference's image size
**Category:** Validation  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/modes/sequence_view_mode.py:210-288; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:3877-3980; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:218-272; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:313-341`  
**Plain English:** The first frame marked as reference fixes the required height and width. Later frames of another size are refused, and the lock is released only when no references remain.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Timeline with no references; frame 5 is 1920x1080 and frame 9 is 1280x720  
  When  The user presses G on frame 5, then G on frame 9  
  Then  Frame 5 becomes 'reference' and the required size is locked at 1080 high x 1920 wide. Frame 9 is refused with 'Cannot add reference: image is 1280x720 but reference requires 1920x1080' and keeps its status. '+ All Before' on frame 40 adds every earlier frame that matches and reports the rest as '(N skipped: dimension mismatch)'. '+ All Labeled' considers only frames that already have an annotation file in any of the 7 formats.  
**Parameters:** Size is read from the image header (QImageReader); the (height, width) match must be exact; Size compared as (height, width), read from the file header  
**Edge cases handled:** If the header size cannot be read, the frame is accepted with no size check.; '+ All Before' follows on-screen order when the timeline is sorted.; Marking a reference copies the CURRENT frame's segments into every added frame's record (sequence_view_mode.py:239-240, 248-267); propagation never uses that copy.; Removing the last reference unlocks the size.; add_reference_frame falls back to the raw timeline index for an unmapped frame, which can point at a different image; 'Add all before' and 'Add all labeled' report rejections as 'skipped: dimension mismatch'; 'Add all labeled' accepts any annotation format although its docstring says NPZ; An unreadable image header skips the size check and the frame is accepted  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in
