Every non-reference, non-dimension-mismatch frame status is reset to PENDING (sequence_view_mode.py:143-159) and the timeline is repainted with frame 10 = 'reference' gold rgb(255,193,7) (main_window.py:4241-4246; timeline_widget.py:32). FRAME 10: the engine emits nothing for it — it `continue`s in SAM2 index space before building any result (propagation_manager.py:744-749 non-streaming, 1074-1076 streaming) — and the UI handler would return early anyway (main_window.py:4475-4481); its status stays REFERENCE and it never loads propagated masks when visited (main_window.py:3592-3606). FRAME 30: the engine still creates a PropagationResult per object, appends it to state.frameResults[30] and adds 30 to state.propagatedFrames (propagation_manager.py:783-805; streaming 1110-1125); the UI handler sees 30 in skipLabeledFrames, paints the timeline cell 'skipped' = rgb(139,69,19) brown with immediate repaint, and returns BEFORE the buffer (main_window.py:4466-4473) — so viewMasks[30] stays empty, no confidence is recorded, and the stored model status stays PENDING because markFramePropagated / recordSkippedObject never run (sequence_view_mode.py:290-345). FRAME 31: two objects return at 0.997 and 0.995, both \>= 0.99, so nothing is flagged; they accumulate in the per-frame buffer and commit via markFramePropagated(31, {1: mask, 2: mask}, min = 0.995) when the next non-skipped frame arrives or at propagation end (main_window.py:4483-4535, 4537-4574, 4596-4602), giving status PROPAGATED = green rgb(76,175,80). Clicking Save All then takes propagatedFrames {30, 31} minus flaggedFrames {} = [30, 31], announces 'Saving 2 frames...' (main_window.py:4761), `continue`s on frame 30 because getPropagatedMasks(30) is empty (main_window.py:4766-4768), writes only frames/img_031.npz and frames/img_031.txt, and reports 'Saved 1 frames to NPZ' (main_window.py:4846). frames/img_030_coco.json is byte-identical afterwards and no frames/img_030.npz or frames/img_030.txt is created.  
  And   The no-overwrite guarantee rests on TWO independent stores and a rewrite must gate the SAVE path, not only the paint path. The propagation engine keeps skip-labeled frame 30's masks in state.frameResults[30] and counts 30 in state.propagatedFrames (propagation_manager.py:793-805, 1119-1125), so the 'Propagation complete: N frames' message (main_window.py:4622-4634), the 'Saving N frames...' message (main_window.py:4761) and the post-save 'remaining' count (main_window.py:4843-4844) all over-count by the number of skipped frames. Frame 30 survives Save All only because the view-model store has no masks for it (main_window.py:4766-4768). The dormant PropagationSaveWorker reads the engine's own store (propagation_worker.py:295-370) and is never instantiated anywhere in the codebase; reviving that path would bypass this rule while every engine-level unit test still passed. Separately, the reference-frame half is an unconditional invariant enforced at four points (propagation_manager.py:744-749 and 1074-1076, main_window.py:4222-4224, 4475-4481, 3592-3606), whereas the skip-labeled half is a user-toggleable opt-out; only the first half must hold unconditionally in the rewrite.  
**Parameters:** skipLabeled: UI checkbox default TRUE (sequence_widget.py:347-348), read at sequence_widget.py:646-647 and carried through main_window.py:4096 -\> 4181 -\> 4200 -\> 4219; every engine/function-level default is FALSE (main_window.py:4017, 4181 `params.get("skip_labeled", False)`, 4200), and the checkbox state is NOT persisted to settings (no occurrence of skip_labeled outside main_window.py, sequence_widget.py and propagation_manager.py). keepFlagged: default FALSE (sequence_widget.py:334-336); engine receives skipFlagged = !keepFlagged (main_window.py:4377). confidenceThreshold: 0.99 (propagation_manager.py:94). 'Already labeled' = os.path.exists(splitext(imagePath)[0] + suffix) for the first hit in FileManager._LOAD_CHAIN, in this exact order: '.npz', '_seg.txt', '_coco.json', '_CM.npz', '.xml', '_createml.json', '.txt' (file_manager.py:128-136, 138-151). Same directory, same basename stem, existence only — the file is never opened, parsed or size-checked. Timeline colors: 'skipped' rgb(139,69,19), 'reference' rgb(255,193,7), 'propagated' rgb(76,175,80), 'pending' rgb(100,100,100), 'flagged' rgb(244,67,54), 'saved' rgb(0,188,212) (timeline_widget.py:31-40). Frame model statuses: PENDING/REFERENCE/PROPAGATED/FLAGGED/SAVED/SKIPPED/SUGGESTED (sequence_view_mode.py:20-30). Export formats default ['NPZ','YOLO_DETECTION'] (settings.py:8-9); autoSave default true (settings.py:45).  
**Edge cases handled:** The labeled set is a snapshot taken once in _start_propagation before the worker runs (main_window.py:4219-4231). A sidecar created during the run is not protected; a sidecar deleted during the run still causes the frame to be skipped.; Existence only — no parsing. A stray, empty, corrupt or unrelated \<stem\>.txt or \<stem\>.xml makes the frame count as labeled and suppresses propagation for it (file_manager.py:146-151). Conversely an annotation stored under any other name is invisible and the frame is propagated over.; Brown is not guaranteed. The timeline cell is painted 'skipped' only if the engine yields at least one result for that frame. If every object's mask is empty the engine drops it before yielding (propagation_manager.py:756-761, 1092-1093), frameDone never fires, and the frame keeps the gray 'pending' it was reset to at main_window.py:4243 — the labels are still preserved.; Brown is only a view state. The model status stays PENDING (sequence_view_mode.py:196-203), so any batch refresh from the model repaints the cell gray (main_window.py:3470-3472). Brown is also overloaded: dimension-mismatch frames use the same color but DO store FrameStatus.SKIPPED (sequence_view_mode.py:119-121, 590-596) and are exempt from the PENDING reset (sequence_view_mode.py:152-158).; Ordering: the skip-labeled check runs BEFORE the previous frame's buffer is committed and before _propagationPrevFrame is advanced (main_window.py:4466-4487). A skipped or reference frame therefore does not flush the prior frame's buffer; that frame commits on the next non-skipped frame or at propagation end (main_window.py:4596-4602). A rewrite that flushes on every frameDone changes timeline update ordering.; 'Frame 31 gets masks' is conditional. With Keep Flagged off (default), a single object below 0.99 sets any_failed and discards ALL of frame 31's masks; only the min confidence is recorded and the frame goes FLAGGED red (main_window.py:4553-4569; propagation_manager.py:764-776). Save All also excludes engine-flagged frames outright (main_window.py:4749-4756).; Propagation itself never writes to disk for any frame, with the flag on or off. Asserting 'img_030_coco.json untouched' immediately after propagation passes even if the guard is deleted. The guard only becomes observable at Save All (main_window.py:4741-4846) and at auto-save on navigate (main_window.py:3424-3427, 3480-3519).; With Skip Labeled OFF, writes go to the ENABLED export formats only (save_export_manager.py:117-122). With the defaults that is \<stem\>.npz and \<stem\>.txt, so an existing \<stem\>_coco.json is not rewritten but is permanently shadowed — .npz is first in the load chain (file_manager.py:128-136) — while an existing \<stem\>.txt is truly overwritten. An existing \<stem\>.xml is overwritten only if Pascal VOC is an enabled format.; Deletion path: if the frame's segment list ends up empty, save_single_view_output calls _delete_associated_files -\> delete_all_outputs, which removes EVERY registered format's sidecar for that image, not just the enabled ones (save_export_manager.py:106-109, 523-530; exporters/__init__.py:209-215). This is the only code path that deletes user annotation files and it can fire from auto-save on navigate.; Save All is not undoable: mark_frame_saved sets SAVED and deletes the frame's in-memory masks (sequence_view_mode.py:365-373), and undo history is frame-scoped and cleared on navigation (main_window.py:3588-3589).; The Skip Labeled tooltip says it protects 'saved annotations (NPZ files)' (sequence_widget.py:349-353) while the executable check covers all seven formats. The code is authoritative; following the tooltip reintroduces the data-loss bug recorded in CHANGELOG.md:20 and guarded by tests/unit/core/exporters/test_bbox_roundtrip.py:816-839.; Reference frames are excluded from the skip set (main_window.py:4222-4224) so their own annotations are protected by the reference guards, not by Skip Labeled — turning Skip Labeled off does not expose them.; Frames outside the propagation range are never reached, so they are neither painted nor propagated regardless of the flag.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** The open question is whether a bare sibling \<stem\>.txt (or any other same-stem file that is not really an annotation) should count as 'already labeled'. Answer: yes — keep the legacy behavior exactly. findAnnotationFile is a pure existence probe over the seven _LOAD_CHAIN suffixes in priority order and never opens the file (file_manager.py:128-136, 146-151), so a stray notes file suppresses propagation for that frame. Under the approved decisions this is the correct failure direction: a false 'labeled' only declines to propagate (non-destructive and user-visible as a brown cell), whereas a false 'unlabeled' leads to a bulk write that shadows or overwrites hand-made ground truth, and 'match legacy output within tolerance' forbids narrowing the set. What the card got wrong: (1) 'Frame 31 gets propagated masks' is unconditional — in fact with Keep Flagged off (default) a single object below the 0.99 threshold discards ALL of that frame's masks and flags it red (main_window.py:4553-4569; propagation_manager.py:764-776), and masks only land when the buffer is committed on the next non-skipped frame or at finish (main_window.py:4483-4487, 4596-4602). (2) 'Frame 30 is painted skipped (brown)' is unconditional — the paint only happens if the engine yields at least one non-empty result for that frame; all-empty masks are dropped before the yield (propagation_manager.py:756-761, 1092-1093) and the cell stays gray. (3) The card claims no masks exist for frame 30, but that is only true of the view-model store: the engine keeps them in state.frameResults[30] and counts 30 in state.propagatedFrames (propagation_manager.py:793-805, 1119-1125), which is why the 'Saving N frames...' and remaining counts over-count (main_window.py:4761, 4843-4844) and why the guarantee must gate the save path (main_window.py:4766-4768), not just the paint path. (4) 'Skip Labeled default True' is a UI-only default; every Python-level default is False (main_window.py:4017, 4181, 4200) and the checkbox is not persisted, so a service layer that copies the signature inherits the unsafe default. (5) The card's edge case 'propagated masks overwrite those files' is imprecise — writes only touch the enabled export formats (save_export_manager.py:117-122), default NPZ + YOLO Detection, so a _coco.json is shadowed by a new .npz rather than rewritten (file_manager.py:128-136); and the card omits the genuinely destructive path, where an empty segment set makes save_single_view_output call delete_all_outputs and remove every format's sidecar (save_export_manager.py:106-109; exporters/__init__.py:209-215). (6) The card omitted that the stored status staying 'pending' has one exception: a dimension-mismatch frame keeps FrameStatus.SKIPPED through the reset (sequence_view_mode.py:152-158), and that brown is overloaded between the two. An implementer must watch three things: gate the save path on the view store rather than the engine's propagatedFrames; do not flush the previous frame's buffer when a skipped or reference frame arrives (main_window.py:4466-4487); and never resurrect a save path that reads the engine's frameResults directly, the shape of the dormant PropagationSaveWorker (propagation_worker.py:295-370), which would silently defeat this rule. **Still open for the owner:** Two product calls remain. (a) The rule as written protects a frame whose only sidecar is a stray, empty or corrupt same-stem .txt/.xml; legacy parity says keep it, but the product owner may want a later, separately-versioned option to validate content before counting a file as 'labeled' — that would be a deliberate divergence from legacy output, not a bug fix. (b) The Skip Labeled tooltip (sequence_widget.py:349-353) understates the rule as 'NPZ files'; whether the rewrite ships the corrected wording ('any supported annotation file') is a copy decision. Both are cosmetic relative to the contract; neither blocks the equivalence test.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The fidelity judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-082: Save All propagated frames eligibility
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:4741-4846; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/modes/sequence_view_mode.py:365-373; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:3982-4009; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:97-133; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:394-417; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:300-309; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/propagation_manager.py:1254-1273`  
**Plain English:** 'Save All' exports each frame the propagation engine lists as propagated and not flagged, using its stored masks and each object's reference class, through the normal save (all selected formats, current crop).  
**Specification:**  
  Given Frame 3 propagated with masks, frame 4 flagged, frame 5 propagated but its masks were discarded  
  When  The user clicks Save All  
  Then  Only frame 3 is saved and marked SAVED (its in-memory masks cleared); frames 4 and 5 are skipped; notification 'Saved 1 frames to NPZ'  
**Parameters:** Eligibility = engine propagated_frames minus engine flagged_frames, and masks present; Object class = its reference annotation's class, else class 0 with name 'Class 0'  
**Edge cases handled:** The current crop applies to every saved frame; Frames already visited and auto-saved are skipped because their propagated masks were cleared; After 'Clear References', reference annotations are gone but propagated frames remain, so Save All writes every object as class 0; Image size and crop are taken from the frame currently displayed; Eligibility uses the propagation manager's flagged set, which follows later threshold changes, while timeline colors do not; Runs whatever the Auto-Save setting is.; After a Trim or New Timeline the engine is reset, so Save All reports 'No propagated frames to save' even while green frames with unsaved masks remain.; The export image size and active crop come from the frame on screen. If that frame's size differs (e.g. a skipped frame), every write fails with an error notice, but each frame is still marked saved and its masks dropped.; Saved segments are typed 'ai' (lowercase), while interactive AI segments use 'AI'.; The message says NPZ even when other formats are written.  
**Suspected defect:** When the reference class has no alias, propagated frames get alias 'Class N', so VOC/CreateML labels read 'Class 3' while the reference frame exports '3'; the message says NPZ although all selected formats are written. | Unsaved edits on the current frame are discarded; class-0 fallback can mislabel all objects. | Exclusion uses the engine's flagged set, which is recalculated when Min Conf changes, while the timeline's flags are not, so the frames saved can differ from the red frames shown. Failed writes are still recorded as saved.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** P0, OVERRIDING THE COMPLIANCE JUDGE, and both product questions answered. PRIORITY FIRST. The panel split on whether this guards annotation data integrity. It decides which frames get exported and what class ids their objects carry, and the card records that it gets the class NAMES wrong: when a reference class has no alias, propagated frames export the label 'Class 3' while the reference frame exports '3' - so one propagation produces two different names for one class inside a single dataset. That is a corrupted export, not a cosmetic difference, and VOC and CreateML carry names rather than ids so there is no id to fall back on. P0. SHOULD SAVE ALL FIRST COMMIT EDITS ON THE OPEN FRAME? Yes, and under decision 7 it asks rather than assuming. The alternative is what legacy does: Save All exports the STORED masks for every eligible frame, so a user who has just corrected the open frame and presses Save All exports the uncorrected version of it and their edit is gone. A bulk action must not silently discard the edit in front of it. SHOULD OBJECTS WITH UNKNOWN CLASS BE SAVED AS CLASS 0? No. Class 0 is a real class a user can draw, so writing unknown objects into it silently merges them with a legitimate one and the result is indistinguishable afterwards. An object whose class cannot be resolved is a reportable condition: name the frames, say how many objects, and let the user assign a class or skip them. Refusing to guess is the same principle as everything else in decision 7. AND THE ALIAS DEFECT IS FIXED RATHER THAN PRESERVED. A propagated object takes its reference object's class AND that class's alias, or no alias at all if there is none - never a manufactured 'Class N' string. 'Class N' is display text; it belongs in the UI and never in a file, which is the same rule RULE-042 states about the active-class marker. The card also notes the confirmation message says NPZ when the save writes the selected formats. Ship the corrected wording.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should Save All first commit edits on the open frame, and should objects with unknown class be saved as class 0? | The compliance judge rated this not P0 while the fidelity judge rated it P0. Decide whether it guards annotation data integrity and belongs in the behavior contract. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-083: Saving an image with no segments deletes all of its annotation files
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P0  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:97-133; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/save_export_manager.py:523-542; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/exporters/__init__.py:189-215; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:187-234; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/export_format_widget.py:94-106; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:6588-6594`  
**Plain English:** Every save rebuilds the image's annotations from the segments in memory. With at least one segment, each selected format is written and other formats are left alone. With zero segments, files in all seven formats are deleted.  
**Specification:**  
  Given LEGACY BEHAVIOUR ONLY - this specification documents what the PyQt6 app does, for migration/characterisation tests. It is NOT the target behaviour; see "answer". Given: the app is in single-view mode (main_window.view_mode == "single"); cat.png is the current image (main_window.current_image_path); the in-memory segment list is empty (segment_manager.segments == []); beside the image on disk sit cat.npz, cat.txt and cat_coco.json (and none of cat_CM.npz, cat_seg.txt, cat.xml, cat_createml.json); the selected export formats are the defaults {NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION}; "Auto-Save on Navigate" is ON (its default); there are no pending polygon points and no pending AI segment preview (Enter commits either of those first, and a committed one would add a segment and take the image off this path).  
  When  Any save is triggered for that image. In this configuration there are five distinct triggers, all of which converge on save_export_manager.save_single_view_output (save_export_manager.py:97-133): (1) the user presses Return or Enter (hotkey "save_output"/"save_output_alt", config/hotkeys.py:67-69 -\> main_window.py:1014 -\> keyboard_event_manager.handle_enter_press:187-234, which reaches main_window._save_output_to_npz:1993-1998 at line 205, 230 or 234); (2) the user picks another file in the file tree, with auto_save on (file_navigation_manager.py:157-160); (3) the user navigates next/previous, with auto_save on (file_navigation_manager.py:271-274); (4) in sequence view, the timeline moves to a different frame, with auto_save on (main_window.py:3424-3427 -\> _auto_save_sequence_frame:3480-3519, which temporarily points current_image_path at the frame being left, line 3494-3496); (5) "Save All Propagated" in sequence view, which is NOT gated by auto_save (main_window.py:4741-4825, saving at 4813). In every case the check at save_export_manager.py:107 - `if not self.segment_manager.segments:` - is evaluated before the export-format setting is read at 118-120, and on an empty list control goes to _delete_associated_files (line 108) and returns at 109 without writing anything.  
  Then  _delete_associated_files(cat.png) (save_export_manager.py:523-542) calls delete_all_outputs (core/exporters/__init__.py:209-215), which walks EXPORTERS.values() in registration order - coco, createml, npz, npz_class_map, pascal_voc, yolo_detection, yolo_segmentation (import order, core/exporters/__init__.py:224-230) - and for each exporter calls delete_output(image_path), which computes os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + \<that format's suffix\>, and if os.path.exists() then os.remove()s it (identical five-line body in all seven: coco.py:102-107, createml.py:69-74, npz.py:32-37, npz_class_map.py:49-54, pascal_voc.py:65-70, yolo_detection.py:48-53, yolo_segmentation.py:58-63). Seven paths are probed - cat_coco.json, cat_createml.json, cat.npz, cat_CM.npz, cat.xml, cat.txt, cat_seg.txt - and the three that exist are permanently removed: cat_coco.json, cat.npz, cat.txt. cat_coco.json is deleted even though COCO JSON was never a selected format: the selected-format set is not consulted on this path at all. The file-tree row for cat.png is refreshed (save_export_manager.py:531-534) and a neutral notification is shown whose text is exactly "Deleted: cat_coco.json, cat.npz, cat.txt" - the deleted paths in the iteration order above, basename only, joined by ", " (save_export_manager.py:536-538) - followed by _update_all_lists() and QApplication.processEvents() (539-540). No confirmation dialog is shown before or after (there is no QMessageBox anywhere in save_export_manager.py or main_window.py) and there is no undo entry. CONTRAST, same image with 2 segments: the empty check at 107 is false, export_all (core/exporters/__init__.py:189-206) runs only the two selected exporters and never deletes, so cat.npz and cat.txt are overwritten, cat_coco.json is left untouched on disk, and the notification reads "Saved: cat.npz, cat.txt" (save_export_manager.py:124-126).  
  And   And the deletion is unconditional on provenance, content and history: delete_output tests only os.path.exists on a name derived from the image's basename. Nothing records who wrote the file, whether LazyLabel ever read it, whether the user changed anything this session, or whether the image loaded successfully - there is no dirty flag and no "user cleared the annotations" flag anywhere on the path. And the only guard before the delete is `if not self.mw.current_image_path` (save_export_manager.py:102-104, warning "No image loaded."); if nothing was found to delete the user instead gets the warning "No segments to save." (save_export_manager.py:541-542). And in multi-view the same destruction runs through a different function with different rules: main_window._save_multi_view_annotations (6559-6636) loops `for viewer_idx in range(2)` (6581) and calls delete_all_outputs directly at 6590 when that viewer's segment manager is empty; it never reads auto_save, and its three navigation callers (main_window.py:6497 _load_next_multi_batch, 6530 _load_previous_multi_batch, file_navigation_manager.py:402-403 load_multi_view_from_path) do not check auto_save either, so turning Auto-Save off does not stop deletion in multi-view. And multi-view deletion is silent - a logger.debug at 6592 only (no "Deleted:" notification, no "No segments to save."), while pressing Enter in multi-view still reports success: "Multi-view annotations saved!" (save_export_manager.py:592-593) even when the call only deleted files. And the deletion is not transactional: delete_output has no try/except, and in single view the call at 106-109 sits outside the try that starts at 111, so if os.remove raises (a file locked by another process on Windows) the loop stops part-way with some files already gone, and the exception escapes save_output -\> _save_output_to_npz into file_navigation_manager.py:160/274, which has no handler, aborting the navigation; the sequence path (main_window.py:3498-3517) and the multi-view path (6588, 6635-6636) catch and log it instead.  
**Parameters:** Deleted path formula: os.path.splitext(image_path)[0] + suffix, probed and deleted in this exact order (EXPORTERS dict insertion order, set by the import block at core/exporters/__init__.py:224-230): 1. "_coco.json" (coco.py:100), 2. "_createml.json" (createml.py:67), 3. ".npz" (npz.py:30), 4. "_CM.npz" (npz_class_map.py:47), 5. ".xml" (pascal_voc.py:63), 6. ".txt" (yolo_detection.py:46), 7. "_seg.txt" (yolo_segmentation.py:56). Exactly seven ExportFormat members exist (core/exporters/__init__.py:14-23). The order matters only if a test asserts the notification string. The class-aliases file \<base\>.json is NOT in this set and is never deleted on any live path (see edge cases). Trigger predicate: `not segment_manager.segments` evaluated at save time (save_export_manager.py:107) - no other condition. Selected export formats are irrelevant to deletion (read only at 118-120, after the early return at 109). Defaults: export_formats = {NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION} (config/settings.py:8-9, mirrored at core/exporters/__init__.py:60-63); at least one format must stay selected - unchecking the last one re-checks it (ui/widgets/export_format_widget.py:94-103); auto_save = True (config/settings.py:45, ui/widgets/settings_widget.py:43 and 200), labelled "Auto-Save on Navigate" (settings_widget.py:39). Exact user-visible strings: "Deleted: " + ", ".join(basenames) (save_export_manager.py:536-538, neutral notification); "No segments to save." (542, warning); "No image loaded." (103, warning); "Saved: " + ", ".join(basenames) on the write path (125); "Multi-view annotations saved!" (593, success); "No AI segment preview to accept" (ui/managers/ai_segment_manager.py:127-128). Multi-view processes only viewer indices 0 and 1 (main_window.py:6581), regardless of the 2_view/4_view grid setting.  
**Edge cases handled:** Nothing exists to delete: delete_all_outputs returns [], so no file-tree refresh and no "Deleted:" notice - the user sees the warning "No segments to save." instead (save_export_manager.py:529, 541-542).; No image open: save_single_view_output returns at 102-104 with the warning "No image loaded."; nothing is written or deleted.; Enter in any mode other than polygon first calls _accept_ai_segment (keyboard_event_manager.py:231-233). With no preview pending the user sees "No AI segment preview to accept" (ai_segment_manager.py:127-128) and line 234 still runs the save, so the deletion happens anyway. With a preview pending, accepting it adds a segment and the save writes files instead.; Enter in single-view polygon mode first finalises pending polygon points (keyboard_event_manager.py:198-202); polygon_drawing_manager.finalize_polygon returns immediately if fewer than 3 points (172-173), so 1-2 stray points do not save the image from deletion, but 3+ do.; Enter in sequence view while in polygon mode does nothing at all: handle_enter_press branches only on view_mode "single" (198) and "multi" (206) inside the polygon branch, with no else, so no save and no deletion occur (keyboard_event_manager.py:193-230).; Multi-view navigation deletes with Auto-Save OFF: _save_multi_view_annotations (main_window.py:6559-6636) never reads auto_save and is called unconditionally by _load_next_multi_batch (6496-6497), _load_previous_multi_batch (6529-6530) and load_multi_view_from_path (file_navigation_manager.py:402-403).; Multi-view deletion is invisible: only logger.debug (main_window.py:6591-6593). Enter in multi-view then reports "Multi-view annotations saved!" (save_export_manager.py:592-593) even when the operation only deleted files.; The eighth file \<base\>.json (class aliases) is NEVER deleted by any reachable path. _delete_multi_view_files (save_export_manager.py:544-567) does delete it, but its only caller is _save_viewer_output (454-521, call at 519), and _save_viewer_output has zero callers in src/ or tests/. Both are dead code; a rewrite must not reproduce them.; "Save All Propagated" in sequence view is not gated by auto_save (main_window.py:4741-4825). It clears the segment manager per frame (4776) and skips any propagated mask that is not 2-D after squeezing (4781-4784); if every mask for a frame is skipped the list stays empty, all seven sidecars for that frame are deleted at 4813, and the frame is still marked saved at 4814 with a green timeline status at 4815-4818.; Deletion is by filename only, with no provenance or content check, so any same-named file is destroyed: a caption file cat.txt written by another tool is deleted as if it were YOLO Detection output, and cat.xml metadata as if it were Pascal VOC (yolo_detection.py:48-53, pascal_voc.py:65-70).; Sibling-image collision: the suffixes are not reserved namespaces. Saving an empty foo.png deletes foo_seg.txt and foo_CM.npz, which are the YOLO-Segmentation and NPZ outputs of the different images foo_seg.png and foo_CM.png. Likewise foo.png and foo.jpg in one folder share every sidecar name, so emptying one wipes the other's annotations.; A sidecar that loads successfully but yields zero segments stops the load chain and dooms the healthy files behind it: load_existing_mask returns at file_manager.py:205 after the first format that does not raise, so an NPZ with neither "mask" nor "masks" (silent return at 241-242), a _CM.npz with no class_map or a shape mismatch against the image (298-300, 309-311, 313-318), or a .txt whose lines do not have exactly 5 fields (433-434, plus the ValueError skip at 437-440) all leave 0 segments with no error - and the next save deletes every sidecar, including the lower-priority .xml or _coco.json that was never even read. Load-chain order is .npz, _seg.txt, _coco.json, _CM.npz, .xml, _createml.json, .txt (file_manager.py:128-136).; CORRECTED from the card: a sidecar that RAISES while loading does NOT by itself cause this. file_manager.py:202-204 logs the exception and `continue`s to the next format, so a corrupt top-priority file still lets a healthy lower-priority one load. Only the silent-zero-segment returns above (and an unreadable image, next item) produce the empty list.; An image that cannot be decoded still gets its sidecars deleted. In load_image_by_path the order is: current_image_path = path (file_navigation_manager.py:276), _reset_state() (279, which clears the segment manager at main_window.py:2140-2141), segment_manager.clear() (281), then cv2.imread returns None and the function returns at 299-302 with no annotations loaded. The undecodable image is now the current image with an empty list, so the next auto-save navigation deletes all seven of its sidecars.; The sibling path load_selected_image returns even earlier, at file_navigation_manager.py:175-176, when QImage cannot read the file - after current_image_path was reassigned at 162 but before _reset_state() at 186 - so the PREVIOUS image's segments stay in memory attached to the new path; the next save writes them under the new image's basename, or, if that list was empty, deletes the new image's sidecars.; Partial deletion on I/O error: delete_output has no try/except, so a locked file raises mid-loop and the remaining formats are never probed. In single view the delete runs outside the try block (save_export_manager.py:106-109 vs the try at 111), so the error propagates out through file_navigation_manager.py:160/274 and the next image never loads; sequence mode (main_window.py:3498-3517) and multi-view (6588, 6635-6636) swallow it into the log.; Write-path asymmetry worth pinning in the same test: with segments present, a SELECTED format can still be skipped, leaving a stale file of that very format on disk - npz.py:15-17 returns None when the mask tensor is empty, yolo_detection.py:19-22 and 35-36 and yolo_segmentation.py:28, 46 return None when no annotations are produced, npz_class_map.py:22-26 when nothing is active, coco.py:85 and createml.py:51 likewise. export_all only reports paths actually written (core/exporters/__init__.py:198-206).; An active crop is applied to the written masks (save_export_manager.py:412-417, crop_coords from crop_manager.current_crop_coords) but has no effect on the deletion path, which never builds an ExportContext.; Documentation contradicts the code and must not be used as the contract: core/exporters/__init__.py:192-196 states that writing never deletes and that neighbouring files "may be ... ground truth that shipped with the dataset", and ARCHITECTURE.md:193 says delete_all_outputs is "Used only when the image has no segments left, i.e. the user cleared the annotations". The code makes no such check. The comments at main_window.py:3499 and 3515 also claim only the NPZ is deleted; all seven formats are.  
**Suspected defect:** Nothing tracks whether the image changed or loaded successfully, so an empty list is read as 'delete everything'. | Contradicts export_all's stated policy that writing never deletes ground truth shipped with the dataset; combined with loaders that silently fail, a damaged or unparseable annotation file makes the image look empty and its ground truth is destroyed on navigation. | The export contract says files already next to the image 'may be ... ground truth that shipped with the dataset' and are left alone (core/exporters/__init__.py:192-197), and ARCHITECTURE.md:193 says deletion is used only when the user cleared the annotations. The code cannot distinguish 'user cleared everything' from 'nothing was ever loaded', so it destroys annotations it never read.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** DECISION (already made by the product owner, recorded here so no implementer re-opens it): the rewrite uses explicit save with dirty tracking and NEVER deletes an annotation file without an explicit user action, so this legacy behaviour is a defect to be fixed, not reproduced - deletion must not be inferred from an empty in-memory segment list, must not extend to formats the user did not select, and must not touch files LazyLabel did not write in the current session. Everything in given/when/then/and/parameters/edgeCases above is therefore a description of LEGACY behaviour, intended only for characterisation tests that pin the PyQt6 app before/during migration and for a deliberate, documented divergence note; it is not a target specification, and a TypeScript implementer must not treat it as one. What the card got wrong: (1) the notification order was implied as "cat.npz, cat.txt, cat_coco.json" but delete_all_outputs iterates EXPORTERS.values() in import order (core/exporters/__init__.py:212 with the import block at 224-230), so the real string is "Deleted: cat_coco.json, cat.npz, cat.txt" (save_export_manager.py:536-538); (2) the Given omitted the preconditions that actually gate the path - single-view mode, auto_save on, and no pending polygon points or AI preview, both of which Enter commits first (keyboard_event_manager.py:198-202, 231-234; polygon_drawing_manager.py:172-173); (3) the When named only two of five triggers - it missed sequence-frame change (main_window.py:3424-3427 -\> 3480-3519), Save All Propagated (main_window.py:4741-4825, ungated by auto_save, deleting a frame's files at 4813 when every mask fails the 2-D check at 4781-4784 while still marking it saved at 4814), and the whole multi-view family (main_window.py:6497, 6530; file_navigation_manager.py:402-403 -\> main_window.py:6589-6594), which deletes even with Auto-Save OFF, silently, while Enter still reports "Multi-view annotations saved!" (save_export_manager.py:592-593); (4) the edge case claiming that annotations which "failed to load" leave 0 segments is wrong as stated - file_manager.py:202-204 catches a raising loader and continues down the chain, so a corrupt file does not hide a healthy one; the real hazard is a loader that returns silently with nothing (file_manager.py:241-242, 298-300, 309-311, 313-318, 433-440) and thereby ends the chain at file_manager.py:205; (5) the card's "and" was null and its parameters string was a triple-concatenated duplicate of the suffix list with three near-identical edge cases repeated (the "No image loaded" case appeared twice and the unrelated-.txt case three times) - all deduplicated; (6) I REFUTE the first compliance judge's "eighth file" finding: \<base\>.json is deleted only by _delete_multi_view_files (save_export_manager.py:544-567), whose sole caller _save_viewer_output (454-521, call at 519) has no callers anywhere in src/ or tests/, so it is dead code and the live multi-view path (main_window.py:6590) deletes the same seven files as single view. What an implementer must watch for if they ever run this legacy path for comparison: deletion is name-derived only (splitext(image)[0] + suffix, no provenance check), so it collides with sibling images (foo.png wipes foo_seg.txt and foo_CM.npz, the outputs of foo_seg.png and foo_CM.png) and with same-stem different-extension pairs (foo.png / foo.jpg); it is non-transactional (no try/except in any delete_output, e.g. npz.py:32-37, and in single view it runs outside the try at save_export_manager.py:106-111, so a locked file leaves a half-deleted set and aborts navigation); and it fires on an image that never decoded, because file_navigation_manager.py:276-281 sets current_image_path and clears the segment manager before cv2.imread fails at 299-302. Finally, the executable code - not the prose - is the rule: core/exporters/__init__.py:192-196 and ARCHITECTURE.md:193 both assert guarantees the code does not implement, and main_window.py:3499/3515 claim only the NPZ is removed when all seven formats are.  
**Answer reversed (2026-09-26):** the owner chose "Match the desktop app exactly". A save with no segments deletes all seven sidecar formats, whatever is selected, in legacy's order, with legacy's notices ("Deleted: …", or "No segments to save." when nothing existed). This applies to Enter, the Write button and leaving an image with Auto-Save on, changed or not (`eeefbe0`). In the Multi view an empty side is deleted silently on every move with Auto-Save on (CP-67); with it off, nothing is saved or deleted on a move (the owner, 2026-09-26). `DELETE .../annotations` removes exactly the seven paths; a `PUT` never deletes (`38be6be`, `64198d1`). Kept from 2026-09-19: an image whose annotations could not be read is never written over or deleted (SEC-04, `canSave`); legacy deletes it.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: The compliance judge found the specification unfaithful to the code. Correct the card from the judge's findings before writing its equivalence test. Judges' reasoning: analysis/lazylabel/P0_PANEL.md. | Should deletion happen only when the user explicitly removed previously loaded segments, and only for files LazyLabel itself wrote? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-084: AI features require segment-anything and PyTorch 2.7.1+
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ai_availability.py:8-47; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:274-311; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/sequence_widget.py:825-835; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1234-1238`  
**Plain English:** AI tools are enabled only if segment-anything imports and the PyTorch version is 2.7.1 or newer; otherwise AI actions show the install hint.  
**Specification:**  
  Given torch 2.6.0+cu124 installed  
  When  The application starts  
  Then  AI unavailable; Propagate button disabled; Load Model shows 'AI features require additional packages. Install with: pip install lazylabel-gui[include-ai]'  
**Parameters:** MIN_TORCH_VERSION 2.7.1; local build suffix after '+' ignored; only the first three version parts are compared as integers  
**Edge cases handled:** Pre-release versions such as '2.8.0a0' or '2.10.0rc1' raise ValueError during parsing; only ImportError is caught, so startup fails; SAM 2 additionally requires the sam2 package; Pre-release versions such as 2.8.0a0 or 2.10.0rc1 have a non-numeric third part; int() raises ValueError and only ImportError is caught.  
**Suspected defect:** Unhandled ValueError for pre-release PyTorch version strings. | The version parser catches only ImportError.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** ANSWERED, AND ALREADY BUILT IN PHASE 2. The question had two parts. ARE PRE-RELEASE OR NIGHTLY PYTORCH BUILDS SUPPORTED? Yes, when the version is at or above the 2.7.1 minimum - and the build is reported as a pre-release rather than silently treated as a release, so an odd failure later is explicable. There is no reason to refuse a nightly that is newer than the floor, and refusing one is not what the legacy code does either; it does something considerably worse. DOES THE UNCAUGHT ValueError STOP THE APP FROM STARTING? Yes. `ai_availability.py` computes the version as `[int(p) for p in torch.__version__.split("+")[0].split(".")[:3]]` inside a guard that catches only ImportError. A nightly reports '2.8.0a0', so the third part is '0a0', `int()` raises ValueError, nothing catches it, and the import-time check takes the application down. The failure mode is the worst available combination: a user on a NEWER PyTorch than required cannot start LazyLabel at all, and the traceback points at a file whose entire job is to answer 'are the AI features available?'. A check for whether a feature is present should never be able to stop the program, and this one stops it for being too up to date. THE ANSWER IS IN `modernized/lazylabel-reimagined/inference/src/lazylabel_inference/availability.py`, built as part of the Phase 2 scaffold because it needs no model and no PyTorch to be correct. The parser is total: `parse_version` returns None for anything it cannot read instead of raising, and `check_availability` turns every outcome into a reported reason - not installed, too old, unreadable, segment-anything missing, or available. An unreadable version disables the AI features and says so; it does not crash, and it does not optimistically assume the build is fine. Tested at `inference/tests/test_availability.py` against every string the card names - 2.7.1, 2.7.1+cu124, 2.8.0a0, 2.10.0rc1, 2.7.1.dev20250101+cpu - and against unreadable input, with the version injected so none of it requires installing the build that produces it. That is the part legacy could not have tested even if it had wanted to. One design point worth recording for Phase 3: in the web rebuild this check no longer gates a button in the same process. The inference service answers it on `/health` with the reason attached, the API relays it, and the web app disables the AI tools with that reason shown. A user who cannot use the AI tools is told why and what to install, instead of finding a dead control.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Are pre-release or nightly PyTorch builds supported? With such a build, does the uncaught ValueError during the import-time check stop the app from starting? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-085: Model type detected from file name
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/model_manager.py:58-104; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:264-298; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam2_model.py:600-630`  
**Plain English:** Checkpoints (.pth/.pt, searched recursively) are classified by substrings: names containing sam2, sam2.1, hiera, _t., _s., _b+. or _l. are SAM 2 (size tiny/_t, small/_s, base_plus/_b+, large/_l, default large); others are SAM 1 (vit_l/large, vit_b/base, vit_h/huge, default vit_h).  
**Specification:**  
  Given File sam_vit_b_01ec64.pth  
  When  Selected and loaded  
  Then  Loaded as SAM 1 vit_b  
**Parameters:** SAM 2 size checks: tiny/_t, small/_s, base_plus/_b+, large/_l (image); large/hiera_l, base/hiera_b, small/hiera_s, tiny/hiera_t (video); SAM 1: vit_l/large, vit_b/base, vit_h/huge, default vit_h  
**Edge cases handled:** my_vit_l.pth contains '_l.' and is treated as SAM 2, failing to load; sam2_hiera_large_tuned.pt contains '_t' and gets the tiny config; database_v2.pth contains 'base' and loads as vit_b; Name containing '2.1' selects SAM 2.1 configs  
**Suspected defect:** Substring matching misclassifies renamed checkpoints. | Substring matching can choose different model sizes for the image and video predictors and misdetect names containing '_s' or '_t'.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** EXPLICITLY, FROM A MANIFEST - not from the file name, and not from checkpoint metadata either. Both questions on the card get the same answer for the same reason. SHOULD MODEL TYPE BE CHOSEN EXPLICITLY OR READ FROM CHECKPOINT METADATA? Explicitly, in a manifest that is written once when the checkpoint is mirrored and is then the only thing consulted. Checkpoint metadata sounds better than a file name and is still the wrong source: reading it means loading enough of a .pth to inspect it, which is an unpickle of an untrusted file (SEC-01), and a fine-tune saved by a third-party script may carry no such metadata or carry someone else's. A manifest entry is a statement by whoever put the file there, which is the only party that actually knows. SHOULD MODEL SIZE COME FROM METADATA RATHER THAN FILE-NAME SUBSTRINGS? Same answer, same manifest, same line. WHY THE FILE NAME CANNOT WORK, using the card's own cases. Legacy matches substrings, so: my_vit_l.pth              contains '_l.'  -\> read as SAM 2 large -\> fails to load at all sam2_hiera_large_tuned.pt contains '_t'   -\> read as SAM 2 TINY  -\> loads the wrong config database_v2.pth           contains 'base' -\> read as SAM 1 vit_b -\> loads as the wrong variant The middle one is the instructive failure: it does not error. It builds a tiny config around large weights, and what the user gets is degraded output from a model they believe is their fine-tuned large. The card also records that the image and video predictors can pick DIFFERENT sizes from the same name, because they match different substring tables - so one checkpoint can be two different models depending on which code path reached it. Underneath all of that is the design mistake rather than the bug: renaming a file changes how the software interprets its contents. A name is a place to find bytes, not a claim about what they are. BUILT IN PHASE 2, at `modernized/lazylabel-reimagined/inference/src/lazylabel_inference/manifest.py`. Each entry declares name, family (sam1 or sam2), size, filename, sha256 and byte count; the parser refuses a family or size that is not valid for that family, a hash that is not 64 hex characters, a non-positive size, a duplicate model name, and a filename that is anything other than one plain path component. `tests/test_manifest.py::TestFileNameIsNotMeaning` runs all three of the card's misclassification cases and asserts the manifest reads them correctly, and asserts that renaming a checkpoint changes nothing about how it is interpreted. A consequence worth stating: a checkpoint that is not in the manifest is not loadable. That is deliberate. Legacy searched recursively for .pth and .pt files and guessed at whatever it found, which is how an unrelated file in a subfolder became a model candidate. Adding a checkpoint is now an explicit act with a hash attached. **Still open for the owner:** How a user adds their own fine-tuned checkpoint in a hosted deployment, where they do not have shell access to edit a manifest. Phase 3 needs an upload path that computes the hash and appends an entry, or the feature is desktop-only. Not blocking the port - the manifest is right either way - but it is a capability legacy had and this design does not yet.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should model type be chosen explicitly or read from checkpoint metadata? | Should model size come from checkpoint metadata rather than file-name substrings? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-086: Active class toggle and recent-class hotkey
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:401-440; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2697-2738; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2712-2738 (live X handler; bound at main_window.py:1011 and legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/hotkeys.py:104-106); legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:41-42 (adding a segment records its class as most recently used), :87-95 (unique class IDs sorted ascending), :409-423 (toggle records and returns last toggled class). Remove segment_manager.py:425-440: it is unused and uses the opposite (highest-ID) fallback.`  
**Plain English:** Clicking a class row toggles it as the class for new segments; X toggles the most recently used or toggled class, or if none, the first/last table row (Pixel Priority on, Ascending/Descending) or the lowest class ID.  
**Specification:**  
  Given No class used yet on this image; classes 2 and 5 exist; Pixel Priority off  
  When  The user presses X twice  
  Then  First press: class 2 active ('Class 2 activated for new segments'); second press: deactivated ('No active class - new segments will create new classes')  
**Parameters:** Adding a segment also counts as using its class  
**Edge cases handled:** Adding any segment makes its class the recent class; State resets on image change; An unused helper (segment_manager.py:425-440) would pick the highest ID instead; No classes shows 'No classes available to toggle'  
**Suspected defect:** The unused SegmentManager.get_class_to_toggle_with_hotkey documents a highest-id fallback, conflicting with the live lowest-id behavior.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** THE LIVE CODE WINS, and the referee's citation correction is adopted. The card is right and half its citation points at code that does the opposite. `SegmentManager.get_class_to_toggle_with_hotkey` documents a HIGHEST-id fallback and is never called; the live handler at `main_window.py:2712-2738` implements a LOWEST-id fallback. The specification follows the live code, because that is what users have been using: X toggles the most recently used or toggled class; with none, the first or last row of the table depending on Pixel Priority and its ascending flag; and with no table, the LOWEST class id. The unused method should be deleted rather than ported. Carrying dead code that contradicts the live behaviour into the rewrite is how the wrong branch gets chosen later by someone reading the more plausible-looking one - and it is the more plausible-looking one, which is exactly the hazard. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THE UI. The active class decides which class new segments take (RULE-011), and that is written into every exported file. A hotkey that selects a different class than the user expects produces correctly-formatted annotations with the wrong labels, which is the kind of error a dataset carries silently into training. Phase 4 owns the class table and the active-class state; Phase 5 binds the key. The shared settings schema already refuses to bind X to two actions (RULE-049), so the hotkey half is in place.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Citation was corrected by referee (The rule is right, but only half the citation supports it. The other half points at unused code that does the opposite. Live code, which implements the rule (main_window.py:2712-2738): - X is bound to toggle_recent_class (config/hotkeys.py:105), which calls _toggle_recent_class (main_window.py:1011). - It first uses get_last_toggled_class() and toggles that class if it is set (2715-2718). - Otherwise, if settings.pixel_priority_enabled is on, it reads the class table order from the right panel (right_panel.py:361-371). It takes the first row when ascending (2725-2726) and the last row otherwise (2727-2728). - If pixel priority is off, it takes get_unique_class_ids()[0] (2729-2733). That list is sorted ascending (segment_manager.py:87-95), so this is the lowest ID. - If nothing is found, it shows "No classes available to toggle" (2737-2738). - Spec check: classes {1,4,9}, pixel priority off (the default, settings.py:61), no recent class. The list is [1,4,9], so it picks 1. toggle_active_class(1) sets active_class_id=1 (segment_manager.py:414-419). The spec holds. Problems with the citation: 1. segment_manager.py:425-440 (get_class_to_toggle_with_hotkey) has no callers anywhere in legacy/lazylabel, src or tests. It picks the HIGHEST ID (unique_class_ids[-1], line 438) and ignores pixel priority. It is unused code that contradicts the rule, so it should not be cited as evidence. 2. The parameter "adding a segment counts as using its class" is not in either cited range. There it appears only in docstrings (main_window.py:2713, segment_manager.py:428). The code that does it is segment_manager.py:41-42: add_segment sets last_toggled_class_id. Nuances: - "Otherwise" means "pixel priority off". If pixel priority is on but the class table is empty, there is no fallback to the lowest ID; the "No classes available" message is shown instead. - last_toggled_class_id is only reset by __init__ and clear() (lines 18, 26), not when segments are deleted. So X can re-activate a class that no longer has any segments. - Every file loader calls add_segment (file_manager.py:259, 273, 324, 403, 590, 672, 701). After loading annotations, the last loaded segment's class counts as the recent class. The spec's starting state (classes exist, none used) is therefore rarely reachable in single view. - The X handler only uses self.segment_manager. The multi-view managers set the active class directly (main_window.py:6446, 6456, 7211). I did not check how X behaves in multi-view. I found no instruction-like text and no credentials in the cited regions.) — confirm legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2712-2738 (live X handler; bound at main_window.py:1011 and legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/hotkeys.py:104-106); legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/core/segment_manager.py:41-42 (adding a segment records its class as most recently used), :87-95 (unique class IDs sorted ascending), :409-423 (toggle records and returns last toggled class). Remove segment_manager.py:425-440: it is unused and uses the opposite (highest-ID) fallback. is the authoritative implementation. Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-087: Default model download integrity check
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam_model.py:20-65; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/models/sam_model.py:94-138; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:39-41`  
**Plain English:** Without a custom model, a missing sam_vit_h_4b8939.pth is moved from ~/.cache/lazylabel or downloaded from dl.fbaipublicfiles.com; the only integrity check is bytes received equal Content-Length.  
**Specification:**  
  Given No local checkpoint; network drops after 1.2 GB  
  When  The default model loads  
  Then  'Network error during download' is raised; the partial file is not deleted (only the generic error branch deletes it), so the next start skips download and fails loading the truncated checkpoint  
**Parameters:** timeout 30 s; chunk 1 KiB; default model vit_h / sam_vit_h_4b8939.pth  
**Edge cases handled:** None recorded  
**Suspected defect:** No checksum; partial downloads persist after network errors.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** YES TO BOTH, AND THE RUNTIME DOWNLOAD GOES AWAY ENTIRELY. The question was whether checkpoints should be checksum-verified and partial downloads discarded. WHAT LEGACY DOES. Without a custom model it looks for sam_vit_h_4b8939.pth, moves it from ~/.cache/lazylabel if it is there, and otherwise fetches it from dl.fbaipublicfiles.com in 1 KiB chunks with a 30 second timeout. The only integrity check is that the number of bytes received equals Content-Length. That check sees exactly one failure - a download cut short - and is blind to every other: corruption on disk, a half-written file from a previous run, a mirror serving something else, a file edited after it arrived. And it does not even catch the one thing it is for. The card records that a network error leaves the partial file in place, because only the generic error branch deletes it. So the next start finds a file of the right name, skips the download, and fails to load a truncated checkpoint. The user sees a model-loading error with no indication that the cause is a 1.2 GB fragment sitting on their disk, and retrying does not help, because the retry is what is being skipped. A caching scheme whose failure mode is a permanent one is worse than no caching. ANSWER, and it is stronger than the question asked for. Checksum-verified: every checkpoint is pinned by SHA-256 in a manifest, and one that does not match is refused with the expected and actual digests named. Partial downloads discarded: there are no downloads. Nothing is fetched at runtime at all (SEC-03, SEC-05, SEC-17) - the build pipeline mirrors checkpoints to disk and the manifest describes what should be there. A service that never downloads cannot leave a fragment behind, which is a better answer than remembering to delete one. BUILT IN PHASE 2, at `inference/src/lazylabel_inference/manifest.py`. `check_checkpoint` compares the byte count first because it is free and catches precisely the truncated-file case with a message that names it - 'a partial download left in place is the usual cause' - and then hashes in 1 MiB chunks, because a SAM checkpoint is gigabytes and reading one whole to hash it would cost as much memory as the model. The result is a status, not an exception: present, verified, and a detail string, so `/health` and `/models` can report a broken checkpoint as a configuration fault rather than a crash. Tested at `tests/test_manifest.py`, including the right-size-wrong-bytes case that Content-Length cannot see at all. FOR PHASE 3, one thing this does not yet cover: the load itself must use weights-only loading, so that a checkpoint which passes its hash is still not an arbitrary-code-execution path if the manifest is ever wrong. Hash verification and weights-only loading are different defences against different mistakes, and Phase 3's exit criterion 3 requires both.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should checkpoints be checksum-verified and partial downloads discarded? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-088: One unknown key in settings.json resets every preference; old save flags migrate to export formats
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:2096-2107; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/export_format_widget.py:94-106; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/config/settings.py:12-118; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/widgets/settings_widget.py:148-183`  
**Plain English:** Settings load from JSON into a fixed set of fields, and the old save_npz/save_txt flags are converted to the export format list. Any unknown key or invalid JSON silently falls back to all defaults, which are written back on exit. The export format list can never be empty.  
**Specification:**  
  Given settings.json contains save_npz=false, save_txt=true and dark_mode=false  
  When  The app starts  
  Then  export_formats becomes ['YOLO_DETECTION'] and dark mode is off. If the file also contained yolo_use_alias=true, every field would reset to defaults (Auto-Save on, formats NPZ + YOLO Detection, dark mode on), and the defaults would be saved on close.  
**Parameters:** Legacy keys: save_npz, save_txt, bb_use_alias, save_class_aliases. Both flags false gives ['NPZ']. Default formats NPZ + YOLO_DETECTION.; Default export formats NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION; unchecking the last selected format is reverted; empty or invalid saved lists fall back to defaults; Defaults: auto_save True; export_formats NPZ, YOLO_DETECTION; fragment_threshold 0; polygon_join_threshold 2; polygon_resolution 80; pixel priority off/ascending; operate_on_view False; default model vit_h; stream_window_size 250; multi_view_grid_mode 2_view  
**Edge cases handled:** If only bb_use_alias is present, the missing save_npz and save_txt default to true and overwrite any existing export_formats with NPZ + YOLO_DETECTION.; Values of the wrong type are accepted unchecked.; Unchecking the last selected format re-checks it.; Unknown format names are dropped; if none remain, the defaults are used.; Downgrading after a newer version added a setting loses all user settings  
**Suspected defect:** An unknown key resets everything, and a partial legacy file overwrites export_formats. | A single unknown key silently discards all user settings, changing which sidecar files are written and deleted. | A settings file from a newer version silently resets every preference, including Auto-Save.  
**Confidence:** High — citation confirmed by an independent referee; 2 near-duplicate card(s) from other lenses were folded in

### RULE-091: Up to 10 image embeddings are cached by file path and computed ahead for nearby images
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:223; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/embedding_cache_manager.py:18-72; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:761-766; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/sam_preload_scheduler.py:24-133; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:1604-1636; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/sam_single_view_manager.py:244-267; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/sam_single_view_manager.py:316-366; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:5109-5117; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:7361-7442; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/utils/fast_file_manager.py:1408-1463`  
**Plain English:** Embeddings are kept in a least-recently-used cache of 10 entries keyed by a hash of the image path. After each embed, the app waits 200 ms and then pre-computes the first uncached image among archetype frames, then the next, next-but-one and previous images, retrying every 500 ms while the current image is still embedding.  
**Specification:**  
  Given The cache holds 10 images; the user is on img_050 of 100  
  When  img_050 finishes embedding  
  Then  img_051 is pre-computed and the oldest entry is evicted, followed by img_052 and img_049 if uncached; the model is then pointed back at img_050  
**Parameters:** max_size 10; preload delay 200 ms; retry 500 ms; order: archetype priority queue, N+1, N+2, N-1  
**Edge cases handled:** The cache is never cleared: not on model switch, not on unload, not when a file changes on disk.; Keys are path-only, so embeddings from the previous model can be restored into a newly loaded model with compatible tensor shapes.  
**Suspected defect:** Stale embeddings from another model, or from an older version of the file, may be reused after switching models.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** BOTH QUESTIONS ANSWERED, AND THE SECOND ONE IS WORSE THAN THE CARD SUGGESTS. Q2 first, because it decides Q1: do SAM variants with the same embedding shape accept each other's cached features? YES, silently. `segment_anything.modeling.ImageEncoderViT` takes `img_size=1024` and `out_chans=256` for every variant; vit_b, vit_l and vit_h differ only in `encoder_embed_dim`, `encoder_depth`, `encoder_num_heads` and the global-attention indices, all of which are INTERNAL to the encoder. The tensor that leaves it is 256 x 64 x 64 for all three. So a cached embedding computed by vit_b, handed to a vit_h decoder, is shape-compatible: no exception, no warning, just a mask derived from features the decoder was never trained against. The user gets a plausible-looking wrong mask and no reason to doubt it. The same holds in reverse, and between any two SAM 1 variants. That is the strongest possible argument for Q1. YES, cached embeddings must be invalidated on model change, and on file modification, and the cache key is what enforces it rather than a separate invalidation step that can be forgotten. The legacy key is a hash of the image PATH alone, which is wrong on three axes at once: switch models and the stale features are reused; edit or replace the image and the old features are reused; and with Operate On View on (RULE-089) the pixels SAM encodes are the ADJUSTED ones, so changing brightness changes what should be encoded while the path does not move. THE KEY, for Phase 3: (image identity, model identity, display-adjustment identity). - image identity: size and mtime of the source file, with a content hash when the pipeline has already read the bytes. Path alone is not identity; it is a name for wherever the bytes currently are. - model identity: the manifest entry's SHA-256, not the model's file name or its declared variant. The manifest already pins it (RULE-085, RULE-087), so this is free, and it means two checkpoints of the same variant - a fine-tune and its base - cannot share a cache entry either. - display-adjustment identity: a hash of the adjustment parameters when Operate On View is on, and a constant when it is off. A key that cannot be computed is a cache miss, never a guess. The rest of the card stands and should be preserved: an LRU of 10 entries, and the prefetch order - the first uncached archetype frame, then next, next-but-one and previous - which is what makes the first click after navigating feel immediate (RULE-074's latency budget depends on it). The 200 ms settle and 500 ms retry are legacy timings worth keeping as defaults and worth making configurable rather than constant, since they were tuned against a desktop GPU and a hosted deployment may not behave the same. One addition the legacy design has no place for: the cache now lives in a SERVICE, not a process the user owns, so entries must expire on idle as well as on eviction. `AI_NATIVE_SPEC.md` section 3 already sets that at 30 minutes and caps retention at 10, which matches this card; the two should stay in step. **Still open for the owner:** Whether the 200 ms settle and 500 ms retry stay at legacy's values once the encode runs on a shared server rather than the user's own GPU. They are defaults to measure in Phase 3 against the 150 ms p95 budget, not constants to port unexamined.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: Should cached embeddings be invalidated on model change or file modification? In practice, do SAM variants with the same embedding shape (e.g. vit_b and vit_h) accept each other's cached features? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

### RULE-092: Linked multi-view shape mirroring and class ids
**Category:** Policy  
**Priority:** P1  
**Source:** `legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/multi_view_coordinator.py:42-47; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:5778-5815; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/keyboard_event_manager.py:99-123; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:6392-6456; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/main_window.py:6938-7006; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/multi_view_coordinator.py:197-214; legacy/lazylabel/src/lazylabel/ui/managers/ai_segment_manager.py:301-403`  
**Plain English:** Viewers start linked, and linked operations apply to both viewers at the same coordinates; polygons share one class id, but boxes, circles and AI masks take each viewer's own active or next class id.  
**Specification:**  
  Given Linked viewers, viewer 1 classes {0,1}, viewer 2 classes {0}, no active class  
  When  The user draws a box  
  Then  Viewer 1's box gets class 2 and viewer 2's box gets class 1  
**Parameters:** Link default on; Link state default True  
**Edge cases handled:** Unlinked operations affect only the active viewer; Only viewers 0 and 1 exist despite a 4-view setting; Multi-view polygon add, erase, merge and delete are not undoable; When unlinked, only the active viewer is targeted.; Viewers with fewer than 3 points are skipped.; Each viewer's accepted AI mask takes that viewer's active class, else its next free id.; Multi-view polygon erases are not recorded for undo.  
**Suspected defect:** The same mirrored object is saved with different class ids in the two images, while a mirrored polygon would share one id. | Linked AI accept can give the same object different class IDs in the two images.  
**Answer (2026-09-19):** YES - in linked mode one linked operation makes ONE object, so it must carry the same class in both images. Legacy's split behaviour - polygons share a class id while boxes, circles and accepted AI masks each take their own viewer's active or next id - is an inconsistency with no rationale in the code, and decision 8 already settles how to treat it: multi-view is rebuilt as a synchronized split view from the linked-operation rules, and the half-migrated legacy path is deleted rather than ported. A rule that behaves three different ways depending on which tool drew the shape is exactly what 'rebuild from the rules' is for. SAYING 'THE SAME CLASS ID' IS NOT QUITE ENOUGH, AND THE GAP MATTERS. Decision 6 keeps class ids PER IMAGE, so the number 3 in one image's annotation file and the number 3 in another's are not the same class - each file carries its own alias table, and 'car' can be id 1 in one and id 2 in the next. Copying the numeric id across a linked pair therefore guarantees nothing on its own: it produces matching numbers that can mean different things, which is worse than a visible mismatch because every export looks consistent. THE INVARIANT IS THE NAME, AND THE ID FOLLOWS FROM IT. A linked operation resolves the active class to its ALIAS, then, in each image independently, uses the id that alias already has there, or allocates that image's next free id and records the alias against it. Both images then name the object the same thing, which is what a user means by 'the same class', and each file stays internally consistent with decision 6 and byte-identical to what legacy would have written for that image alone. Where the active class has no alias, the id is the name - legacy writes str(class_id) - so the two images must share the numeric id in that case, and allocating a different one would be the same bug in a different place. AN UNRESOLVED PART, DELIBERATELY LEFT TO PHASE 6 ENTRY. If the two images already disagree - 'car' is id 1 in the left and id 4 in the right - the rule above is still well defined and produces the right names. What it cannot do is keep ids equal as well, and nothing here should pretend otherwise. Decision 8 explicitly schedules the per-viewer class-id space as a Phase 6 entry decision, so the choice between 'names always agree, ids may differ' (what this answer specifies) and a stricter 'reconcile the id spaces on link' belongs there, with the split view in front of the approver. PRIORITY: not P0. The card's suspected defect is real, but it mislabels rather than destroys, and it is recoverable by reassignment. What Phase 5 owes this rule is that the linked-operation path resolves classes by alias rather than by id, so Phase 6 has one behaviour to extend instead of three to reconcile. **Still open for the owner:** Whether a linked pair whose images already assign different ids to the same alias should also have its ids reconciled, or only its names. Decision 8 schedules the per-viewer class-id space as a Phase 6 entry decision; this answer specifies name agreement, which is well defined either way.  
**Confidence:** Medium — SME question: In linked mode must the same object always receive the same class ID in both images? Answered on 2026-09-19, above.

## Questions answered since extraction

Each rule below had its specification re-derived from the cited legacy source, and its question answered from that code or from an approved decision in `MODERNIZATION_BRIEF.md` §7. The full answer, and the corrections made to the card, are on the card itself.

- **[RULE-001](#rule-001-coco-json-export-structure-and-area)** (P0, Medium) COCO JSON export structure and area: The open question - should COCO 'area' be the mask pixel count instead of the contour polygon area - is answered NO by the approved decision that exports stay byte-identical to legacy output: the Node/TypeScript port must keep area = trunc(contourArea(contour)) for contours of 3+ points and w*h otherwise (coco.py:73-77), which yields 81 for the 100-pixel 10x10 block.
  - Given: disambiguated to explicit pixel extents (x 100-109, y 50-59) and added the 11x11 counter-example for polygon-corner input; added image size, class_order = [2] and the fact that COCO selection is what populates per-object contours
  - Then: replaced the two fragments with the complete file body in written key order (images/annotations/categories), including file_name basename and full-image width/height, and recorded that the numbers were re-verified against OpenCV 4.12.0
  - And: was null; now carries the serialization and file-lifecycle contract (indent=2, key/field order, ensure_ascii \\uXXXX escaping, CRLF on Windows vs LF elsewhere, no trailing newline, directory creation, write-nothing-delete-nothing on zero annotations)
  - Parameters: area restated as TRUNCATE toward zero (Math.trunc/Math.floor), not 'int()', with the 24.5 -\> 24 and 0.5 -\> 0 evidence and an explicit warning against Math.round
  - Parameters: clarified that the \>= 3 threshold counts contour POINTS and that a 3+-point contour uses contour area even when it is 0
  - Parameters: added the full categories rule - ascending class_order ids, raw class id with no +1 shift (class 0 -\> category 0), emitted even with zero annotations, str(class_id) for both fields when no alias, supercategory = name when the alias has no dot
  - Parameters: added that bbox is edge-inclusive (w = maxX - minX + 1) and that segmentation is a list containing exactly one flat array
  - Parameters: added the contour source (RETR_EXTERNAL, CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE) and the explicit contrast with YOLO Segmentation's approxPolyDP epsilon = 0.001 * arcLength
  - Parameters: removed the duplicated 'annotation ids restart at 1 per file' / 'annotation ids start at 1 per file' clause
  - Edge cases: added area truncation, the sub-3-point fallback numbers (1 / 10 / 100 for pixel, line, diagonal), and the 1-/2-point padding to a 4-point ring
  - Edge cases: added holes-are-filled (ring: 300 pixels, area 361) and one-segment-many-annotations
  - Edge cases: added annotation ORDER - segment order then OpenCV contour order, with the verified (30,30)-before-(5,5) example
  - Edge cases: added the no-alias case (str(class_id) in both fields) and extended the alias-split cases with 'a.b.c', 'dog.' and '.dog'
  - Edge cases: added the lossy alias round-trip ('x.x' reloads as 'x', file_manager.py:635-637)
  - Edge cases: added category-without-annotation when crop or pixel priority removes every pixel of a class
  - Edge cases: added full-image coordinate space under crop, and that the multi-view save path applies no crop at all
  - Edge cases: rewrote 'No file when there are no objects' as the stale-file hazard - writes nothing, deletes nothing, old file stays loadable at LOAD_PRIORITY rank 3
  - Edge cases: expanded the id-collision note to record that the loader ignores id, image_id, area, iscrowd and the images block entirely
  - Edge cases: added the empty-instances fallback branch, which contours merged per-class channels and fuses same-class objects
- **[RULE-003](#rule-003-npz-class-map-export-resolves-overlaps-to-lowest-class-and-stores-foreground)** (P0, Medium) NPZ Class Map export resolves overlaps to lowest class and stores foreground: Open question, answered: the card's export Given/When/Then is in fact faithful — I re-ran npz_class_map.py:66-73 verbatim under NumPy 2.2.6 with class_order [2, 7] and got class_map [7, 2, 0] and foreground [true, true, false] exactly as written; the fidelity judge's 'faithful no' verdict applies to a different folded sub-card (the import/size-mismatch scenario, P0_PANEL.md 'NPZ Class Map import requires matching size'), whose Given described a foreground key covering only some 0-valued pixels, which would make np.unique(class_map[foreground]) return [0] and produce no class-4 segment at all (file_manager.py:320) — that scenario is replaced here by the concrete 2x3 round trip in 'and'.
  - Recorded that the export Given/When/Then is faithful as written (re-verified against npz_class_map.py:66-73 under NumPy 2.2.6); the 'faithful no' verdict belongs to the folded import sub-card, not to this scenario
  - Made Given concrete and complete: 2x3 image, explicit per-channel 0/1 uint8 tensor, and the precondition that class_order is get_unique_class_ids() sorted ascending (segment_manager.py:87-95)
  - Made When name the two early-return guards (mask_tensor.size == 0, no element \> 0) at npz_class_map.py:21-26
  - Kept Then as a worked example but added the two keys the card omitted — class_order (int64) and class_aliases — plus explicit dtypes for all four arrays (npz_class_map.py:37-43)
  - Filled the previously null 'and' with the reload half: load order gate, alias restore before the shape gate, ascending np.unique segment emission, and the lossy overlap round trip (file_manager.py:128-136, 282-333)
  - Replaced 'dtype uint16 (class IDs up to 65535)' with the exact range 0..65535 inclusive and the hard OverflowError failure mode, including that export_all does not catch it
  - Restated the tie-break as 'first channel via argmax', valid as 'lowest class id' only under the ascending sort; added the unsorted-order counterexample (order [7,2] gives B = 7)
  - Added Pixel Priority as a parameter: applied to the tensor before export, ascending keeps 2, descending gives 7 (segment_manager.py:247-250, 356-361)
  - Deleted the false edge case 'Class IDs above 65535 overflow' and merged it with the correct one; added that negative ids fail identically
  - Qualified 'No file when no pixel is labelled' as 'no new file written, stale file retained', and added the crop path that reaches it
  - Added the rejection side effect: class_aliases is replaced at file_manager.py:308 before the shape check at :313
  - Added the chain-stop defect (file_manager.py:205 vs its docstring at :161-163 and CHANGELOG.md:24) and its data-loss consequence via auto-save + delete_all_outputs, flagged as not-to-be-ported
  - Added the asymmetric IndexError path for a mis-shaped foreground array (file_manager.py:320, outside the try), which does continue the chain
  - Added the precondition that _CM.npz is only read when no .npz/_seg.txt/_coco.json exists (file_manager.py:128-136)
  - Added reload ordering, int() coercion, type 'Loaded'/vertices null, and the unreachable empty-mask guard at file_manager.py:322-323
  - Added that class_aliases is the whole session dict (possibly empty) and that restoring replaces rather than merges
